Some of these images were later overwritten in the study folder, so this is the only place they survive. The raw record is the transcript.

The brief

You are a student learning to paint one thing, in the painterly pixel-art language of Mark Ferrari: the shallow channels of a braided river (ribbons of water winding between gravel bars, reflecting the sky, with riffles over stones and wet edges).

How you learn: build up, one form at a time, in stages. Don't move to the next stage until the current one is truly convincing to your own eye. Look at every image you make with the Read tool.

  1. A still strip of water, in greyscale, seen at a low angle (about 5–10° down): it mirrors the sky and whatever stands beside it. Work out the value of the reflection against the sky, the sheen band, and the darker shallows where you see the bottom.
  2. The edge: where water meets a gravel bank. The lip, the wet dark band, the reflected bank, the lapping light line. Then the same edge where it meets grass.
  3. Moving water: shallow riffles over stones, marks that say flow, and where the mirror breaks. Say flow with as few deliberate marks as possible.
  4. Colour: the water reusing the sky's own ramp, plus a few water-only tints; dusk, midday and overcast versions.
  5. A channel as one form: a ribbon that bends, narrows, splits and rejoins, seen at low angle (where it becomes bright ribbons of reflected sky) and at 30° oblique. How its width and marks change with distance, down to a few-pixel thread.
  6. Sheets: your technique as code with parameters (width, depth, flow, sky and hour, surrounding bank, camera angle, distance, seed), swept on labelled sheets. Every cell should look painted.

At every stage, make side-by-sides: a photo crop | your pixels | the nearest thing in Ferrari's work, at 8×. For Ferrari:

  • his water's edge in ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/out/cmp_c4.png, with the rules in master-copy/src/c4_water.py;
  • his Mountain Stream and Mirror Pond scenes in pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw/ and shared/refs.

The earlier water study found that he colour-cycles only reflection edges, faster for faster flow (~/work/pixelart-studies/water/POST.md). Treat it as background, not a template.

Photos:

  • ~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_*;
  • more via python3 ~/work/pixelart-studies/tools/imgfetch.py "braided river channel reflection" -n 12 -o refs --sheet.

Background reading: ~/work/pixelart-studies/SYNTHESIS.md and pedagogy/*/LESSONS.md.

Deliverables in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/:

  • LESSONS.md: each stage with its images, what you learned, and an honest comparison;
  • the technique code;
  • the stage images;
  • the sheets. Read anywhere under ~/work/pixelart-studies/, write only in your folder. Resources: nice -n 10, at most 4 threads. Take the time mastery takes.

# Painter core: one method (draft 1)

Synthesized by the integrator from the four pedagogy arms (`pedagogy/{atelier,master-copy,analysis,observation}`) and, secondarily, the seven topic studies.

Each rule is tagged:

- **[shown]**: a student implemented it and it visibly works in their sheets.
- **[measured]**: a student measured it in Ferrari's pixels or in photos.
- **[mine]**: my inference, not yet built by anyone.

This isn't a committee of special strengths. The arms converged on one machine independently, and the draft describes that machine.

## The convergence

Working alone, all four arms landed on the same architecture:

- **Paint indices, not colours.**
  - Master copy found that Ferrari's index map is literally a form map: indices are bins of surface direction, and "the palette is the light". The same index image serves morning and afternoon. [measured]
  - Atelier paints in "step space" along one material ramp. [shown]
  - Sky found that two of Ferrari's hour pairs are identical pixels. [measured]
  - Analysis found that value is the structure and colour is temperature and distance. [measured]
- **Two light families that never touch.** Atelier's terminator rule [shown], analysis's three-value picture plan with every mass kept inside its group [measured], and rock's lit and shadow groups [shown] are the same idea at two scales.
- **Dither mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp.** All four arms use it. Ordered 2×2 dither suits hard or precise things (rock, sky seams, water rows); random or diffusion dither suits organic texture (lawns, foam). Master copy measured this [measured]; analysis cites Ferrari's GDC talk for the same split.
- **The medium's own geometry.** Contours are clean runs (1:1, 2:1, 4-4-4 flats), measured on Ferrari's crests by analysis and atelier [measured]. Atelier redraws every major contour by snapping edges to clean ratios [shown]. Masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat run with a 1-px darker contact line [measured: analysis, observation].
- **Clean runs are for narrow forms; mass boundaries are where texture ends.** David's correction (2026-09-25): the clean-run rule came from the *grass strokes*. It applies to anything narrow, drawn as a line: blades, cracks, joints, twigs, conifer spires, stems, a snowline drawn as a stroke. A 1–2 px form must be a clean run of 1:1, 2:1 or 4-4-4 steps, or it reads as noise [measured: analysis, atelier; the runs they found on crests are this rule too, since a crest line against the sky is drawn as a stroke]. A *mass outline* isn't drawn as a line. A textured mass (grass mound, foliage, scrub, scree) ends in a fringe: blade tips break its top, dark holes open along the crest with lit peaks just behind, and the form turns through mark density [measured: master cop

## 0. What the reference turned out to be, and the finding that set up the rest

The reference file is 1268×938. Run-length analysis shows it is an exact 2× upscale of a **634×469 native image with 172 colours**. It is Ferrari's **Mirror Pond** from *Living Worlds*, shown in a dusk palette.

The original indexed data of the colour-cycling scenes is public. I fetched it from the Canvas Cycle demo (`effectgames.com/demos/canvascycle/images/*.LBM.json`; `src/lw2png.py`) for these scenes: Mirror Pond (Morning V16, Afternoon V16PM), Mountain Stream (V19, V19PM), Mountain Fortress Dusk, Seascape Sunset, Pond Ripples, Forest Edge, Mountain Storm, Island Fires, Approaching Storm and Deep Forest. The reference aligns with V16PM at offset (3,4). 86% of its pixels map one-to-one onto V16PM indices; the rest are colour-cycled water.

# output:
| **F4 wake** | V lines from the rock flanks, half-angle 38° − 9U (min 12°) | 3–5 R long, fading | light, dashed |
| **F5 eddy** | 0.6–3.2 R behind emergent rocks | pocket | returns to still mirror (calm) |
| **F6 foam cluster** | aeration T (holes, steps, pillows, U > 2 m/s), seeded **on wave crests** | r ≈ 0.075 m × px per m, ellipse 1.9:0.6 | 4-step foam ramp lit top and sun side, plus a 2-px lace fringe |
| **L1 lip / tongue / bend** | top of a fall | lip 1 px; tongue 8–22% of the drop (grows with volume); bend band ⅓ down the tongue | lip brightest; tongue dark with streaks |
| **L2 strands** | across the curtain, width 1–3 px (warped) | bimodal values: white rope / grey body / dark gap | glass edges brightest on the sun side for the top 45% |
| **L3 fray** | each strand ends at its own height, 55–95% down | then 25% drops | |
| **L4 plunge / mist** | under the wet ribbons only | band ∝ volume; mist height (0.15 + 0.7 v^1.5) × drop | foam clusters; mist is 25–50% pale dither |
| **S1 swell crest** | level set of phase φ = d̃/λ + t | 1 px, broken by noise | sky-light |
| **S2 face / lip shadow** | frac(φ) ∈ [0.8, 0.96] / [0.95, 1) in the break and bore zones | 2–3 px / 1 px | lightest body (backlit) / darkest |
| **S3 bore front + lace** | frac(φ) < 0.07 + 0.15E; lace is thresholded fine noise fading seaward | | foam clusters; lace is foam with a checker seam only at its threshold |
| **S4 swash, lace edge, wet sand, wrack** | −r < d < 0 on sand; edge at d = −r; wet to 1.25 R_max; wrack at 1.45 R_max | | 50% water over sand; white; sand one step down with 20% sky; kelp clumps |

The vocabulary at 5× (`plates/vocabulary_card.png`):

![vocabulary](plates/vocabulary_card.png)

### Step 5: cut the shape

Water is a *hole* in the picture, and the banks do half its drawing:

- **Near banks overlap the water** with serrated grass blades or fern fronds. The overhang says "edge" better than any outline can.
- **The far shore is a thin dark strip** (sedge, rocks), then the M1 glint just under it.
- **Stream banks are cut into the ground.** A 1–2 px earth lip runs along the near edge, and a crease on the far edge.
- **Rocks standing in water** get the crease or glint, their own reflection, and, in moving water, a pillow and a wake.
- **Nothing on the bank may sit on top of the water's shape.** Bank rocks whose footprint would overlap the channel are dropped. A guard rule, but a necessary one: the silhouette of the water is the most important shape in the picture.

### Step 6: camera and distance

The same spec paints under three cameras. The differences are all *derived*, not hand-set.

**Landscape (8–15° down, perspective).** The grazing angle goes from about 0° at the horizon to about 20° at the frame's bottom edge, so Fresnel runs from about 0.9 to 0.15. The far water is almost pure mirror, plus glare wherever it's rough. The near water is where you see stones. Marks scale with px per m (`f/z`), so the far third of a lake shows only M1 glints and a s

# output:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
            if e.code == 429:
                with _glock:
                    SLOW_HOSTS[host] = min(8.0, SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, 0.34) * 2)  # adapt: this host wants us slower
            if e.code in (429, 500, 502, 503, 504) and i < tries - 1:
                wait = min(15, int(e.headers.get("Retry-After") or 0) or 2 * 2 ** i)
                time.sleep(wait); continue
            raise
        except (urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError):
            if i < tries - 1:
                time.sleep(2 * (i + 1)); continue
            raise

def commons(query, n, width):
    q = urllib.parse.urlencode({"action": "query", "generator": "search", "gsrnamespace": 6, "gsrlimit": min(50, n * 3),
                                "gsrsearch": query + " filetype:bitmap", "prop": "imageinfo",
                                "iiprop": "url|size|extmetadata", "iiurlwidth": min((b for b in (330, 500, 960, 1280) if b >= width), default=1280), "format": "json"})
    d = json.loads(get("https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php?" + q))
    out = []
    for p in sorted(d.get("query", {}).get("pages", {}).values(), key=lambda

Bash: Zoom crops of Ferrari river passages
mkdir -p ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/{src,out,study}; cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; cat > src/zoomcrop.py <<'EOF'
"""zoomcrop.py <img> x y w h scale out  -- nearest-neighbour crop zoom (native pixels)."""
import sys
from PIL import Image
f,x,y,w,h,s,o=sys.argv[1:8]; x,y,w,h,s=map(int,(x,y,w,h,s))
im=Image.open(f).convert('RGB').crop((x,y,x+w,y+h))
im.resize((w*s,h*s),Image.NEAREST).save(o)
EOF

"""Copy 4: water's edge -- mirrored reflection, sheen, shallow shelf with stones, and the
shadowed grass bank whose blades break the skyline of the water.
Ref crop native (250,330,200,139).

Discovered procedure:

  * The water re-uses the SKY'S OWN ramp (index-identical in the Living Worlds file) for
    the mirrored sky, then adds a few water-only tints: a lighter, pinker SHEEN band just
    below the reflected objects (grazing-angle reflection is brightest), sinking to a
    duller lilac, then a dark violet-brown SHALLOW shelf near the shore where you see
    bottom instead of sky.  Value falls toward the viewer.
  * Mirrored objects keep their colours and even their lit rims (now on the lower edge),
    but a reflected pine trails a soft dithered streak below its tip: the only 'blur'.
  * Transitions in water: checkerboard between near-identical tints + sparse horizontal
    DASH lines (every other pixel of a row in the next-lighter tint) -- the surface.
  * Shallow shelf edges are flat horizontal lines; stones are flat lozenges: dark body,
    darker underside line, a 1px light water-line lapping round them.
  * Shadow bank: 4 classes (black, black-olive, olive, grey-olive), strands shorter and
    busier than the lit bank (vertical coherence 0.53 vs 0.78) and leaning '\\' (tips to
    the upper-left, down-slope).  The silhouette against the water is not an edge but a
    fringe of separate 1px blades, 4-14 px, curved, often in V / Y pairs.
"""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import fl, compare

W, H = 200, 139
WATER = [(64, 48, 55), (103, 80, 87), (131, 101, 112), (154, 119, 136), (173, 141, 165),

Bash: Measure sky vs water luminance in photos
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; cat > src/sample.py <<'EOF'
"""Sample mean L* in named boxes of a photo; write an annotated copy so the boxes can be checked by eye."""
import sys, json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from skimage.color import rgb2lab

def run(path, boxes, out):
    im = Image.open(path).convert('RGB'); a = np.asarray(im) / 255.0
    L = rgb2lab(a)[..., 0]
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    res = {}
    for name, (x, y, w, h) in boxes.items():
        res[name] = round(float(L[y:y+h, x:x+w].mean()), 1)
        d.rectangle((x, y, x+w, y+h), outline=(255, 0, 0), width=2); d.text((x+2, y+2), name, fill=(255, 0, 0))
    im.save(out); return res

if __name__ == '__main__':
    spec = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
    for k, v in spec.items():
        print(k, run(v['path'], v['boxes'], 'study/samp_' + k + '.jpg'))
EOF

Bash: Percentile-based water vs gravel luminance
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; python3 - <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from skimage.color import rgb2lab
R='~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/'
def L(p): return rgb2lab(np.asarray(Image.open(R+p).convert('RGB'))/255.)[...,0]
a=L('braided_lowangle_overcast_nz.jpg')
# water band region rows 250-470; water = top 10% brightest in each row band
for y0 in (240,280,320,360,400,440):
    b=a[y0:y0+40,200:900].ravel(); print('nz rows',y0, 'water~p97',round(np.percentile(b,97),1),'gravel~p30',round(np.percentile(b,30),1))
print('nz sky hz',round(a[150:200,0:300].mean(),1))
a=L('braided_bars_denali.jpg')
for y0 in (380,420,480,540,600):
    b=a[y0:y0+30,0:960].ravel(); print('denali',y0,round(np.percentile(b,95),1),round(np.percentile(b,30),1))
print('denali sky', round(a[100:180,300:700].mean(),1))
EOF

def load(name):
    t=open(f'ref/lw/{name}.json').read()

# output:
"""Compare a copy against its reference crop.
Outputs side-by-side 3x (whole crop) and 8x (a detail window), and prints metrics:
  blurLab : mean deltaE of Gaussian-blurred (sigma 3px) images  -> masses / value structure "at a squint"
  hist    : L1 distance between colour-usage histograms, after snapping both to the ref palette (0..2)
  runs    : mean vertical/horizontal same-colour run lengths (texture grain / stroke direction)
  edges   : density of colour changes per pixel (busyness)
"""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter

def srgb2lab(a):
    a=a/255.0
    a=np.where(a>0.04045,((a+0.055)/1.055)**2.4,a/12.92)
    M=np.array([[0.4124,0.3576,0.1805],[0.2126,0.7152,0.0722],[0.0193,0.1192,0.9505]])
    xyz=a@M.T/np.array([0.9505,1.0,1.089])
    f=np.where(xyz>0.008856,np.cbrt(xyz),7.787*xyz+16/116)
    L=116*f[...,1]-16; A=500*(f[...,0]-f[...,1]); B=200*(f[...,1]-f[...,2])
    return np.stack([L,A,B],-1)

def runs(img):
    k=img[...,0].astype(np.int64)*65536+img[...,1]*256+img[...,2]
    v=(np.diff(k,axis=0)!=0).mean(); h=(np.diff(k,axis=1)!=0).mean()
    return 1/max(v,1e-6),1/max(h,1e-6)

def metrics(copy,ref):
    c=copy.astype(float); r=ref.astype(float)
    bl=np.linalg.norm(gaussian_filter(srgb2lab(c),(3,3,0))-gaussian_filter(srgb2lab(r),(3,3,0)),axis=-1).mean()
    pal=np.unique(ref.reshape(-1,3),axis=0).astype(float)
    def snap(x):
        d=((x.reshape(-1,1,3)-pal[None])**2).sum(-1); return d.argmin(1)
    hc=np.bincount(snap(c),minlength=len(pal))/c[...,0].size
    hr=np.bincount(snap(r),minlength=len(pal))/r[...,0].size
    return dict(blurLab=round(bl,2),hist=round(np.abs(hc-hr).sum(),3),
                runs_copy=tuple(round(x,2) for x in runs(copy)),runs_ref=tuple(round(x,2) for x in runs(ref)),
                ncol_copy=len(np.unique(copy.reshape(-1,3),axis=0)),ncol_ref=len(pal))

def sheet(copy,ref,out,detail=None,label=''):
    h,w=ref.shape[:2]
    a=Image.fromarray(ref.astype(np.uint8)).resize((w*3,h*3),Image.NEAREST)
    b=Image.fromarray(copy.astype(np.uint8)).resize((w*3,h*3),Image.NEAREST)
    if detail is None: detail=(w//2-20,h//2-15,40,30)
    x,y,dw,dh=detail
    a8=Image.fromarray(ref[y:y+dh,x:x+dw].astype(np.uint8)).resize((dw*8,dh*8),Image.NEAREST)
    b8=Image.fromarray(copy[y:y+dh,x:x+dw].astype(np.uint8)).resize((dw*8,dh*8),Image.NEAREST)
    W=max(w*6+12, dw*16+12); H=h*3+dh*8+40
    o=Image.new('RGB',(W,H),(250,250,250)); d=ImageDraw.Draw(o)
    o.paste(a,(0,16)); o.paste(b,(w*3+12,16))
    d.text((2,2),'FERRARI (ref) 3x',fill=(0,0,0)); d.text((w*3+14,2),'COPY 3x  '+label,fill=(0,0,0))
    y0=h*3+36
    o.paste(a8,(0,y0)); o.paste(b8,(dw*8+12,y0))
    d.text((2,y0-14),f'ref 8x detail @({x},{y})',fill=(0,0,0)); d.text((dw*8+14,y0-14),'copy 8x',fill=(0,0,0))
    for ox in (0,w*3+12):
        d.rectangle([ox+x*3,16+y*3,ox+(x+dw)*3,16+(y+dh)*3],outline=(255,0,0))
    o.save(out)
"""pair.py x y w h s out img1 img2 ... : side by side crops"""
import sys
fro

"""pixkit: small shared helpers for the channel study.

Index canvases (int arrays) + palettes (N x 3 uint8). Everything is nearest-neighbour.
"""
import re, json
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter

LW = '~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw/'
BAYER2 = np.array([[0, 2], [3, 1]]) / 4.0 + 0.125
BAYER4 = np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) / 16.0 + 1 / 32


def lw_load(name):
    t = open(LW + name + '.json').read()
    t = re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'", '"')
    d = json.loads(t)
    d['pal'] = np.array(d['colors'], dtype=np.uint8)
    d['idx'] = np.array(d['pixels'], dtype=np.int32).reshape(d['height'], d['width'])
    return d


def tile(m, h, w, ox=0, oy=0):
    my, mx = m.shape
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    return m[(yy + oy) % my, (xx + ox) % mx]


def rgb(idx, pal):
    return pal[np.clip(idx, 0, len(pal) - 1)]


def zoom(a, s):
    return np.repeat(np.repeat(a, s, 0), s, 1)


def save(a, path, s=1):
    Image.fromarray(zoom(a.astype(np.uint8), s)).save(path)


# ---------- dithering ----------------------------------------------------------------

def ordered(v, matrix=BAYER2, ox=0, oy=0):
    """continuous step coordinate v -> integer step, mixing only adjacent steps (ordered)."""
    h, w = v.shape
    t = tile(matrix, h, w, ox, oy)
    f = np.floor(v)
    return (f + ((v - f) > t)).astype(int)


def banded(v, width=0.5):
    """squash a continuous coordinate so it holds flat and crosses between integers over `width`."""
    f = np.floor(v); r = v - f
    lo = 0.5 - width / 2
    return f + np.clip((r - lo) / max(width, 1e-6), 0, 1)


def value_noise(h, w, scale, rng, octaves=1, aniso=(1.0, 1.0), persistence=0.5):
    out = np.zeros((h, w)); amp = 1.0; tot = 0
    for o in range(octaves):
        sy = max(1, int(h / (scale * aniso[0]) / 2 ** -o) + 2)
        sx = max(1, int(w / (scale * aniso[1]) / 2 ** -o) + 2)
        g = rng.random((sy + 1, sx + 1))
        yy = np.linspace(0, sy - 1, h); xx = np.linspace(0, sx - 1, w)
        y0 = np.floor(yy).astype(int); x0 = np.floor(xx).astype(int)
        fy = (yy - y0)[:, None]; fx = (xx - x0)[None, :]
        fy = fy * fy * (3 - 2 * fy); fx = fx * fx * (3 - 2 * fx)
        a = g[y0][:, x0]; b = g[y0][:, x0 + 1]; c = g[y0 + 1][:, x0]; d = g[y0 + 1][:, x0 + 1]
        out += amp * (a * (1 - fx) * (1 - fy) + b * fx * (1 - fy) + c * (1 - fx) * fy + d * fx * fy)
        tot += amp; amp *= persistence
    return out / tot


# ---------- geometry -----------------------------------------------------------------

def seg_dist(px, py, ax, ay, bx, by):
    dx, dy = bx - ax, by - ay
    L2 = dx * dx + dy * dy + 1e-9
    t = np.clip(((px - ax) * dx + (py - ay) * dy) / L2, 0, 1)
    return np.hypot(px - (ax + t * dx), py - (ay + t * dy)), t


def polyline_field(h, w, pts, widths, ky=1.0):
    """distance-to-polyline in a space where y is stretched by ky; returns (d - halfwidth) (<0 inside)."""
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w].astype(float)
    best = np.full((h, w), 1e9)
    for i in range(len(pts) - 1):
        (ax, ay), (bx, by) = pts[i], pts[i + 1]
        d, t = seg_dist(xx, yy * ky, ax, ay * ky, bx, by * ky)
        hw = widths[i] * (1 - t) + widths[i + 1] * t
        best = np.minimum(best, d - hw / 2)
    return best


# ---------- comparison ---------------------------------------------------------------

def srgb2lab(a):
    a = a / 255.0
    a = np.where(a > 0.04045, ((a + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4, a / 12.92)
    M = np.array([[0.4124, 0.3576, 0.1805], [0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722], [0.0193, 0.1192, 0.9505]])
    xyz = a @ M.T / np.array([0.9505, 1.0, 1.089])
    f = np.where(xyz > 0.008856, np.cbrt(xyz), 7.787 * xyz + 16 / 116)
    return np.stack([116 * f[..., 1] - 16, 500 * (f[..., 0] - f[..., 1]), 200 * (f[..., 1] - f[..., 2])], -1)


def runs(img):
    k = img[..., 0].astype(np.int64) * 65536 + img[..., 1] * 256 + img[..., 2]
    v = (np.diff(k, axis=0) != 0).mean(); hh = (np.diff(k, axis=1) != 0).mean()
    return round(1 / max(v, 1e-6), 2), round(1 / max(hh, 1e-6), 2)


def metrics(copy, ref):
    c = copy.astype(float); r = ref.astype(float)
    bl = np.linalg.norm(gaussian_filter(srgb2lab(c), (3, 3, 0)) - gaussian_filter(srgb2lab(r), (3, 3, 0)), axis=-1).mean()
    return dict(blurLab=round(float(bl), 2), runs_copy=runs(copy), runs_ref=runs(ref))


def panel(items, s, gap=8, label_h=14, bg=(250, 250, 250)):
    """items: list of (rgb array, label). Side by side at scale s."""
    ims = [Image.fromarray(zoom(a.astype(np.uint8), s)) for a, _ in items]
    W = sum(i.width for i in ims) + gap * (len(ims) - 1); H = max(i.height for i in ims) + label_h
    o = Image.new('RGB', (W, H), bg); d = ImageDraw.Draw(o); x = 0
    for im, (_, lab) in zip(ims, items):
        o.paste(im, (x, label_h)); d.text((x + 2, 1), lab, fill=(0, 0, 0)); x += im.width + gap
    return o


def stack(imgs, gap=8, bg=(250, 250, 250)):
    W = max(i.width for i in imgs); H = sum(i.height for i in imgs) + gap * (len(imgs) - 1)
    o = Image.new('RGB', (W, H), bg); y = 0
    for i in imgs:
        o.paste(i, (0, y)); y += i.height + gap
    return o


def compare_sheet(ref, copy, out, details, label='', s_whole=3, s_det=8):
    """ref|copy at s_whole, then each detail window (x,y,w,h) ref|copy at s_det."""
    rows = [panel([(ref, 'FERRARI %dx' % s_whole), (copy, 'COPY %dx %s' % (s_whole, label))], s_whole)]
    for (x, y, w, h) in details:
        rows.append(panel([(ref[y:y + h, x:x + w], 'ref 8x @(%d,%d)' % (x, y)),
                           (copy[y:y + h, x:x + w], 'copy 8x')], s_det))
    stack(rows).save(out)

Write: elements/channel/src/c0a_v02.py
"""Copy A: the winding low-angle ribbons of V02 (Living Worlds, 'Mountain Fortress Dusk'), native crop
(0,282, 92x82). Painted in Ferrari's own palette indices, by procedure, from a copyist's sketch
(ribbon centre-lines + widths, a few hand values). See LESSONS.md, stage 0.

Discovered procedure (read off his index map):
  WATER FILL  the water is the SKY RAMP (indices 1..15, horizon->zenith), and the index CLIMBS with
              nearness: far threads 2/3, mid 4/5, 6/7, near 7/8 -- nearer water mirrors higher sky.
              Steps hold flat, crossing as sustained 50% checkers (4545 over several rows).
              Hot glints: small horizontal clusters (1-4 px) of 42/43 (bright orange), sparse.
              Near ribbon: a checker of COLOUR-CYCLED peach (128..135) against static 7/8/3/40: glitter.
  SHAPE       at low angle the ribbon is horizontal runs; a far thread is ONE row, stepping a row at a
              time; turns are staircases; thickness in rows grows toward the viewer.
  EDGES       near side (land below / left of water on a down-going edge): LIGHT lip -- 73 runs directly
              under water, and on steep diagonals a 75/water checker, 73, then warm 51/52.
              far side (land above water): DARK -- runs of 80 (bank face + its dark reflection); on
              steep diagonals a water/dark checker fringe (dark pixels are cycled 233..239).
  MARSH       banded ordered ramp 73->75 far (flat rows + 50% seams), 76-78 mid with 2x2 order,
              79/80(/81) near, checkers that skip a step (79/81), + sparse single light pixels next to
              dark ones (contrast pairs) + warm 51/48 specks growing toward the viewer.
"""
import sys
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk

X0, Y0, W, H = 0, 282, 92, 82


def sketch():
    """copyist's sketch: polylines (x, y) in crop pixels with widths (stretched units, ky)."""
    thread = [(95, 1.5), (72, 2.2), (40, 6.6), (40, 8.0), (55, 9.2), (55, 10.6), (22, 11.2), (4, 12.4),
              (8, 15.4), (0, 17.3)]
    far = [(96, 21.3), (84, 22.3), (64, 24.2), (46, 26.0), (35, 27.3)]
    prong_a = [(35, 27.5), (37, 30.5)]
    prong_b = [(44, 27.6), (46, 30.6)]
    left = [(47, 31.3), (26, 31.6), (-4, 31.2)]
    main = [(37, 32.2), (22, 34.2), (26, 36.6), (26, 38.2), (16, 40.6), (15, 42.6), (20, 44.8), (30, 47.8),
            (40, 50.6), (50, 53.4), (52, 55.0), (48, 57.4), (35, 60.2), (20, 64.0), (6, 68.0), (-8, 73.0)]
    return [(thread, [3.0] * len(thread)),
            (far, [7, 8, 9, 10, 10]),
            (prong_a, [5, 5]), (prong_b, [5, 5]),
            (left, [10, 11, 11]),
            (main, [8, 9, 9, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18, 22, 26, 30, 34])]


KY = 4.0


def water_mask(h, w):
    f = np.full((h, w), 1e9)
    cl = np.zeros((h, w), bool)
    for pts, wid in sketch():
        f = np.minimum(f, pk.polyline_field(h, w, pts, wid, ky=KY))
        # the centre-line itself is always water (thin threads never break)
        for (ax, ay), (bx, by) in zip(pts[:-1], pts[1:]):
            n = int(max(abs(bx - ax), abs(by - ay)) * 2) + 2
            for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n):
                x, y = int(round(ax + t * (bx - ax))), int(round(ay + t * (by - ay) - 0.5))
                if 0 <= x < w and 0 <= y < h:
                    cl[y, x] = True
    return (f < 0) | cl


def marsh(h, w, rng):
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    # base step along the ramp 73..81 (copyist's value study, by row)
    v = np.interp(y, [282, 286, 292, 298, 302, 306, 312, 318, 324, 332, 340, 364],
                  [73.0, 73.0, 74.0, 75.0, 75.5, 76.0, 77.0, 77.8, 78.7, 79.0, 79.3, 79.5])
    far = y < 304
    out = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    # far: banded + ordered 2x2 (flat rows, 50% seams)
    vb = pk.banded(v, 0.55)
    out[far] = pk.ordered(vb)[far]
    # mid/near: ordered with growing jitter; near checkers skip a step (79/81)
    amp = np.clip((y - 304) / 40.0, 0, 1)
    jit = (rng.random((h, w)) - 0.5) * (0.6 + 1.4 * amp)
    vn = v + jit
    o = pk.ordered(vn)
    skip = (y > 336) & (o == 80) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.45)
    o = np.where(skip, np.where((xx + yy) % 2 == 0, 79, 81), o)
    out[~far] = o[~far]
    # contrast pairs: single light pixels (75) sitting beside a dark one
    lp = (~far) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.03 * (0.5 + amp))
    out[lp] = 75
    # dark specks and warm specks toward the viewer
    ds = (y > 335) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.04 * amp)
    out[ds] = 82
    ws = (y > 328) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.07 * amp)
    out[ws] = np.where(rng.random((h, w)) < 0.8, 51, 48)[ws]
    return np.clip(out, 48, 84)


def water_fill(mask, rng):
    h, w = mask.shape
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    # the reflected-sky step climbs with nearness
    v = np.interp(y, [283, 290, 296, 304, 309, 316, 320, 324, 329, 334, 340], [2.3, 3.0, 3.5, 4.0, 4.5, 5.0, 5.5, 6.0, 6.5, 7.0, 7.6])
    v = v + (pk.value_noise(h, w, 8, rng, 1, aniso=(0.4, 2.0)) - 0.5) * 0.5
    base = pk.ordered(pk.banded(v, 0.7))
    out = np.where(mask, base, 0)
    # hot glints: short horizontal clusters of 42/43 in the mid ribbons
    g = mask & (y > 300) & (y < 336)
    seeds = g & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.035)
    for sy, sx in zip(*np.nonzero(seeds)):
        L = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        c = 43 if rng.random() < 0.5 else 42
        for k in range(L):
            if sx + k < w and mask[sy, sx + k]:
                out[sy, sx + k] = c if k % 2 == 0 or rng.random() < 0.5 else 40 + int(rng.integers(0, 2))
    # near glitter: checker of cycled peach against static 7/8/3/40
    near = mask & (y >= 337)
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    cyc = rng.integers(128, 136, (h, w))
    pink = rng.integers(160, 168, (h, w))
    cyc = np.where((y > 346) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.35), pink, cyc)
    stat = rng.choice([7, 8, 8, 7, 3, 40, 4, 36], (h, w))
    out = np.where(near & par, cyc, out)
    out = np.where(near & ~par, stat, out)
    out = np.where(near & (y > 347) & ~par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.5), cyc, out)
    return out


def edges(canvas, mask, rng):
    h, w = mask.shape
    out = canvas.copy()
    up = np.zeros_like(mask); up[1:] = mask[:-1]          # water directly above this pixel
    dn = np.zeros_like(mask); dn[:-1] = mask[1:]          # water directly below
    land = ~mask
    # near side: land pixel under water -> light lip 73
    lip = land & up
    out[lip] = 73
    # far side: land pixel over water -> dark 80, two rows where the water edge is long
    far1 = land & dn
    out[far1] = 80
    # horizontal ends of runs: which side are they on?  look at the edge's vertical facing
    # left/right ends of each row's water run
    lft = np.zeros_like(mask); lft[:, :-1] = mask[:, 1:]
    rgt = np.zeros_like(mask); rgt[:, 1:] = mask[:, :-1]
    for y in range(h):
        for x in range(w):
            if not land[y, x] or lip[y, x] or far1[y, x]:
                continue
            if lft[y, x] or rgt[y, x]:
                # decide by where the water is going: water below-left/right -> we are far side (dark),
                # water above -> near side (light)
                a = mask[max(0, y - 2):y, max(0, x - 3):x + 4].sum()
                b = mask[y + 1:y + 3, max(0, x - 3):x + 4].sum()
                if b > a:
                    # far side: run of dark extending away from the water
                    d = -1 if lft[y, x] else 1
                    n = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
                    for k in range(n):
                        xx = x + d * k
                        if 0 <= xx < w and land[y, xx]:
                            out[y, xx] = 80
                else:
                    d = -1 if lft[y, x] else 1
                    n = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
                    for k in range(n):
                        xx = x + d * k
                        if 0 <= xx < w and land[y, xx]:
                            out[y, xx] = 73 if k < n - 1 else 75
    return out


def paint(seed=3):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    m = water_mask(H, W)
    cv = marsh(H, W, rng)
    wf = water_fill(m, rng)
    cv = np.where(m, wf, cv)
    cv = edges(cv, m, rng)
    return cv, m


if __name__ == '__main__':
    d = pk.lw_load('V02')
    ref_idx = d['idx'][Y0:Y0 + H, X0:X0 + W]
    pal = d['pal']
    cv, m = paint(int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 3)
    ref = pk.rgb(ref_idx, pal); cp = pk.rgb(cv, pal)
    tag = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 'v1'
    pk.compare_sheet(ref, cp, 'study/c0a_%s.png' % tag, [(0, 34, 60, 40), (30, 0, 60, 30)], label=tag)
    W_set = set(list(range(1, 48)) + list(range(128, 136)) + list(range(160, 168)))
    rm = np.isin(ref_idx, list(W_set))
    print(pk.metrics(cp, ref), 'water IoU %.2f' % ((rm & m).sum() / (rm | m).sum()))

Bash: Add edge profile diagnostic to pixkit
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> pixkit.py <<'EOF'


def edge_profile(lum, mask, y0=0, maxk=10):
    """mean luminance of land pixels by rows to the nearest water below (far bank) / above (near bank)."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    A = [[] for _ in range(maxk + 1)]; B = [[] for _ in range(maxk + 1)]
    for x in range(w):
        col = mask[:, x]
        for y in range(y0, h):
            if col[y]:
                continue
            for k in range(1, maxk + 1):
                if y + k < h and col[y + k]:
                    A[k].append(lum[y, x]); break
            for k in range(1, maxk + 1):
                if y - k >= 0 and col[y - k]:
                    B[k].append(lum[y, x]); break
    f = lambda L: [round(float(np.mean(v)), 0) if v else None for v in L[1:]]
    return f(A), f(B)


def luma(idx, pal):
    p = pal.astype(float)
    return (0.3 * p[:, 0] + 0.59 * p[:, 1] + 0.11 * p[:, 2])[np.clip(idx, 0, len(pal) - 1)]
EOF

import re
s=open('c0a_v02.py').read()
start=s.index('def water_mask'); end=s.index("if __name__")
new='''def fields(h, w):
    """F: signed distance (stretched units, <0 inside) to the ribbons; thread: 1-row centre-line raster."""
    F = np.full((h, w), 1e9)
    thread = np.zeros((h, w), bool)
    for k, (pts, wid) in enumerate(sketch()):
        if k == 0:
            for (ax, ay), (bx, by) in zip(pts[:-1], pts[1:]):
                n = int(max(abs(bx - ax), abs(by - ay)) * 2) + 2
                for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n):
                    x, y = int(round(ax + t * (bx - ax))), int(np.floor(ay + t * (by - ay)))
                    if 0 <= x < w and 0 <= y < h:
                        thread[y, x] = True
            continue
        F = np.minimum(F, pk.polyline_field(h, w, pts, wid, ky=KY))
    return F, thread


def marsh(h, w, rng, F, gy, steep):
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    # base step along the ramp 73..81 (copyist's value study, by row)
    v = np.interp(y, [282, 286, 292, 298, 302, 306, 312, 318, 324, 332, 340, 364],
                  [73.0, 73.0, 74.0, 75.0, 75.5, 76.0, 76.8, 77.6, 78.4, 78.9, 79.3, 79.6])
    v = v + (pk.value_noise(h, w, 10, rng, 2, aniso=(0.35, 2.5)) - 0.5) * np.clip((y - 300) / 30, 0, 1) * 1.2
    # banks: the far side darkens over ~3 rows toward the water, the near side has a lit lip then a trough
    near_mid = np.clip((y - 300) / 12.0, 0, 1)
    far_side = (gy < 0) & (F >= 0)
    near_side = (gy >= 0) & (F >= 0)
    v = v + far_side * near_mid * np.interp(F, [0, 4, 8, 12, 16], [2.2, 1.4, 0.7, 0.2, 0.0])
    v = v + near_side * near_mid * np.interp(F, [0, 4, 8, 14, 22, 30], [0, -1.0, 0.0, 0.5, 0.3, 0.0])
    far = y < 300
    out = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    out[far] = pk.ordered(pk.banded(v, 0.55))[far]
    amp = np.clip((y - 304) / 40.0, 0, 1)
    o = pk.ordered(v + (rng.random((h, w)) - 0.5) * (0.2 + 0.9 * amp), pk.BAYER4)
    skip = (y > 336) & (o == 80) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.45)
    o = np.where(skip, np.where((xx + yy) % 2 == 0, 79, 81), o)
    out[~far] = o[~far]
    lp = (~far) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.025 * (0.5 + amp)) & (F > 6)
    out[lp] = 75
    ds = (y > 335) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.04 * amp)
    out[ds] = 82
    ws = (y > 328) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.06 * amp) & (F > 6)
    out[ws] = np.where(rng.random((h, w)) < 0.8, 51, 48)[ws]
    return np.clip(out, 48, 84)


def water_fill(mask, rng):
    h, w = mask.shape
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    v = np.interp(y, [283, 290, 296, 304, 309, 316, 320, 324, 329, 334, 340], [2.3, 3.0, 3.5, 4.0, 4.5, 5.0, 5.5, 6.0, 6.5, 7.0, 7.6])
    v = v + (pk.value_noise(h, w, 8, rng, 1, aniso=(0.4, 2.0)) - 0.5) * 0.5
    base = pk.ordered(pk.banded(v, 0.7))
    out = np.where(mask, base, 0)
    g = mask & (y > 300) & (y < 336)
    seeds = g & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.03)
    for sy, sx in zip(*np.nonzero(seeds)):
        L = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        c = 43 if rng.random() < 0.5 else 42
        for k in range(L):
            if sx + k < w and mask[sy, sx + k]:
                out[sy, sx + k] = c if k % 2 == 0 or rng.random() < 0.5 else 40 + int(rng.integers(0, 2))
    near = mask & (y >= 337)
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    cyc = rng.integers(128, 136, (h, w))
    pink = rng.integers(160, 168, (h, w))
    cyc = np.where((y > 346) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.35), pink, cyc)
    stat = rng.choice([7, 8, 8, 7, 3, 40, 4, 36], (h, w))
    out = np.where(near & par, cyc, out)
    out = np.where(near & ~par, stat, out)
    out = np.where(near & (y > 347) & ~par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.5), cyc, out)
    return out


def edges(cv, F, gy, steep, rng):
    h, w = F.shape
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    out = cv.copy()
    mid = y >= 300
    nearish = y >= 312
    # near side, land: the lit lip (1 row on flats, a horizontal run on diagonals)
    lip = mid & (gy >= 0) & (F >= 0) & (F < KY)
    out[lip] = np.where(nearish[lip], 73, 74)
    # near side, water, steep diagonal: water/75 checker
    wl = nearish & (gy >= 0) & (F < 0) & (F > -KY) & steep & par
    out[wl] = 75
    # warm band beyond the lip on the steep near edge of the near ribbon
    warm = (y > 336) & (gy >= 0) & (F >= KY) & (F < 3 * KY) & steep
    out[warm] = np.where(F[warm] < 2 * KY, 51, 52)
    # far side, land: the dark bank face
    dk = mid & (gy < 0) & (F >= 0) & (F < KY)
    out[dk] = np.where(nearish[dk], 80, 78)
    # far side, water, steep: checker fringe of dark (cycled darks 233..239)
    fr = nearish & (gy < 0) & (F < 0) & (F > -KY) & steep & par
    out[fr] = rng.choice([80, 233, 234, 237, 239], fr.sum())
    return out


def paint(seed=3):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    F, thread = fields(H, W)
    gy, gx = np.gradient(F)
    steep = np.abs(gx) > 0.6 * np.abs(gy) / KY * 4      # edge normal mostly horizontal
    m = (F < 0) | thread
    cv = marsh(H, W, rng, F, gy, steep)
    cv = np.where(m, water_fill(m, rng), cv)
    cv = edges(cv, F, gy, steep, rng)
    cv[thread] = water_fill(thread, rng)[thread]
    return cv, m

Bash: Column-distance bank darkening; run v8

def col_dist(mask, maxd=64):
    """per pixel: rows to the nearest mask pixel below (db) and above (da) in the same column."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    db = np.full((h, w), maxd, float); da = np.full((h, w), maxd, float)
    cur = np.full(w, maxd, float)
    for y in range(h - 1, -1, -1):
        cur = np.where(mask[y], 0, np.minimum(cur + 1, maxd)); db[y] = cur
    cur = np.full(w, maxd, float)
    for y in range(h):
        cur = np.where(mask[y], 0, np.minimum(cur + 1, maxd)); da[y] = cur
    return db, da
EOF

38. elements/channel/study/v11_cropB.png

Write: elements/channel/src/c0b_v11.py
"""Copy B: the river with a riffle and rocks in V11AM (Living Worlds, monoliths scene, morning),
native crop (370,335, 120x90). Ferrari's own palette indices; procedure + copyist's sketch.

Discovered procedure (read off his index map):
  STILL WATER  the sky ramp 7..19 (lilac -> blue, near-isovalue), climbing ~1 step per 7 rows toward
               the viewer, as clean ORDERED patterns per row (8888 / 8889 / 8989 / 9a9a ...).
  REFLECTION   the dark mesa's reflection has a wide 50% CHECKER edge (5-6 px across), whose water
  EDGES        pixels are colour-cycled darker blues (209..216): the soft edge is the shimmer.
  RIFFLE       a fan of thin strokes of cycled mid-blues (192..202, darker than the mirror) drawn on
               the checker lattice (every other pixel), rising from the rocks and leaning outward,
               dense at the foot, sparse at the tips: flow seen end-on at low angle.
  NEAR DASHES  near the viewer: long dotted rows (cycled index on every other pixel), consecutive
               cycle indices along a dash so a light runs along it when the palette turns.
  ROCKS        flat dark slabs, a lighter top row, warm maroon (70/82) on the sun-side face and in the
               checker below, a dark checker reflection hanging under them.
"""
import sys
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
from matplotlib.path import Path

X0, Y0, W, H = 370, 335, 120, 90
DARK = [92, 93, 94, 95, 69, 57, 54]


def poly(h, w, pts):
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    return Path(pts).contains_points(np.c_[xx.ravel() + 0.5, yy.ravel() + 0.5]).reshape(h, w)


def sketch():
    mesa = [(-1, -1), (52, -1), (30, 2), (14, 5), (4, 8), (8, 13), (14, 16), (26, 17), (26, 22), (12, 24),
            (4, 27), (-1, 29)]
    bank_r = [(121, 13), (104, 15), (92, 17), (86, 19), (90, 22), (104, 28), (116, 33), (121, 34)]
    rocks = [[(0, 47), (18, 46), (22, 49), (22, 57), (12, 60), (0, 61)],
             [(47, 63), (70, 62), (76, 66), (76, 74), (64, 76), (48, 73)],
             [(35, 69), (42, 68), (45, 70), (40, 72), (34, 71)],
             [(17, 57), (30, 56), (33, 59), (18, 60)]]
    bank_n = [(62, 91), (72, 84), (90, 76), (106, 70), (121, 67), (121, 91)]
    fan = dict(cx=72, base=66, top=20, spread=34)
    return mesa, bank_r, rocks, bank_n, fan


def fringe(mask, width_px, par):
    """pixels within width_px (horizontal) of the mask, outside it."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    out = np.zeros_like(mask)
    for k in range(1, width_px + 1):
        out[:, :-k] |= mask[:, k:]
        out[:, k:] |= mask[:, :-k]
    return out & ~mask


def paint(seed=1):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    h, w = H, W
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    mesa, bank_r, rocks, bank_n, fan = sketch()
    # still water: sky ramp climbing with nearness, ordered 4x4, banded
    v = np.interp(y, [338, 344, 350, 355, 358, 364, 369, 374, 380, 386, 393, 399, 405, 410, 416, 424],
                  [8.0, 9.0, 10.0, 10.5, 11.0, 12.0, 12.5, 13.0, 14.0, 14.5, 15.0, 16.0, 16.5, 17.0, 18.0, 18.5])
    cv = pk.ordered(pk.banded(v, 0.6), pk.BAYER4)
    # mesa reflection: dark body, wide checker edge whose water pixels cycle (209..216)
    M = poly(h, w, mesa)
    band = np.zeros_like(M)
    for k in range(1, 7):
        sh = np.zeros_like(M); sh[:-k] = M[k:]   # pixels above... use a soft dilation in both axes
        band |= sh
    fr = fringe(M, 14, par) & (yy < 30)
    dilated = fr | band
    cv[M] = rng.choice(DARK[:4], M.sum())
    e = dilated & ~M
    cv[e & par] = rng.choice(DARK[:3], (e & par).sum())
    cv[e & ~par] = rng.integers(209, 217, (e & ~par).sum())
    # right bank: dark + warm checker, lower edge checker into water
    B = poly(h, w, bank_r)
    warm = pk.value_noise(h, w, 5, rng, 2) > 0.5
    cv[B] = np.where(par & warm, 82, np.where(warm & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.4), 70, 92))[B]
    be = fringe(B, 3, par) & (yy > 17)
    cv[be & par] = 82
    # riffle fan: strokes on the checker lattice, cycled 192..202
    cx, base, top, spread = fan['cx'], fan['base'], fan['top'], fan['spread']
    for i in range(170):
        x0 = cx + rng.normal(0, spread * 0.45)
        y0 = base - rng.random() * 8
        L = int(rng.integers(4, 26) * (1 - abs(x0 - cx) / (spread * 1.6)))
        lean = (x0 - cx) / spread * 0.9 + rng.normal(0, 0.15)
        x, yv = x0, y0
        k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
        for s in range(max(L, 2)):
            yi, xi = int(yv), int(round(x))
            if (xi + yi) % 2:
                xi += 1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1
            if 0 <= yi < h and 0 <= xi < w and yi > top and not M[yi, xi] and not B[yi, xi]:
                if rng.random() < 0.8:
                    cv[yi, xi] = 192 + (k0 + s) % 11
            yv -= 1; x += lean
    # near dotted dashes (cycled, consecutive indices along the dash)
    for yd in range(60, h):
        pr = np.interp(yd, [60, 64, 70, 78, 90], [0.0, 0.5, 0.3, 0.45, 0.35])
        x = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
        while x < w:
            if rng.random() < pr * 0.35:
                L = int(rng.integers(3, 12)); k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
                ramp = (192, 11) if yd < 79 else (209, 8)
                for s in range(L):
                    xi = x + 2 * s
                    xi += (xi + yd) % 2
                    if xi < w:
                        cv[yd, xi] = ramp[0] + (k0 - s) % ramp[1]
                x += 2 * L + int(rng.integers(2, 10))
            else:
                x += int(rng.integers(2, 8))
    # rocks: dark slab, lighter top row, warm maroon on the right face, checker reflection below
    for rp in rocks:
        R = poly(h, w, rp)
        cv[R] = rng.choice([92, 93, 94, 69], R.sum(), p=[0.3, 0.3, 0.2, 0.2])
        top_ = R & ~np.roll(R, 1, 0)
        cv[top_] = 57
        rx = np.array([p[0] for p in rp]); right = R & (xx > rx.max() - 5)
        cv[right & par] = 82; cv[right & ~par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.4)] = 70
        refl = np.zeros_like(R)
        for k in range(1, 4):
            refl[k:] |= R[:-k]
        refl &= ~R
        cv[refl & par] = np.where(rng.random((h, w)) < 0.6, 92, 82)[refl & par]
    # near bank
    N = poly(h, w, bank_n)
    cv[N] = np.where(par & (pk.value_noise(h, w, 4, rng, 2) > 0.62), 82,
                     rng.choice([92, 93, 94, 95], (h, w)))[N]
    ne = fringe(N, 2, par)
    cv[ne & par] = rng.choice([92, 82], (ne & par).sum())
    return cv


if __name__ == '__main__':
    d = pk.lw_load('V11AM')
    ref_idx = d['idx'][Y0:Y0 + H, X0:X0 + W]
    pal = d['pal']
    cv = paint(int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 1)
    ref = pk.rgb(ref_idx, pal); cp = pk.rgb(cv, pal)
    tag = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 'v1'
    pk.compare_sheet(ref, cp, 'study/c0b_%s.png' % tag, [(40, 20, 60, 40), (0, 50, 60, 40)], label=tag)
    print(pk.metrics(cp, ref))
Bash: Run copy B v1

Bash: Riffle as solid body with rising chains
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('c0b_v11.py').read()
a=s.index("    # riffle fan: strokes"); b=s.index("    # near dotted dashes")
s=s[:a]+'''    # riffle: a solid body of cycled bluer tones (facets tilted toward us mirror higher sky),
    # ragged top dissolving through checker, 45-degree pixel chains rising and fanning from it
    Rb = poly(h, w, fan['body'])
    tone = pk.value_noise(h, w, 4, rng, 2)
    body = 192 + np.clip((tone * 9).astype(int), 0, 10)
    cv[Rb] = body[Rb]
    lil = Rb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.08)
    cv[lil] = rng.choice([219, 220, 221, 205], lil.sum())
    dk = Rb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.05)
    cv[dk] = rng.choice([210, 211, 214, 215], dk.sum())
    # checker transition above the body (2-4 rows)
    above = np.zeros_like(Rb)
    for k in range(1, 4):
        above[:-k] |= Rb[k:]
    above &= ~Rb
    tr = above & par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.8)
    cv[tr] = body[tr]
    # rising chains from the top edge, leaning outward from the fan centre
    tops = [(x, int(np.argmax(Rb[:, x]))) for x in range(w) if Rb[:, x].any()]
    for i in range(fan['n']):
        x0, y0 = tops[int(rng.integers(0, len(tops)))]
        L = int(rng.integers(3, fan['maxlen']))
        side = 1 if (x0 > fan['cx'] or rng.random() < 0.3) and rng.random() < 0.8 else -1
        k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
        xi, yi = x0, y0 - 1
        steep = rng.random() < 0.35
        for st in range(L):
            if (xi + yi) % 2:
                xi += side
            if 0 <= yi < h and 0 <= xi < w and not M[yi, xi] and not B[yi, xi]:
                if rng.random() < 0.9:
                    cv[yi, xi] = 192 + (k0 + st) % 11
            yi -= 1
            if not steep or st % 2:
                xi += side
            if st == L // 2 and rng.random() < 0.3:
                side = -side          # the arch turns over: a fountain
'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace("    fan = dict(cx=72, base=66, top=20, spread=34)",
"""    body = [(0, 44), (14, 42), (30, 40), (44, 43), (56, 41), (66, 44), (78, 46), (90, 52), (98, 58), (100, 64),
            (90, 64), (76, 70), (60, 66), (44, 63), (30, 60), (18, 58), (0, 60)]
    fan = dict(cx=60, body=body, n=110, maxlen=18)""")
open('c0b_v11.py','w').write(s)

Bash: Strand body and curling chains for riffle
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('c0b_v11.py').read()
a=s.index("    tone = pk.value_noise(h, w, 4, rng, 2)"); b=s.index("    # checker transition above the body")
s=s[:a]+'''    # body as vertical strands of adjacent cycle tones (run 2-5), like grass strands
    body = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    tone = pk.value_noise(h, w, 6, rng, 2)
    for x in range(w):
        yv = 0
        while yv < h:
            L = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
            t = int(np.clip(tone[yv, x] * 11 + rng.normal(0, 1.8), 0, 10))
            body[yv:yv + L, x] = 192 + t
            yv += L
    cv[Rb] = body[Rb]
    lil = Rb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.06)
    cv[lil] = rng.choice([219, 220, 221, 205], lil.sum())
    dk = Rb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.04)
    cv[dk] = rng.choice([210, 211, 214, 215], dk.sum())
'''+s[b:]
a=s.index("    tops = [(x, int(np.argmax"); b=s.index("    # near dotted dashes")
s=s[:a]+'''    tops = [(x, int(np.argmax(Rb[:, x]))) for x in range(w) if Rb[:, x].any()]
    for i in range(fan['n']):
        x0, y0 = tops[int(rng.integers(0, len(tops)))]
        L = int(rng.integers(3, fan['maxlen']))
        out = np.sign(x0 - fan['cx'] + rng.normal(0, 8)) or 1.0
        lean0 = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.35) * out
        curl = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.08) * out
        k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
        x = float(x0)
        for st in range(L):
            yi = y0 - 1 - st
            xi = int(round(x))
            if (xi + yi) % 2:
                xi += 1 if x > xi else -1
            if 0 <= yi < h and 0 <= xi < w and not M[yi, xi] and not B[yi, xi] and rng.random() < 0.88:
                cv[yi, xi] = 192 + (k0 + st) % 11
            x += lean0 + curl * st
'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace("fan = dict(cx=60, body=body, n=110, maxlen=18)","fan = dict(cx=58, body=body, n=70, maxlen=16)")

Bash: Riffle as density field with jets
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('c0b_v11.py').read()
a=s.index("    Rb = poly(h, w, fan['body'])"); b=s.index("    # rising chains from the top edge")
s=s[:a]+'''    Rb0 = poly(h, w, fan['body'])
    # density: 1 in the body core, decaying upward over ~12 rows, cut into vertical jets
    from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt
    dup = distance_transform_edt(~Rb0)                      # px from the body
    core = distance_transform_edt(Rb0)
    jets = pk.value_noise(h, w, 3, rng, 2, aniso=(4.0, 0.5))
    D = np.where(Rb0, np.clip(0.55 + core * 0.12, 0, 1), np.exp(-dup / 5.0) * 0.8)
    D = D * (0.55 + 0.9 * jets)
    body = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    tone = pk.value_noise(h, w, 6, rng, 2)
    for x in range(w):
        yv = 0
        while yv < h:
            L = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
            t = int(np.clip(tone[yv, x] * 11 + rng.normal(0, 1.8), 0, 10))
            body[yv:yv + L, x] = 192 + t
            yv += L
    solid = D > 0.78
    chk = (D > 0.42) & ~solid & par
    spr = (D > 0.2) & (D <= 0.42) & par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.35)
    Rb = solid | chk | spr
    Rb &= ~M & ~B
    cv[Rb] = body[Rb]
    lil = Rb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.06)
    cv[lil] = rng.choice([219, 220, 221, 205], lil.sum())
    dk = Rb & solid & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.05)
    cv[dk] = rng.choice([210, 211, 214, 215], dk.sum())
    Rb = solid
'''+s[b:]
a=s.index("    # checker transition above the body"); b=s.index("    # rising chains from the top edge")
s=s[:a]+s[b:]
open('c0b_v11.py','w').write(s)
EOF

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 1 — 2026-09-25 21:22 — stage 0 master copy, `study/c0a_v4.png` (V02 winding ribbons)
**Verdict: REVISE** — the far ribbons are nearly there; the near ribbon gives itself away. (My read; your call. The bar: indistinguishable from his crop at 8× except by subject.)

What's working:

- **Far band (bottom 8× pair).** The thin ribbons as 1-px-tall horizontal runs with stepped joins, and the doubled loop, are very close to his. The sky-lilac field with its faint checker also matches.
- The value plan of the near crop (dark banks, bright ribbon, glinting gravel band) is right at 3×.

What gives it away:

1. **The ruled diagonal.** In "COPY 3x" and the upper 8× copy, the lower edge of the glinting band and the dark wedge at the bottom right are one straight 45° line from roughly x=200 to x=430 (3×). He has no straight edge there: the glint band dies out upward and downward through thinning dither, with scattered orange and pink specks spilling into the dark. A ruled diagonal is the fastest giveaway in the whole copy.
2. **The ribbon reads as a road.** Your near ribbon keeps a nearly constant width with parallel edges, and a clean V at the bend. His ribbon breathes: the banks bite into it with stepped notches 2–4 px deep, dark tongues of bank overlap the water, and the width pinches and swells. Look at the ref 8× around (180–260, 330–420): the upper edge of his ribbon is a series of little stepped bays. That irregularity is what makes it water in a gravel bed instead of paint on a surface.
3. **The dark banks are too smooth.** His near-left bank carries patches of lighter-violet checker in irregular clumps (vegetation texture). Yours has the checker, but spread more evenly.

Next: kill the ruled edge first (1), then give the near ribbon's banks the stepped bites (2).

def photo_crop(path, box, size):
    """crop box=(x,y,w,h) of a photo and area-resample to size=(w,h) native pixels."""
    x, y, w, h = box
    im = Image.open(path).convert('RGB').crop((x, y, x + w, y + h)).resize(size, Image.BOX)
    return np.asarray(im)


def triptych(photo, pixels, ferrari, out, s=8, labels=('photo', 'mine', 'Ferrari')):
    items = [(a, l) for a, l in zip((photo, pixels, ferrari), labels) if a is not None]
    panel(items, s).save(out)
EOF

# Braided-river channels, after Mark Ferrari

A student's notebook. Each stage has its images, what I learned, and an honest comparison. All images are made by programs in `src/`; every pixel is a palette index chosen by a rule. Comparisons are at native pixels, nearest-neighbour, 8× unless marked.

Photos: `~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_*` and `photos/` (sources in `photos/sources.tsv`).

---

## Stage 0: copying Ferrari first

Before painting my own water I looked for the passages of his work nearest my subject, and found two better than the ones I was pointed to:

- **V02 ("Mountain Fortress Dusk")**: winding low-angle ribbons of reflected sunset through a dark marsh. This is a braided plain seen from low down, painted by him.
- **V11AM (the monoliths, morning)**: a river at low angle with a riffle, rocks, the soft edge of a dark reflection, and near-water ripple dashes. This is my stage 3.

Mirror Pond's water edge had already been copied by the master-copy student (`pedagogy/master-copy/out/cmp_c4.png`), so I used it as reading, not as a copy.

His index data is public (Canvas Cycle JSON), so I could read the **index map** directly: which colours are sky ramp, which are colour-cycled, and what sits next to what. `src/pixkit.py lw_load`.

### Copy A: V02 ribbons (`src/c0a_v02.py`, native crop (0,282) 72×82)

![copy A](out/s0_copyA_v02.png)
![photo | copy | Ferrari](out/s0_triptych_A.png)

What I found in his index map (every item below is measured, not guessed):

1. **The water is the sky ramp, and the index climbs with nearness.** Indices 1–15 are his sky, horizon (hot pink/orange) to zenith (violet), all near one value (luma 155–174). The far threads use 2/3, the middle ribbons 4/5, then 6/7, and the near ribbon 7/8. Nearer water mirrors higher sky. Steps hold flat and cross as *sustained* 50% checkers (`4545` for several rows), not narrow seams.
2. **Shape at low angle is horizontal runs.** A far thread is exactly one row tall and steps a row at a time (runs of 5–15 px). A turn is a staircase. Thickness in rows grows toward the viewer. A ribbon going toward you is wide in x and thin in y, so I rasterise the ribbons as thick polylines in a space where y is stretched ×4.
3. **Near edge light, far edge dark.** Land directly under water (the near bank) gets a 1-row lip in his lightest marsh colour (73, luma 121). Land directly over water (the far bank) is darkest (80, luma 36), in runs of 2–7 px along the edge. On a steep diagonal the far edge dissolves into a water/dark checker whose dark pixels are *colour-cycled* (the shimmer sits on reflection edges, as the water study found), and the near edge becomes a 75/water checker, then 73, then a warm band of 51/52.
   Measured as mean luma of land by rows from the water: far bank 61, 60, 65, 69, 74, 75, 76; near bank **100**, 78, 66, 65, 63, 64, 68. The far bank darkens over about 3 rows; the near bank has one bright row, one medium row, and then a slight trough.
4. **The far bank's dark band widens toward the viewer.** A bank face projects taller when nearer, so between two arms the whole wedge goes dark.
5. **The marsh is ordered dither of a slow field.** Far: flat rows of 73/74/75 with 50% checker seams (banded). Middle: 4×4 Bayer patterns (`GGGF GGGF`, period 4 or 8 in a row) of 76–78. Near: checkers that *skip* a step (79/81) plus sparse warm specks (51/48) and single light pixels beside dark ones.
6. **Hot glints** are clusters of 1–4 px of bright orange (42/43) on the far ribbons, sparse, horizontal.
7. **The near ribbon is glitter:** a checker of colour-cycled peach (128–135) against static sky steps. In a still frame it is a sparkle of several tones.

**Honest comparison.** At 3× the masses, values and the far band match. The far threads at 8× are nearly indistinguishable. The critic's round 1 caught two tells, both fixed in v10: a ruled 45° lower edge on the glitter band, and a near ribbon of constant width like a road. His banks bite into the water in stepped notches; I now add blocky noise, held flat over 2–4 px runs, to the ribbon field. His glitter also thins out through dither with specks spilling into the dark.
**Still not his:** the ribbon *shapes* come from my rough sketch, so the arm at the turn is too wide. The dissolve along the near ribbon's lower edge is a little mottled where his is crisper. At 8× a trained eye would still pick mine, mostly on shape, not on procedure.

### Copy B: V11AM river, riffle and rocks (`src/c0b_v11.py`, native crop (370,335) 120×90)

![copy B](out/s0_copyB_v11.png)

What his index map says:

1. **Still water** is again the sky ramp (7–19, lilac to blue, near-isovalue), climbing about 1 step every 7 rows toward the viewer, in clean ordered per-row patterns (`8888`, `8889`, `8989`, `9a9a`).
2. **A dark reflection's edge is a wide graded checker** (dark with colour-cycled darker blues 209–216) that spreads sideways: about 15 px horizontally on a shallow edge.
3. **The riffle is a zone where the mirror shows higher sky.** Its marks are *bluer and darker* than the lilac mirror, because facets tilted toward you reflect higher sky. It is laid out as a density field: a solid core of cycled tones, a checker margin, then sparse checker spray, cut into vertical jets. Thin pixel chains rise from the top, on the checker lattice, leaning and curling outward like a fountain. Dark horizontal trough bands lie on its downstream side. (The mark map is `study/v11_marks.png`.)
4. **Near ripples are dotted dashes:** a cycled index on every other pixel, with *consecutive* cycle indices along the dash, so that when the palette turns a light runs along the dash. On a still frame they read as dotted strokes of varied tone.
5. **Rocks** are flat dark slabs with a lighter top, a warm maroon face, and a dark checker reflection under them.

**Honest comparison.** At 3× it reads as the same kind of passage. At 8× my riffle is busier, with too many lilac pixels, and his chains are longer and fewer. My rocks lack his lit top planes. My sketch is also rougher here than in copy A. **Partly matched.** I stopped at the point where the *procedure* was captured, because the braided channel only needs the riffle grammar. The remaining gaps are named above.

### What I take forward from stage 0

- The water is the sky ramp, indexed by the reflected elevation. Nearer means higher sky.
- Low-angle shape means horizontal runs with a y-stretched thickness.
- Near edge light, far edge dark. The far dark widens with nearness.
- Moving water means marks in "higher sky" tones, placed by a density field; chains on the checker lattice; dotted dashes.
- Only reflection edges and marks shimmer; I keep the cycled indices as a reserved palette block.

Write: elements/channel/src/channel.py
"""channel.py -- the braided-channel painter (grows stage by stage; see LESSONS.md).

Index canvas + named palette. Materials own index ranges; the water REUSES the sky ramp (Ferrari)
plus a few water-only tints. Everything a water pixel shows is chosen by a rule about something
else: the sky at the reflected elevation, the object standing at the reflected ray, the bed seen
through it.
"""
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- palette ---------------------
class Pal:
    """named ramps -> global indices. `grey` palettes for the value stages; colour later."""

    def __init__(self, ramps):
        self.ramps = {}
        self.cols = []
        for name, cols in ramps:
            self.ramps[name] = list(range(len(self.cols), len(self.cols) + len(cols)))
            self.cols += [tuple(int(v) for v in c) for c in cols]
        self.arr = np.array(self.cols, dtype=np.uint8)

    def r(self, name):
        return np.array(self.ramps[name])

    def rgb(self, idx):
        return self.arr[idx]


def grey(*vals):
    return [(v, v, v) for v in vals]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- camera ----------------------
class Cam:
    """pinhole, small pitch: row = hz + f*tan(elev). Ground is y=0, eye at height h."""

    def __init__(self, W, H, f, h, pitch_deg):
        self.W, self.H, self.f, self.h = W, H, f, h
        self.hz = H / 2 - f * np.tan(np.radians(pitch_deg))

    def row_of(self, z, y=0.0):
        """screen row of a point at distance z (m) and height y (m)."""
        return self.hz + self.f * (self.h - y) / z

    def mirror_row(self, z, y):
        """row where the reflection of (z, y) appears (mirror camera at -h)."""
        return self.hz + self.f * (self.h + y) / z

    def z_of_row(self, r):
        d = np.maximum(r - self.hz, 1e-3)
        return self.f * self.h / d

    def elev_of_row(self, r):
        """elevation angle (rad) of the view ray through row r (negative = below horizon)."""
        return -np.arctan((r - self.hz) / self.f)

    def x_m(self, col, z):
        return (col - self.W / 2) * z / self.f

    def col_of(self, x, z):
        return self.W / 2 + self.f * x / z


def fresnel(graze):
    """Schlick, graze in radians above the surface."""
    return 0.02 + 0.98 * (1 - np.sin(graze)) ** 5


def fresnel_art(graze):
    """the water study's artistic gain: keeps low-angle water light, as Ferrari does."""
    return np.clip(0.22 + 1.15 * fresnel(graze), 0, 1)
Write: elements/channel/src/s1_still.py
"""Stage 1: a still strip of water, greyscale, seen from 1.6 m looking ~5 deg down.
It mirrors the sky above the frame and what stands on the far bank: a boulder, a willow, a stump.

Pipeline: physical value study (continuous) -> painted index canvas (Ferrari rules).
"""
import sys
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
import channel as ch

W, H = 192, 108
cam = ch.Cam(W, H, f=300, h=1.6, pitch_deg=5.0)
Z_FAR, Z_NEAR = 14.0, 9.0            # water edges (m)
BANK_H, BANK_RUN = 0.20, 1.2         # far bank rises 0.2 m over 1.2 m
NEAR_H, NEAR_RUN = 0.12, 1.0


# ------------------------------------------------------------- world (values 0..255) -------------
def sky_value(elev, col):
    """clear sky, lighter to the horizon, with a cumulus bank at 5-11 deg on the left."""
    e = np.degrees(np.maximum(elev, 0))
    v = 234 - 44 * np.clip(e / 14.0, 0, 1) ** 0.8
    # cumulus: lit tops 246, grey bases 206, azimuth-limited
    x = (col - W * 0.30) / (W * 0.22)
    cloud = np.exp(-x ** 2) * np.exp(-((e - 8.0) / 2.6) ** 2)
    base = np.exp(-x ** 2) * np.exp(-((e - 5.8) / 1.0) ** 2)
    v = v + cloud * 22 - base * 18
    return v


OBJECTS = [  # far-bank objects: x (m), z (m), half-width (m), height (m), kind
    dict(x=-3.4, z=15.0, hw=0.75, ht=0.85, kind='boulder'),
    dict(x=3.2, z=16.0, hw=1.4, ht=2.3, kind='willow'),
    dict(x=0.6, z=14.9, hw=0.12, ht=0.55, kind='stump'),
]


def obj_height(o, xm):
    """silhouette height (m) at lateral position xm, or 0."""
    u = (xm - o['x']) / o['hw']
    if o['kind'] == 'boulder':
        return np.where(np.abs(u) < 1, o['ht'] * np.clip(1 - np.abs(u) ** 2.5, 0, 1) ** 0.5, 0)
    if o['kind'] == 'willow':
        return np.where(np.abs(u) < 1, o['ht'] * (1 - u ** 2) ** 0.6 * (0.85 + 0.15 * np.cos(u * 9)), 0)
    return np.where(np.abs(u) < 1, o['ht'], 0)


def obj_value(o, yrel, from_below=False):
    """value of object at relative height yrel (0 foot .. 1 top). Seen from below -> the underside."""
    if o['kind'] == 'boulder':
        v = 70 + 90 * yrel ** 1.5          # lit top, dark foot
    elif o['kind'] == 'willow':
        v = 55 + 40 * yrel
    else:
        v = 95 + 20 * yrel
    return v - (18 if from_below else 0)


def ground_value(z):
    """dry gravel, lighter and greyer with distance."""
    return 118 + 30 * np.clip((z - 8) / 60, 0, 1)


# ------------------------------------------------------------- the physical value study ----------
def value_study():
    V = np.zeros((H, W))
    what = np.zeros((H, W), 'U8')
    rows = np.arange(H)[:, None] + 0.5
    cols = np.arange(W)[None, :] + 0.5
    elev = cam.elev_of_row(rows) + 0 * cols
    # sky + distant ridge + scrub line
    sky = sky_value(elev, cols)
    ridge = cam.hz - cam.f * np.tan(np.radians(2.2 + 1.3 * np.sin(cols / 23.0) + 0.6 * np.sin(cols / 7.3)))
    scrub_top = cam.row_of(140, 3.0) + 0.8 * np.sin(cols / 3.1)
    V[:] = sky; what[:] = 'sky'
    V[rows > ridge] = 150; what[rows > ridge] = 'ridge'
    V[rows > scrub_top] = 80; what[rows > scrub_top] = 'scrub'
    # ground (far bar), rows below the scrub foot
    zg = cam.z_of_row(rows) + 0 * cols
    grd = (rows > cam.row_of(140)) & (zg > Z_FAR)
    V[grd] = ground_value(zg)[grd]; what[grd] = 'ground'
    # far bank slope: from bank top (z=Z_FAR+RUN, y=BANK_H) down to the waterline
    top_row = cam.row_of(Z_FAR + BANK_RUN, BANK_H)
    wl_row = cam.row_of(Z_FAR)
    slope = (rows >= top_row) & (rows < wl_row) + 0 * cols
    t = (rows - top_row) / (wl_row - top_row) + 0 * cols           # 0 top .. 1 waterline
    V[slope] = (112 - 10 * t - 38 * np.clip((t - 0.72) / 0.28, 0, 1))[slope]   # wet band at the foot
    what[slope] = 'bank'
    # water
    nr = cam.row_of(Z_NEAR)
    water = (rows >= wl_row) & (rows < nr) + 0 * cols
    z = cam.z_of_row(rows) + 0 * cols
    graze = np.arctan(cam.h / z)
    F = ch.fresnel_art(graze)
    refl = sky_value(graze, cols)
    hitwhat = np.full((H, W), 'sky', 'U8')
    # reflected ray height at the far bank top
    ry_bank = (Z_FAR + BANK_RUN - z) * np.tan(graze)
    hb = ry_bank < BANK_H
    refl = np.where(hb, 70, refl); hitwhat[hb] = 'rbank'
    # objects (nearest first overrides)
    for o in sorted(OBJECTS, key=lambda o: -o['z']):
        xm = cam.x_m(cols, o['z']) + 0 * rows
        ht = obj_height(o, xm)
        ry = (o['z'] - z) * np.tan(graze)
        hit = (ry < ht) & (ry >= 0)
        refl = np.where(hit, obj_value(o, np.clip(ry / np.maximum(ht, 1e-6), 0, 1), True), refl)
        hitwhat[hit] = o['kind']
    # body: depth profile across the channel, shallow at both edges
    s = (z - Z_NEAR) / (Z_FAR - Z_NEAR)                 # 0 near edge .. 1 far edge
    depth = 0.45 * np.clip(4 * s * (1 - s), 0, 1) ** 0.7
    body = 92 - 40 * np.clip(depth / 0.45, 0, 1)       # wet bed seen through -> darker with depth
    Vw = F * refl + (1 - F) * body
    V[water] = Vw[water]; what[water] = np.where(hitwhat == 'sky', 'wsky', 'wobj')[water]
    # near bank lip and near gravel
    ng = rows >= nr
    zn = cam.z_of_row(rows) + 0 * cols
    V[ng] = (ground_value(zn) - 6)[ng]; what[ng] = 'near'
    # objects themselves (direct view), standing on the bar behind the waterline
    for o in OBJECTS:
        xm = cam.x_m(cols, o['z']) + 0 * rows
        ht = obj_height(o, xm)
        foot = cam.row_of(o['z'], BANK_H)
        topr = cam.row_of(o['z'], BANK_H + ht)
        m = (rows >= topr) & (rows < foot) & (ht > 0)
        yrel = np.clip((foot - rows) / np.maximum(foot - topr, 1e-6), 0, 1)
        V[m] = obj_value(o, yrel)[m]; what[m] = o['kind']
    return V, what, dict(wl=wl_row, nr=nr, top=top_row, F=F, depth=depth)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    V, what, info = value_study()
    print({k: (round(float(v), 1) if np.ndim(v) == 0 else None) for k, v in info.items()})
    g = np.clip(V, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    pk.save(np.stack([g] * 3, -1), 'study/s1_value_study.png', 4)

Bash: Add ramp quantizer and pebble glyph scatter
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> channel.py <<'EOF'


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- quantizers ------------------
def to_ramp(V, ramp_vals, ramp_idx, band=0.55, matrix=pk.BAYER2, ox=0, oy=0):
    """continuous value V -> indices of a ramp (values ascending or descending), mixing only
    adjacent steps, banded (holds flat, crosses as a sustained checker)."""
    vals = np.asarray(ramp_vals, float)
    order = np.argsort(vals)
    vs = vals[order]; ix = np.asarray(ramp_idx)[order]
    pos = np.interp(V, vs, np.arange(len(vs)))            # fractional step
    q = pk.ordered(pk.banded(pos, band), matrix, ox, oy)
    return ix[np.clip(q, 0, len(vs) - 1)]


def pebbles(h, w, rng, rows_z, f, sizes_m=(0.04, 0.16), density=1.0, mask=None):
    """scatter of foreshortened pebble glyphs. Returns a 'relative step' canvas: 0 = no pebble,
    +2 lit top, +1 body, -1 shadow underside, -2 contact. rows_z: z (m) per row."""
    out = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    items = []
    for r in range(h):
        z = rows_z[r]
        if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0:
            continue
        ppm = f / z
        # count per row ~ area / pebble footprint
        n = int(density * w * 0.18 * rng.random() + density * w * 0.10)
        for _ in range(n):
            s = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(sizes_m[0]), np.log(sizes_m[1])))
            rx = s * ppm / 2
            if rx < 0.7:
                continue
            ry = max(0.5, rx * 0.45)
            items.append((r, rng.uniform(0, w), rx, ry))
    items.sort()
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    for (r, cx, rx, ry) in items:
        y0, y1 = int(r - ry - 1), int(r + ry + 2)
        x0, x1 = int(cx - rx - 1), int(cx + rx + 2)
        y0, x0 = max(y0, 0), max(x0, 0); y1, x1 = min(y1, h), min(x1, w)
        if y1 <= y0 or x1 <= x0:
            continue
        sy, sx = yy[y0:y1, x0:x1], xx[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        d = ((sx + 0.5 - cx) / rx) ** 2 + ((sy + 0.5 - r) / ry) ** 2
        body = d <= 1.0
        if not body.any():
            continue
        rel = np.where(body, 1, 0)
        ys = sy[body]
        top = sy <= ys.min() + (0 if ry < 1.2 else 0)
        rel = np.where(body & top, 2, rel)
        bot = sy >= ys.max()
        rel = np.where(body & bot & (ry >= 0.9), -1, rel)
        # contact shadow: the row right under the pebble
        sh = (~body) & (sy == ys.max() + 1) & (np.abs(sx + 0.5 - cx) < rx * 0.9)
        rel = np.where(sh, -2, rel)
        blk = out[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        blk[rel != 0] = rel[rel != 0]
    if mask is not None:
        out[~mask] = 0
    return out

s=open('s1_still.py').read()
s=s.replace("""if __name__ == '__main__':
    V, what, info = value_study()
    print({k: (round(float(v), 1) if np.ndim(v) == 0 else None) for k, v in info.items()})
    g = np.clip(V, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    pk.save(np.stack([g] * 3, -1), 'study/s1_value_study.png', 4)""",'''PAL = ch.Pal([
    ('sky', ch.grey(240, 230, 220, 210, 200, 190, 180, 170)),
    ('ridge', ch.grey(140, 154)),
    ('scrub', ch.grey(54, 70, 88)),
    ('grav', ch.grey(40, 56, 72, 88, 104, 120, 136, 152, 170, 190)),
    ('wat', ch.grey(84, 102, 120, 140, 160)),
    ('bould', ch.grey(46, 66, 90, 116, 144, 174)),
    ('wil', ch.grey(30, 42, 56, 72, 90)),
])


def paint(seed=1, V=None, what=None, info=None):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if V is None:
        V, what, info = value_study()
    P = PAL
    cv = np.zeros((H, W), int)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    sky_i, sky_v = P.r('sky'), [c[0] for c in P.arr[P.r('sky')]]
    grav_i, grav_v = P.r('grav'), [c[0] for c in P.arr[P.r('grav')]]
    wat_i, wat_v = P.r('wat'), [c[0] for c in P.arr[P.r('wat')]]
    # 1 sky: banded ordered on the sky ramp
    m = what == 'sky'
    cv[m] = ch.to_ramp(V, sky_v, sky_i, 0.5)[m]
    # 2 ridge, scrub
    m = what == 'ridge'
    cv[m] = P.r('ridge')[0]
    # lit ridge crest: the top 1-2 rows of the ridge get the lighter tone
    crest = m & ~np.roll(m, 1, 0)
    cv[crest | (m & np.roll(crest, 1, 0) & par)] = P.r('ridge')[1]
    m = what == 'scrub'
    cv[m] = np.where(rng.random((H, W)) < 0.3, P.r('scrub')[1], P.r('scrub')[0])[m]
    tip = m & ~np.roll(m, 1, 0)
    cv[tip & par] = P.r('scrub')[2]
    # 3 far bar: gravel as ordered texture of a slow field, finer with distance
    zrow = cam.z_of_row(np.arange(H) + 0.5)
    m = (what == 'ground')
    gv = V + (pk.value_noise(H, W, 6, rng, 2, aniso=(0.4, 2.0)) - 0.5) * 22
    gv += (rng.random((H, W)) - 0.5) * np.clip((40 - zrow[:, None]) / 25, 0, 1) * 30
    cv[m] = ch.to_ramp(gv, grav_v, grav_i, 0.8, pk.BAYER4)[m]
    # nearer bar (z < 30): pebble glyphs begin
    peb = ch.pebbles(H, W, rng, np.where((zrow > Z_FAR) & (zrow < 40), zrow, np.nan), cam.f, (0.05, 0.2), 0.9)
    base_step = np.interp(gv, grav_v, np.arange(len(grav_v)))
    ps = np.clip(np.round(base_step + np.where(peb == 2, 2, np.where(peb == 1, 0.6, np.where(peb == -1, -1.2, -2)))), 0, 9).astype(int)
    mm = m & (peb != 0)
    cv[mm] = grav_i[ps][mm]
    # 4 far bank slope: gravel, wet band at the foot
    m = what == 'bank'
    bv = V + (pk.value_noise(H, W, 3, rng, 1) - 0.5) * 18
    cv[m] = ch.to_ramp(bv, grav_v, grav_i, 0.7, pk.BAYER2)[m]
    return cv, V, what, info


if __name__ == '__main__':
    V, what, info = value_study()
    g = np.clip(V, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    pk.save(np.stack([g] * 3, -1), 'study/s1_value_study.png', 4)

    pk.save(PAL.rgb(cv), 'study/s1_paint_%s.png' % (sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'v1'), 4)''')
