# Critique (newest first)

## Round 2 — 2026-09-25 21:34 — stage 0 `img/stage0_copy.png`, `img/stage0_boulder_zoom.png`; stage 1 `img/stage1_lights_sizes.png`, `img/stage1_compare.png`
**Stage 0: REVISE, and close.** The main boulder is now the best thing in the crop. **Stage 1: REVISE.** (My read; your call.)

### Stage 0
Real progress: in `stage0_boulder_zoom.png` the dome's dither now thins toward the lit upper right and thickens into the core toward the lower left. That's the density-carries-the-turn move, and at 10× the dome's interior is now hard to tell from his.

What still gives it away:

1. **The rim is still concentric bands.** In the copy at 10×, lower left, there are two dark-red bands 2 px wide that follow the contour exactly, with a hard boundary where the dome's checker stops. In his, the checker spills *into* the rim zone, and the rim steps from mid to red-brown to near-black in staggered chunks of different lengths, so there's no line where the dome ends and the rim begins. This is the last tell on the boulder itself.
2. **Tablecloth checkers on the top stones.** The top-right stone in `stage0_copy.png` (x≈990–1150, y≈30–200) is big blocks of perfect checker in two colours. His top stones are small chunky faceted chips with flat planes and short dark crack ticks. Checker in his crop lives on curved lit faces; flat stones get flat planes.
3. **The lower-right shadow zone** (10×, x≈700–846, y≈250–405) is a fine uniform checker over the whole area. His is mostly flat dark with checker confined to a few rows near the lit neighbours.
4. **The right-edge stone** (`stage0_copy.png`, x≈1060–1150, y≈270–370) is a smeary orange blob with a curved dark stain. His is a lit stone with crisp planes.

### Stage 1
The staging is good: a flat ground, a cast shadow on a single tone, a 1 px contact line, and a size ladder. The small sizes from 6 px down read as pebbles.

What's wrong:

- **Outline: every stone is a perfect ellipse.** Look at your own photos in `stage1_compare.png`. The Kuban stone has a flattened top plane, one fatter end, and a nearly straight underside; the Hope stone is a rounded triangle. Ferrari's boulder has a faceted contour: short straight runs meeting at soft corners. A perfect ellipse is the iconic "egg" and the first thing a painter's eye rejects.
- **The terminator is a clean S-curve** ("sun behind-left", "sun low left"). It reads as a moon phase. His terminator (V19) is broken by planes, with a step where one facet turns away.
- **The lit face is a perforated grid.** At 40 px the light side is evenly spaced single dots of the lighter tone on a regular lattice, like pegboard. In his boulder the checker is dense in the core and sparse to absent on the brightest plane. Your stage 0 learned this; carry it over.
- **The 10
Bash: New pebble glyphs, reframe, bank occlusion
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('channel.py').read()
a=s.index("def pebbles("); 
s=s[:a]+'''def pebbles(h, w, rng, rows_z, f, sizes_m=(0.04, 0.16), density=1.0, mask=None):
    """scatter of foreshortened stones, as step OFFSETS on a canvas (0 = none).
    small (under ~2 px): a 1-2 px fleck of the stone's own albedo (+1 or -1);
    larger: flat-bottomed lozenge, lit top row +2, body = albedo, underside -1, contact run -2."""
    out = np.zeros((h, w), int)
    items = []
    for r in range(h):
        z = rows_z[r]
        if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0:
            continue
        ppm = f / z
        n = int(density * w * (0.06 + 0.10 * rng.random()))
        for _ in range(n):
            s = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(sizes_m[0]), np.log(sizes_m[1])))
            rx = s * ppm / 2
            if rx < 0.5:
                continue
            items.append((r, rng.uniform(0, w), rx, max(0.5, rx * 0.42), int(rng.choice([-1, 0, 0, 1]))))
    items.sort()
    for (r, cx, rx, ry, alb) in items:
        if rx < 1.0:                                   # fleck
            x0 = int(cx); L = 1 + (rng.random() < rx)
            out[r, x0:min(w, x0 + L)] = 1 if alb >= 0 else -1
            continue
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[int(r - ry - 1):int(r + ry + 2), int(cx - rx - 1):int(cx + rx + 2)]
        ok = (yy >= 0) & (yy < h) & (xx >= 0) & (xx < w)
        # flat-bottomed: rows above the centre are an ellipse, the bottom row is straight
        d = np.abs((xx + 0.5 - cx) / rx) ** 2.2 + np.maximum((yy + 0.5 - r) / ry, -9) ** 2
        body = (d <= 1.0) & ok
        if not body.any():
            continue
        ys = yy[body]
        rel = np.where(body, np.clip(alb, -1, 1) + (1 if alb >= 0 else 0), 0)   # body tone
        rel = np.where(body & (yy == ys.min()), 2, rel)                         # lit top row
        if ys.max() > ys.min():
            rel = np.where(body & (yy == ys.max()), -1, rel)                    # shaded underside
        sh = ok & (~body) & (yy == ys.max() + 1) & (np.abs(xx + 0.5 - cx) < rx * 0.8)
        rel = np.where(sh, -2, rel)
        sel = rel != 0
        out[yy[sel], xx[sel]] = rel[sel]
    if mask is not None:
        out[~mask] = 0
    return out
'''
open('channel.py','w').write(s)
EOF

new='''    # 5 the far bank's wet foot: the rows just above the waterline darken (wet), the last darkest
    wl = int(np.floor(info['wl'])); nr = int(np.floor(info['nr']))
    notobj = ~np.isin(what, ['boulder', 'willow'])
    bankrows = what == 'bank'
    tb = np.zeros((H, W)); 
    for r in range(H):
        if bankrows[r].any():
            tb[r] = (r - info['top']) / (info['wl'] - info['top'])
    foot = bankrows & (tb > 0.62)
    cv[foot] = np.where(par, grav_i[2], grav_i[3])[foot]
    cv[wl - 1, notobj[wl - 1]] = grav_i[1]
    # 6 water -- painted row-class by row-class, top to bottom (Ferrari's horizontal logic)
    water = np.isin(what, ['wsky', 'wobj'])
    hit = info['hit']; hity = info['hity']
    ws_v = sky_v + wat_v; ws_i = np.r_[sky_i, wat_i]
    # A reflected bank face: mirrored bank texture, smeared sideways, compressed a step
    rb = water & (hit == 'rbank')
    rbank_rows = sorted(set(np.nonzero(rb)[0]))
    for k, r in enumerate(rbank_rows):
        src_r = wl - 1 - k                                     # mirror about the waterline
        rowv = cv[src_r].copy()
        # smear: hold each value over 2-4 px runs (reflections streak horizontally)
        x = 0
        while x < W:
            Lr = int(rng.integers(2, 5)); rowv[x:x + Lr] = rowv[x]; x += Lr
        st = np.searchsorted(grav_i, rowv)
        st = np.clip(st - (1 if k == 0 else 0) + (1 if k >= 2 else 0), 0, len(grav_i) - 1)
        cv[r, rb[r]] = grav_i[st][rb[r]]
    # C sheen + D sky reflection: the physical value, lifted just under the reflected bank
    m = water & (hit == 'sky')
    Vs = V.copy()
    below_rb = np.zeros((H, W))
    if rbank_rows:
        last = max(rbank_rows)
        for r in range(last + 1, nr):
            below_rb[r] = r - last
    Vs = Vs + np.where((below_rb >= 1) & (below_rb <= 3), 26 - 6 * below_rb, 0)
    cv[m] = ch.to_ramp(Vs, ws_v, ws_i, 0.5, pk.BAYER2)[m]
    # the boundary between reflected bank and sheen: one checker row
    if rbank_rows:
        r = max(rbank_rows) + 1
        sel = water[r] & (hit[r] == 'sky') & par[r]
        cv[r, sel] = cv[r - 1, sel]
    # reflected objects: their own ramp, lights one step down (range compressed toward the middle)
    for kind, rname in (('boulder', 'bould'), ('willow', 'wil')):
        m = water & (hit == kind)
        if not m.any():
            continue
        ri = P.r(rname); rv = [c[0] for c in P.arr[ri]]
        q = ch.to_ramp(V, rv, ri, 0.5, pk.BAYER2)
        k = np.searchsorted(ri, q)
        k = np.where(k >= len(ri) - 2, k - 1, k)
        k = np.where(k == 0, 1, k)
        cv[m] = ri[k][m]
    # soften reflection silhouettes: a 50% checker one pixel either side of a hit change
    hh = np.where(water, hit, '')
    chg_h = np.zeros((H, W), bool); chg_h[:, 1:] = hh[:, 1:] != hh[:, :-1]
    chg_v = np.zeros((H, W), bool); chg_v[1:] = hh[1:] != hh[:-1]
    objpx = water & np.isin(hit, ['boulder', 'willow'])
    for dx in (-1, 1):
        nb = np.roll(cv, dx, 1); nbh = np.roll(hh, dx, 1)
        sel = water & (nbh != hh) & par & np.isin(nbh, ['boulder', 'willow']) & (hh == 'sky')
        cv[sel] = nb[sel]
    nb = np.roll(cv, 1, 0); nbh = np.roll(hh, 1, 0)
    sel = water & (nbh != hh) & par & np.isin(nbh, ['boulder', 'willow']) & (hh == 'sky')
    cv[sel] = nb[sel]
    # B the far waterline: a broken light line where the meniscus catches the sky
    x = int(rng.integers(0, 4))
    while x < W:
        Lr = int(rng.integers(2, 9))
        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            seg = slice(x, min(W, x + Lr))
            okc = np.isin(hit[wl, seg], ['rbank', 'sky'])
            cv[wl, seg] = np.where(okc, sky_i[2], cv[wl, seg])
        x += Lr + int(rng.integers(1, 6))
    # E near shallows: flat shelf bands of water-only tints, ragged ends, submerged stones
    for k in range(3):
        r = nr - 1 - k
        x = 0
        while x < W:
            Lr = int(rng.integers(4, 22))
            depth_ok = rng.random() < (0.9, 0.7, 0.35)[k]
            seg = slice(x, min(W, x + Lr))
            sky_here = hit[r, seg] == 'sky'
            if depth_ok:
                cv[r, seg] = np.where(sky_here, wat_i[1 + k], cv[r, seg])
            x += Lr
    for i in range(int(W / 9)):
        sx = int(rng.integers(0, W - 5)); sr = nr - 1 - int(rng.integers(0, 2))
        Ls = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
        if not (hit[sr, sx:sx + Ls] == 'sky').all():
            continue
        cv[sr, sx:sx + Ls] = wat_i[0]
        cv[sr - 1, sx + 1:sx + Ls - 1] = wat_i[3]
    # dash rows: dotted horizontal lines (every other pixel), a step lighter on the reflections of
    # dark things, a step darker on the bright sky -- never scattered, always a line
    for r in range(wl + len(rbank_rows) + 3, nr - 3):
        if rng.random() > 0.3:
            continue
        x = int(rng.integers(0, W))
        Ld = int(rng.integers(3, 10))
        for s_ in range(Ld):
            xi = x + 2 * s_
            xi += (xi + r) % 2
            if xi >= W or not water[r, xi]:
                continue
            if hit[r, xi] == 'sky':
                j = np.searchsorted(ws_i, cv[r, xi])
                vals = np.array(ws_v)[np.argsort(ws_v)]
                cv[r, xi] = ws_i[np.argsort(ws_v)][max(0, list(np.array(ws_i)[np.argsort(ws_v)]).index(cv[r, xi]) - 1)] if cv[r, xi] in ws_i else cv[r, xi]
            else:
                cv[r, xi] = sky_i[5]'''

Bash: Add boulder, shrub painters and tone law
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> channel.py <<'EOF'


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- bank objects ----------------
def paint_boulder(cv, m, ramp, rng, light=(-0.6, -0.8)):
    """two value families on the ramp (ordered dither inside each), hard terminator, dark contact row,
    lit rim on the sun side. ramp: indices dark->light (>=6)."""
    from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt as edt
    if not m.any():
        return
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    cy, cx = ys.mean(), xs.mean()
    ry, rx = (ys.max() - ys.min() + 1) / 2, (xs.max() - xs.min() + 1) / 2
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:m.shape[0], 0:m.shape[1]]
    nx = (xx - cx) / rx
    ny = (yy - (ys.max() - 0.2 * ry * 2)) / (ry * 2)        # dome: the normal tips up toward the top
    nz = np.sqrt(np.clip(1 - nx ** 2 - np.clip(ny, -1, 0) ** 2, 0.05, 1))
    lx, ly = light
    u = -(nx * lx + ny * ly) * 0.8 + nz * 0.35
    ins = edt(m)
    lit = u > 0.35
    # lit family steps 3.4..5 ; shadow family 1..2.2 ; gradation by ordered dither
    s = np.where(lit, 3.4 + (u - 0.35) * 3.0, 1.0 + np.clip(u + 0.3, 0, 0.65) * 1.9)
    s = s + (pk.value_noise(*m.shape, 2, rng, 1) - 0.5) * 0.5
    q = np.clip(pk.ordered(s), 0, len(ramp) - 1)
    q = np.where(lit, np.clip(q, 3, len(ramp) - 1), np.clip(q, 0, 2))
    q = np.where(ins <= 1.0, np.where(lit, np.minimum(q + 1, len(ramp) - 1), np.maximum(q - 1, 0)), q)
    bottom = m & ~np.roll(m, -1, 0)
    q = np.where(bottom, 0, q)
    cv[m] = np.asarray(ramp)[q][m]


def paint_shrub(cv, m, ramp, rng, clump=(1.6, 3.2), light=(-0.6, -0.8)):
    """foliage mass as clumps: each a small blob lit upper-left, dark lower-right; holes; darker base."""
    if not m.any():
        return
    h, w = m.shape
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    y0, y1 = ys.min(), ys.max()
    out = np.full((h, w), -1)
    n = int(m.sum() / 5)
    cand = rng.integers(0, len(ys), n)
    order = np.argsort(ys[cand])                    # higher clumps first; lower ones overlap them
    for i in cand[order]:
        cy, cx = ys[i], xs[i]
        r = rng.uniform(*clump)
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[int(cy - r - 1):int(cy + r + 2), int(cx - r - 1):int(cx + r + 2)]
        ok = (yy >= 0) & (yy < h) & (xx >= 0) & (xx < w)
        d = ((yy + 0.5 - cy) ** 2 + (xx + 0.5 - cx) ** 2) / r ** 2
        b = ok & (d <= 1)
        if not b.any():
            continue
        lx, ly = light
        u = -(((xx + 0.5 - cx) / r) * lx + ((yy + 0.5 - cy) / r) * ly)
        depth = (cy - y0) / max(y1 - y0, 1)          # 0 top .. 1 base
        st = 2.0 + u * 1.4 - depth * 1.2 + rng.normal(0, 0.3)
        out[yy[b], xx[b]] = np.clip(np.round(st[b]), 0, len(ramp) - 1).astype(int)
    out = np.where(m & (out < 0), 0, out)
    holes = m & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.05)
    out[holes] = 0
    sel = out >= 0
    cv[sel] = np.asarray(ramp)[out[sel]]
    return sel


def tone_down(idx, ramp, lights=1, dark_up=True):
    """Ferrari's reflection law on one ramp: the top half drops `lights` steps, the darkest comes up."""
    ramp = list(ramp)
    k = np.array([ramp.index(i) if i in ramp else -1 for i in idx.ravel()]).reshape(idx.shape)
    n = len(ramp)
    k2 = np.where(k >= n // 2, k - lights, k)
    if dark_up:
        k2 = np.where(k == 0, 1, k2)
    return np.where(k >= 0, np.asarray(ramp)[np.clip(k2, 0, n - 1)], idx)
EOF

    (40, 34, 7.6, 12.5), (128, 26, 9.0, 13.5), (178, 22, 8.2, 11.0)]


def sky_value(elev, col):
    """clear sky lighter to the horizon; cumulus above the frame: lit tops, grey flat bases."""
    e = np.degrees(np.maximum(elev, 0))
    v = 234 - 44 * np.clip(e / 14.0, 0, 1) ** 0.8
    for (c0, hw, eb, et) in CLOUDS:
        x = (col - c0) / hw
        bulge = np.clip(1 - x ** 2, 0, 1) ** 0.5            # rounded top, flat base
        top = eb + (et - eb) * bulge
        inside = (e >= eb) & (e <= top) & (np.abs(x) < 1)
        t = np.clip((e - eb) / np.maximum(top - eb, 1e-3), 0, 1)
        cval = 206 + 40 * t ** 0.6                          # grey base -> bright top
        v = np.where(inside, cval, v)
    return v

Bash: Checker-material water quantizer; v8
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> channel.py <<'EOF'


def to_ramp_checker(V, ramp_vals, ramp_idx, flat=0.24, ox=0, oy=0):
    """WATER quantizer (Ferrari's mirrored sky): the checker of two adjacent tints is the material.
    Holds a 50% checker across most of each step, passing through a flat tint only within
    +-flat/2 of an integer step."""
    vals = np.asarray(ramp_vals, float)
    order = np.argsort(vals)
    vs = vals[order]; ix = np.asarray(ramp_idx)[order]
    pos = np.interp(V, vs, np.arange(len(vs)))
    f = np.floor(pos); r = pos - f
    h, w = np.shape(V)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    par = ((xx + ox + yy + oy) % 2) == 0
    lo = f.astype(int); hi = lo + 1
    near_lo = r < flat / 2
    near_hi = r > 1 - flat / 2
    q = np.where(near_lo, lo, np.where(near_hi, hi, np.where(par, lo, hi)))
    return ix[np.clip(q, 0, len(vs) - 1)]
EOF

s=s[:a]+'''def sky_value(elev, col):
    """clear sky lighter to the horizon; cumulus above the frame: lumpy lit tops, grey flat bases,
    soft edges (a cloud is a gradient, never a cut-out)."""
    e = np.degrees(np.maximum(elev, 0))
    v = 234 - 44 * np.clip(e / 14.0, 0, 1) ** 0.8
    for k, (c0, hw, eb, et) in enumerate(CLOUDS):
        x = (col - c0) / hw
        bulge = np.clip(1 - x ** 2, 0, 1) ** 0.5 * (0.8 + 0.2 * np.cos(x * 7 + k))
        top = eb + (et - eb) * bulge
        # soft membership: inside the silhouette, fading over ~0.8 deg at the top and sides
        a = np.clip((top - e) / 0.8, 0, 1) * np.clip((e - eb) / 0.3 + 1, 0, 1) * np.clip((1 - np.abs(x)) / 0.15, 0, 1)
        t = np.clip((e - eb) / np.maximum(top - eb, 1e-3), 0, 1)
        cval = 204 + 34 * t ** 0.6
        v = v * (1 - a) + cval * a
    return v

Bash: Copy B: traced masses, jets, rock faces
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='c0b_v11.py'; s=open(p).read()
a=s.index("def paint(seed=1):"); b=s.index("if __name__ == '__main__':")
s=s[:a]+'''def traced_masses():
    """master-copy allowance: his solid dark masses, traced (cores only; edges are procedure)."""
    from scipy.ndimage import uniform_filter, label
    d = pk.lw_load('V11AM')
    idx = d['idx'][Y0:Y0 + H, X0:X0 + W]
    dark = np.isin(idx, list(range(53, 74)) + [82] + list(range(92, 97)))
    core = (uniform_filter(dark.astype(float), 3) > 0.62) & dark
    lab, n = label(core)
    parts = {'mesa': np.zeros_like(core), 'bank_r': np.zeros_like(core), 'bank_n': np.zeros_like(core), 'rocks': []}
    for k in range(1, n + 1):
        m = lab == k
        if m.sum() < 6:
            continue
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
        if ys.min() == 0 and xs.min() == 0 or (xs.mean() < 40 and ys.mean() < 32):
            parts['mesa'] |= m
        elif xs.mean() > 80 and ys.mean() < 40:
            parts['bank_r'] |= m
        elif ys.max() >= H - 2 and xs.mean() > 50:
            parts['bank_n'] |= m
        else:
            parts['rocks'].append(m)
    return parts


def paint_rock(cv, R, rng, par):
    h, w = R.shape
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(R)
    x0, x1 = xs.min(), xs.max()
    split = x0 + 0.55 * (x1 - x0)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    lit = R & (xx < split)
    shd = R & (xx >= split)
    cv[lit] = 69
    strips = lit & (xx > split - 4) & ((xx % 2) == 0)
    cv[strips] = 70
    cv[lit & (xx < x0 + 2) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.5)] = 70
    cv[shd] = np.where(par, 95, 96)[shd]
    # top plane: first 2-3 rows of each column, value-close lighter
    for x in range(x0, x1 + 1):
        col = np.nonzero(R[:, x])[0]
        if len(col) == 0:
            continue
        t = col.min(); n = 2 if (x1 - x0) < 14 else 3
        for k in range(min(n, len(col) - 1)):
            cv[t + k, x] = rng.choice([56, 58, 57, 53] if k == 0 else [56, 58, 60])
    # reflection: checker of the face colour under the rock, fading over 3 rows
    for x in range(x0, x1 + 1):
        col = np.nonzero(R[:, x])[0]
        if len(col) == 0:
            continue
        b = col.max()
        for k in range(1, 4):
            y = b + k
            if y < h and not R[y, x] and par[y, x] and rng.random() < (0.95, 0.7, 0.35)[k - 1]:
                cv[y, x] = 70 if x < split else rng.choice([92, 95, 71])


def paint(seed=1):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    h, w = H, W
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    y = yy + Y0
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    P = traced_masses()
    v = np.interp(y, [338, 344, 350, 355, 358, 364, 369, 374, 380, 386, 393, 399, 405, 410, 416, 424],
                  [8.0, 9.0, 10.0, 10.5, 11.0, 12.0, 12.5, 13.0, 14.0, 14.5, 15.0, 16.0, 16.5, 17.0, 18.0, 18.5])
    cv = pk.ordered(pk.banded(v, 0.6), pk.BAYER4)
    from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt as edt
    M = P['mesa']
    dm = edt(~M, sampling=(3.0, 1.0))
    cv[M] = rng.choice(DARK[:4], M.sum())
    t4 = pk.tile(pk.BAYER2, h, w)
    for lo, hi, thr in ((0, 5, 0.8), (5, 10, 0.5), (10, 16, 0.22)):
        g = (dm > lo) & (dm <= hi)
        dsel = g & (t4 < thr)
        cv[dsel] = rng.choice(DARK[:3], dsel.sum())
        csel = g & ~dsel & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.7)
        cv[csel] = rng.integers(209, 217, csel.sum())
    B = P['bank_r']
    warm = pk.value_noise(h, w, 5, rng, 2) > 0.5
    cv[B] = np.where(par & warm, 82, np.where(warm & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.4), 70, 92))[B]
    be = fringe(B, 3, par) & (yy > 17)
    cv[be & par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.7)] = 82
    rocks_all = np.zeros((h, w), bool)
    for R in P['rocks']:
        rocks_all |= R
    blocked = M | B | rocks_all | P['bank_n']
    # riffle: a few distinct JETS rising from a base band, long leaning chains from their tops,
    # plain mirror between them
    base_y = 44
    jets = [(8, 20), (22, 26), (36, 17), (52, 24), (63, 30), (74, 22), (88, 16), (98, 12)]
    for (jx, jh) in jets:
        jx = jx + rng.normal(0, 1.5)
        jw = rng.uniform(2.5, 5.0)
        top = base_y + 12 - jh
        for yr in range(int(top), base_y + 14):
            f = (yr - top) / max(jh, 1)                        # 0 at the tip .. 1 at the base
            half = jw * (0.35 + 0.65 * f)
            for xr in range(int(jx - half - 1), int(jx + half + 2)):
                if not (0 <= xr < w and 0 <= yr < h) or blocked[yr, xr]:
                    continue
                dx = abs(xr - jx) / max(half, 0.5)
                if dx > 1:
                    continue
                dens = f * (1 - dx * 0.6)
                if dens > 0.55 or (par[yr, xr] and rng.random() < dens * 1.6):
                    cv[yr, xr] = 192 + int(np.clip(rng.normal(5 + 3 * (0.5 - dx), 2), 0, 10))
        # chains from the jet's crown, leaning outward, long
        for c_ in range(int(rng.integers(3, 7))):
            side = rng.choice([-1, 1])
            lean = rng.uniform(0.25, 0.9) * side; curl = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.06) * side
            xf = jx + rng.normal(0, 1); y0 = int(top + rng.integers(0, 5)); L = int(rng.integers(6, 20))
            k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
            for st in range(L):
                yi = y0 - st; xi = int(round(xf))
                if (xi + yi) % 2:
                    xi += 1 if xf > xi else -1
                if 0 <= yi < h and 0 <= xi < w and not blocked[yi, xi] and rng.random() < 0.9:
                    cv[yi, xi] = 192 + (k0 + st) % 11
                xf += lean + curl * st
    # the base band between the rocks: dense strands, then dark trough runs under it
    band = (yy >= base_y + 6) & (yy <= base_y + 14) & ~blocked
    bn = pk.value_noise(h, w, 3, rng, 2, aniso=(3.0, 0.6))
    selb = band & ((bn > 0.45) | (par & (bn > 0.3)))
    cv[selb] = 192 + np.clip((bn * 11 + rng.normal(0, 1.5, (h, w))).astype(int), 0, 10)[selb]
    lil = selb & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.05)
    cv[lil] = rng.choice([219, 220, 221], lil.sum())
    for yb in range(base_y + 15, base_y + 19):
        x = 0
        while x < w:
            if rng.random() < 0.18:
                L = int(rng.integers(3, 10))
                for xi in range(x, min(w, x + L)):
                    if not blocked[yb, xi]:
                        cv[yb, xi] = rng.choice([209, 210, 215, 216, 223])
                x += L + int(rng.integers(3, 9))
            else:
                x += 1
    # near dotted dashes
    for yd in range(62, h):
        pr = np.interp(yd, [62, 66, 70, 78, 90], [0.0, 0.5, 0.3, 0.45, 0.35])
        x = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
        while x < w:
            if rng.random() < pr * 0.3:
                L = int(rng.integers(3, 12)); k0 = int(rng.integers(0, 11))
                ramp = (192, 11) if yd < 79 else (209, 8)
                for s_ in range(L):
                    xi = x + 2 * s_; xi += (xi + yd) % 2
                    if xi < w and not blocked[yd, xi]:
                        cv[yd, xi] = ramp[0] + (k0 - s_) % ramp[1]
                x += 2 * L + int(rng.integers(2, 10))
            else:
                x += int(rng.integers(2, 8))
    for R in P['rocks']:
        paint_rock(cv, R, rng, par)
    N = P['bank_n']
    cv[N] = np.where(par & (pk.value_noise(h, w, 4, rng, 2) > 0.62), 82, rng.choice([92, 93, 94, 95], (h, w)))[N]
    ne = fringe(N, 2, par)
    cv[ne & par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.7)] = rng.choice([92, 82], (ne & par & (rng.random((h, w)) < 2)).sum()) if False else 92
    return cv


'''+s[b:]

Bash: Add low-angle gravel painter (paired marks)
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> channel.py <<'EOF'


def gravel(mask, base_v, zrow, f, rng, ramp_i, ramp_v, stone_m=(0.03, 0.14), density=1.0, matrix=None):
    """gravel at a low angle, as marks on an ordered base.
    base: the value field through a 4x4 ordered dither (the smooth mass).
    marks: every stone is a light-over-dark PAIR (lit top row, shaded row under it, contact under that),
    its width from the stone size projected at that row's distance; far stones shrink to 1-px flecks of
    light or dark; stones never sit in rows (random y), and they overlap nearer-over-farther."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    matrix = pk.BAYER4 if matrix is None else matrix
    vals = np.asarray(ramp_v, float); n = len(vals)
    step = np.interp(base_v, vals, np.arange(n))
    out_step = pk.ordered(step, matrix).astype(float)
    # candidate stones: area density per pixel ~ 1/(stone footprint in px)
    marks = []
    for y in range(h):
        z = zrow[y]
        if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0:
            continue
        ppm = f / z
        k = int(w * density * (0.10 + 0.35 * np.clip(ppm / 60.0, 0, 1)))
        for _ in range(k):
            s = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(stone_m[0]), np.log(stone_m[1])))
            wpx = s * ppm
            marks.append((y + rng.random(), rng.uniform(-2, w), wpx, rng.choice([-1, 0, 0, 1])))
    marks.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
    for (yf, x0, wpx, alb) in marks:
        y = int(yf)
        if wpx < 0.8:
            # a fleck: light or dark single pixel (half the time nothing: the base carries it)
            if rng.random() < 0.5:
                continue
            xi = int(x0)
            if 0 <= xi < w and mask[y, xi]:
                out_step[y, xi] = step[y, xi] + (1.2 if alb >= 0 else -1.2)
            continue
        L = max(1, int(round(wpx)))
        tall = wpx > 2.6                      # big stones: a body row between top and underside
        xs = np.arange(int(x0), int(x0) + L)
        xs = xs[(xs >= 0) & (xs < w)]
        if len(xs) == 0:
            continue
        top_off = 1.6 + 0.4 * alb
        rows = [(y, top_off)]
        if tall:
            rows.append((y + 1, 0.6 + 0.4 * alb))
            rows.append((y + 2, -1.0))
            rows.append((y + 3, -2.0))
        else:
            rows.append((y + 1, -1.0 + 0.3 * alb))
            if wpx > 1.6:
                rows.append((y + 2, -1.8))
        for (yy, off) in rows:
            if yy >= h:
                continue
            xx = xs
            if off < -1.5:                                    # contact shadow is shorter than the stone
                xx = xs[1:-1] if len(xs) > 2 else xs[:1]
            if off > 1.0 and len(xs) > 2:                     # the lit top is shorter too (rounded)
                xx = xs[1:] if rng.random() < 0.5 else xs[:-1]
            sel = mask[yy, xx]
            out_step[yy, xx[sel]] = step[yy, xx[sel]] + off
    q = np.clip(np.round(out_step), 0, n - 1).astype(int)
    return np.asarray(ramp_i)[q]

        else:                      # foliage keeps its texture; only the range compresses
            cv[rr, cc] = ch.tone_down(vals, P.r(rname))
        # wind lines cross dark reflections as dotted light dashes
        rows_k = sorted(set(rr))
        for r in rows_k[2::5]:
            xs_ = cc[rr == r]
            if len(xs_) < 4 or rng.random() < 0.3:
                continue
            x0 = int(rng.integers(xs_.min(), max(xs_.min() + 1, xs_.max() - 3)))
            for s_ in range(int(rng.integers(2, 7))):
                xi = x0 + 2 * s_ + ((x0 + r) % 2)
                if xi <= xs_.max() and hit[r, xi] == kind:
                    cv[r, xi] = P.r(rname)[-2]""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
cd ..; nice -n 10 python3 src/s1_still.py v15 2>&1 | grep -v -i warn | grep -v "return np"; python3 -c "

Bash: Shallows as reusable function; rerender
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; cat >> channel.py <<'EOF'


def shallows(cv, water, edge_row, rng, tint, mirror_ok, rows=6, stones_per_px=0.10, lap=None):
    """the near-edge shelf where you see the bed (Ferrari's shallow shelf, C4).
    edge_row[c]: first land row under the water in column c. tint: water-only ramp dark->light (>=4).
    Graded upward: full tint at the edge, then checker with the mirror, then the mirror.
    The shelf's upper boundary steps in runs; stones are flat lozenges: dark body, sky-lit top row;
    stones that break the surface get a light lap line under them. mirror_ok: where the mirror is sky
    (reflections of objects are left alone)."""
    h, w = water.shape
    out = cv.copy()
    top = np.zeros(w, int); x = 0
    while x < w:
        L = int(rng.integers(3, 16)); top[x:x + L] = int(rng.integers(rows - 2, rows + 1)); x += L
    for c in range(w):
        e = edge_row[c]
        for k in range(1, top[c] + 1):
            r = e - k
            if r < 0 or not water[r, c] or not mirror_ok[r, c]:
                continue
            f = k / top[c]                                   # 0 edge .. 1 shelf top
            if f <= 0.34:
                out[r, c] = tint[0] if k == 1 else tint[1]
            elif f <= 0.67:
                out[r, c] = tint[1] if (r + c) % 2 else tint[2]
            elif f < 1.0:
                out[r, c] = tint[2] if (r + c) % 2 == 0 else out[r, c]
            else:                                            # the shelf lip catches the sky
                if rng.random() < 0.7:
                    out[r, c] = tint[3]
    n = int(w * stones_per_px)
    for i in range(n):
        c0 = int(rng.integers(0, w - 3)); L = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
        e = edge_row[c0]; k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, top[c0])))
        r = e - k
        cs = np.arange(c0, min(w, c0 + L))
        if r - 1 < 0 or not (water[r, cs].all() and mirror_ok[r, cs].all()):
            continue
        out[r, cs] = tint[0]
        if L >= 3:
            out[r - 1, cs[1:-1]] = tint[3]                   # sky-lit wet top
        if k <= 1 and lap is not None and r + 1 < h:
            out[r + 1, cs] = lap                             # emergent: lap line under it
    return out
EOF

Bash: Shallows as flat stepped shelves
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='channel.py'; s=open(p).read()
a=s.index("def shallows("); 
s=s[:a]+'''def shallows(cv, water, edge_row, rng, tint, mirror_ok, rows=6, stones_per_px=0.10, lap=None):
    """the near-edge shelf where you see the bed (Ferrari's shallow shelf, C4): FLAT tints with
    stepped boundaries (runs of 3-16 px), never a one-row checker. Two shelves: the outer (lighter,
    deeper) and the inner (darker, shallowest). A broken light line on the outer shelf's lip.
    Stones: flat lozenges, dark body, sky-lit top row; stones at the very edge break the surface and
    get the lap line under them."""
    h, w = water.shape
    out = cv.copy()

    def steps(lo, hi):
        t = np.zeros(w, int); x = 0
        while x < w:
            L = int(rng.integers(3, 17)); t[x:x + L] = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1)); x += L
        return t
    outer = steps(rows - 2, rows)
    inner = np.minimum(steps(1, max(2, rows // 2)), outer - 1)
    for c in range(w):
        e = edge_row[c]
        for k in range(1, outer[c] + 1):
            r = e - k
            if r < 0 or not water[r, c] or not mirror_ok[r, c]:
                continue
            if k <= inner[c]:
                out[r, c] = tint[0] if k == 1 else tint[1]
            else:
                out[r, c] = tint[2]
        r = e - outer[c] - 1
        if r >= 0 and water[r, c] and mirror_ok[r, c] and rng.random() < 0.55:
            out[r, c] = tint[3]
    n = int(w * stones_per_px)
    for i in range(n):
        c0 = int(rng.integers(0, w - 3)); L = int(rng.integers(3, 9))
        e = edge_row[c0]; k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, outer[c0])))
        r = e - k
        cs = np.arange(c0, min(w, c0 + L))
        if r - 1 < 0 or not (water[r, cs].all() and mirror_ok[r, cs].all()):
            continue
        out[r, cs] = tint[0]
        out[r - 1, cs[1:-1]] = tint[3] if k > 1 else tint[4]            # sky-lit wet top
        if k <= 1 and lap is not None and r + 1 < h:
            out[r + 1, cs[:-1]] = lap                                  # emergent: lap line under it
    return out
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)

s=s.replace("""**Honest comparison.** At 3× the masses, values and the far band match. The far threads at 8× are nearly indistinguishable. The critic's round 1 caught two tells, both fixed in v10: a ruled 45° lower edge on the glitter band, and a near ribbon of constant width like a road. His banks bite into the water in stepped notches; I now add blocky noise, held flat over 2–4 px runs, to the ribbon field. His glitter also thins out through dither with specks spilling into the dark.
**Still not his:** the ribbon *shapes* come from my rough sketch, so the arm at the turn is too wide. The dissolve along the near ribbon's lower edge is a little mottled where his is crisper. At 8× a trained eye would still pick mine, mostly on shape, not on procedure.""","""**Honest comparison.** At 3× the masses, values and the far band match. The far threads at 8× are nearly indistinguishable. The critic's round 1 caught two tells: a ruled 45° lower edge on the glitter band, and a near ribbon of constant width like a road. Round 2 caught a new costume for the same tell, a 45° checker "zipper" where my glitter dissolved. It also pointed out that a master copy may trace his shapes, so that the test is the procedure. v12 therefore takes his water *shape* from the index map (`TRACED = True`) and paints everything else by rule, blurLab 1.84 (it was 3.7). The glitter margin is now short, with sparse specks and no dissolve band. The free-shape version (`TRACED = False`, stepped "breathing" banks from blocky noise) is kept for my own painter.
**Still not his:** his glitter clumps a little more (runs of the brightest peach), and my marsh clumps are slightly too light on the far left. With his shapes, what's left is texture statistics, not structure.""")
s=s.replace("""**Honest comparison.** At 3× it reads as the same kind of passage. At 8× my riffle is busier, with too many lilac pixels, and his chains are longer and fewer. My rocks lack his lit top planes. My sketch is also rougher here than in copy A. **Partly matched.** I stopped at the point where the *procedure* was captured, because the braided channel only needs the riffle grammar. The remaining gaps are named above.""","""**Honest comparison.** v7 traces his solid masses (rock and bank cores) from the index map; their edges and everything in the water are procedure. After the critic's round 2, the riffle is now a few distinct **jets**, with plain mirror between them and leaning chains out of their crowns (it was a uniform fizz). Rocks now have a lighter top plane, a lit face with maroon strips, a darkest checker shadow face, and a checker reflection of the face colours under them. **Partly matched.** At 8× his jets are more irregular. My base band is a flat horizontal slab where his follows the rocks diagonally. My maroon strips are too regular. I stopped once the *procedure* was captured, because the braided channel needs the riffle grammar, not this riffle.""")
s += '''
---

## Stage 1: a still strip of water, greyscale, at a low angle

`src/s1_still.py` (the scene), `src/channel.py` (the technique as it grows), `src/s1_sheet.py` (these images).

![stage 1](out/s1_still.png)
![photo | mine | Ferrari](out/s1_triptych.png)
8× crops: `out/s1_still_8x_left.png` (boulder reflection, far and near edges), `out/s1_still_8x_right.png` (willow and its reflection).

**Set-up.** Eye at 1.6 m, looking 7° down, f = 300 px on a 192×108 canvas. A strip of water from 7 m to 11 m, so its grazing angles are 8–13°. The far bank is a gravel bar 0.2 m high, with a boulder and a willow standing on it. Clouds float *above the frame*.

**First I computed a physical value study** (`study/s1_value_study.png`). Each water pixel shoots its reflected ray. That ray hits the far bank (which occludes what stands behind it), an object, or the sky at the reflected elevation. The water's value is F·(what it hits) + (1−F)·(the bed seen through it), with F from Schlick plus the water study's artistic gain. Then I *painted* from it by Ferrari's rules, which is where the real work was.

**What the geometry taught me:**

- At 5–10° down the strip mirrors sky 8–13° up, which is *above the frame*. So the water shows clouds you can't see. A flat mirror under an empty upper sky is dull, so I put cumulus up there, and their bright tops hang down in the water as soft inverted domes.
- A **sloping** bank reflects only 1–3 rows, because the mirror sees it edge-on. Only a *cut* bank reflects as tall as it stands. Objects set back from the bank lose the bottom of their reflection behind the bank's own reflection (the boulder's reflection starts part-way up).
- The brightest water is directly under the reflected bank, where the ray just clears the bank top and meets the lowest visible sky at the highest Fresnel. That is Ferrari's **sheen band**, and it falls out of the physics. I lift it a little so it reads.

**Values (L\\*, measured on my canvas):** horizon sky about 93; sheen 77; open water 67 → 62 toward the viewer; the shallow shelf 54 → 36; wet margin 28–36; dry gravel about 47. The photos agree on the order: overcast NZ ribbons 95–99 against gravel 30, and Denali water 78 against sky 99 and gravel 45–60.

- **Reflection against sky:** the open water is about 1½–3 sky steps below the horizon sky in frame, because it mirrors *higher* sky and F is below 1. In Ferrari the mirror reuses the sky's own indices and carries the same value as the sky it mirrors, darkening only because nearer water mirrors higher sky. I follow that: the water quantizes onto the sky ramp plus water-only tints.
- The first version had water (62) equal to the gravel (62). The notan failed, and the strip didn't read as water. I darkened the gravel and lightened the bed seen through the water: sky and water light, land mid, wet margins dark.

**What painting it taught me (each of these was a failure first):**

1. **The water's material is the checker, not the band.** My first water was flat bands with seams, like his *sky*. His water at 8× is a 50% checker of two neighbouring tints almost everywhere; flat tints appear only as he passes through a step. `channel.to_ramp_checker`. Sky and water differ as materials by exactly this.
2. **The mirror never reaches the sky's top step** (it's capped one step below), so the checker never saturates into a flat white patch.
3. **Reflections copy the painting, then apply the tone law.** A reflection re-quantized from physical values came out as a flat blob. Copying the direct pixel at the reflected hit point keeps the texture. A convex solid (boulder) drops two steps in its lights, because the mirror sees its shaded underside. Foliage drops one step, and its darkest comes up one: range compressed, texture kept. Wind lines cross dark reflections as dotted light dashes.
4. **Any single row of 50% checker reads as a dotted line.** It happened four times: the waterline "Morse code", a checker row between reflection and sheen, the shelf gradient, and the wet foot. Every one of those boundaries is now either a flat tint with a **stepped** boundary (runs of 3–16 px), or a value change that the gravel texture carries (the wet foot and wet margin are the gravel painter run on darkened values).
5. **The shallows are flat shelves** (two tints with stepped edges, and a broken light line on the outer lip). Stones are flat lozenges with a sky-lit top; stones at the very edge break the surface and sit on a light lap line. `channel.shallows`.
6. **Gravel at a low angle is light-over-dark pairs:** a lit top row, a shaded row, and a contact row under the bigger stones, on a 4×4 ordered base. Widths come from the stone size projected at that row, and far stones shrink to 1-px flecks. Glyphs placed row by row made bricks; random y and sizes fixed that. `channel.gravel`.

**Honest comparison (the triptych).** The strip reads as water at a glance, with the right notan. The far edge (wet foot, dark reflected bank, sheen) and the near edge (shelves, lap line, wet margin) now read in the right order. **Not Ferrari yet:**

- His water is calmer and more luminous. Mine still has too many horizontal events in 25 rows.
- My boulder is an ordered-dither dome with a straight terminator, far below his rock (the cobbles student owns that problem). My willow is a clump mass without his foliage drawing.
- My shallow stones are too faint at this value spacing. His are distinct flat lozenges with a clear light line.
- The gravel reads as gravel but is noisier than his ordered textures would be.
'''
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
EOF

def boulder_mask(h, w, cx, base_y, width, height, rng, nv=7):
    """a faceted boulder silhouette: convex polygon, flat bottom on base_y, top a broken plane.
    Returns (mask, facets) where facets labels 0 top plane, 1 sun face, 2 shadow face."""
    from matplotlib.path import Path
    hw = width / 2
    # vertices: flat bottom corners, shoulders, a tilted top plane with a corner
    top_y = base_y - height
    ridge_x = cx + rng.uniform(-0.15, 0.25) * width          # where the sun face meets the shadow face
    pts = [(cx - hw, base_y), (cx - hw * rng.uniform(0.95, 1.05), base_y - height * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.5)),
           (cx - hw * rng.uniform(0.55, 0.75), top_y + height * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.18)),
           (cx - hw * rng.uniform(0.0, 0.25), top_y),
           (cx + hw * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.6), top_y + height * rng.uniform(0.0, 0.12)),
           (cx + hw * rng.uniform(0.85, 1.0), base_y - height * rng.uniform(0.45, 0.65)),
           (cx + hw, base_y)]
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    m = Path(pts).contains_points(np.c_[xx.ravel() + 0.5, yy.ravel() + 0.5]).reshape(h, w)
    # facets: the top plane is the band within ~22% of the height under the top edge
    lab = np.full((h, w), -1)
    topedge = np.full(w, 10 ** 6)
    for c in range(w):
        col = np.nonzero(m[:, c])[0]
        if len(col):
            topedge[c] = col.min()
    tp = m & (yy - topedge[None, :] < max(1.5, height * 0.24))
    lab[m] = np.where(xx[m] < ridge_x + (yy[m] - top_y) * 0.25, 1, 2)
    lab[tp] = 0
    return m, lab


def paint_faceted(cv, m, lab, ramp, rng, top=(4.2, 5.0), sun=(3.0, 4.2), shade=(1.0, 1.8)):
    """planes, not a dome: each facet holds its own value family; within a facet the ordered dither
    DENSITY carries the turn (sparse in the middle of the plane, thicker toward its far edge), a
    lighter echo under the top edge, a dark core where the sun face turns to the shadow face,
    contact row at the bottom, and a couple of short crack ticks in the light."""
    from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt as edt
    h, w = m.shape
    n = len(ramp)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
    s = np.zeros((h, w))
    for k, (lo, hi) in enumerate((top, sun, shade)):
        f = lab == k
        if not f.any():
            continue
        d = edt(f)
        dn = np.clip(d / max(d.max(), 1), 0, 1)             # 0 at the facet edge .. 1 in the middle
        s[f] = (lo + (hi - lo) * dn ** 0.7)[f]
    s += (pk.value_noise(h, w, 2.0, rng, 1) - 0.5) * 0.35
    q = np.clip(pk.ordered(s), 0, n - 1)
    # the core: the shadow-face pixels touching the sun face go one darker
    core = (lab == 2) & (np.roll(lab, 1, 1) == 1)
    core |= (lab == 2) & (np.roll(lab, 2, 1) == 1) & (rng.random((h, w)) < 0.5)
    q[core] = np.maximum(q[core] - 1, 0)
    bottom = m & ~np.roll(m, -1, 0)
    q[bottom] = 0
    # crack ticks: 2-3 px diagonal darks inside the lit faces
    lit = np.argwhere(np.isin(lab, [0, 1]) & m)
    for i in range(max(1, len(lit) // 90)):
        y, x = lit[int(rng.integers(0, len(lit)))]
        for t in range(int(rng.integers(2, 4))):
            yi, xi = y + t, x + t * (1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1) // 1
            if 0 <= yi < h and 0 <= xi < w and m[yi, xi] and lab[yi, xi] != 2:
                q[yi, xi] = max(q[yi, xi] - 2, 1)
    cv[m] = np.asarray(ramp)[q][m]
EOF

Write: elements/channel/src/world.py
"""world.py -- the braided plain modelled only as far as the painting needs, and a raycaster that
turns it into a per-pixel G-buffer for the painter (painter.py).

Ground: y = height(x, z) in metres; water surface at y = 0 wherever the ground is below 0.
Camera: eye at height h, pitch small, pinhole f (px). Columns are vertical planes through the eye,
so a reflected ray stays in its column: the reflection of anything is found by marching the same
column upward from the water point.
"""
import numpy as np


class Cam:
    def __init__(self, W, H, f, h, pitch_deg):
        self.W, self.H, self.f, self.h = W, H, f, h
        self.pitch = pitch_deg
        self.hz = H / 2 - f * np.tan(np.radians(pitch_deg))

    def row_of(self, z, y=0.0):
        return self.hz + self.f * (self.h - y) / z

    def mirror_row(self, z, y):
        return self.hz + self.f * (self.h + y) / z

    def z_of_row(self, r):
        return self.f * self.h / np.maximum(r - self.hz, 1e-3)

    def x_of(self, c, z):
        return (c - self.W / 2) * z / self.f

    def col_of(self, x, z):
        return self.W / 2 + self.f * x / z


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- terrains --------
class StraightChannel:
    """a channel along x between z_near(x) and z_far(x); banks of a chosen form.
    far/near bank forms: ('slope', height, run) | ('cut', height) ; bed depth profile parabolic.
    Edge positions and bank heights 'breathe' along x (stepped noise) so no edge is a ruled line."""

    def __init__(self, z_near, z_far, depth=0.4, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0),
                 breathe=0.25, bar_rise=0.0, seed=0):
        self.z_near, self.z_far, self.depth = z_near, z_far, depth
        self.far, self.near = far, near
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        # stepped wander of both edges along x (metres): piecewise constant over 0.3-1.2 m runs,
        # smoothed a little so a run's ends slope for a pixel or two
        xs = np.arange(-60, 60, 0.05)
        def stepped(amp):
            v = np.zeros_like(xs); i = 0
            while i < len(xs):
                L = int(rng.integers(6, 24)); v[i:i + L] = rng.normal(0, amp); i += L
            k = np.ones(3) / 3
            return np.convolve(v, k, 'same')
        self.xs = xs
        self.dfar = stepped(breathe)
        self.dnear = stepped(breathe * 0.7)
        self.hfar = 1 + stepped(0.25)
        self.bar_rise = bar_rise

    def _interp(self, arr, x):
        return np.interp(x, self.xs, arr)

    def height(self, x, z):
        zf = self.z_far + self._interp(self.dfar, x)
        zn = self.z_near + self._interp(self.dnear, x)
        y = np.zeros(np.broadcast(x, z).shape)
        inside = (z > zn) & (z < zf)
        s = np.clip((z - zn) / np.maximum(zf - zn, 1e-3), 0, 1)
        bed = -self.depth * np.clip(4 * s * (1 - s), 0, 1) ** 0.7 - 0.01
        y = np.where(inside, bed, y)
        # far bank
        kind = self.far[0]
        hb = self.far[1] * self._interp(self.hfar, x)
        d = z - zf
        if kind == 'slope':
            run = self.far[2]
            yf = np.clip(d / run, 0, 1) * hb
        else:  # cut: a near-vertical face of 0.08 m run
            yf = np.clip(d / 0.08, 0, 1) * hb
        yf = yf + np.maximum(d - 1.0, 0) * self.bar_rise
        y = np.where(z >= zf, yf, y)
        # near bank (rises toward the viewer)
        kind = self.near[0]
        hn = self.near[1]
        d = zn - z
        if kind == 'slope':
            yn = np.clip(d / self.near[2], 0, 1) * hn
        else:
            yn = np.clip(d / 0.08, 0, 1) * hn
        y = np.where(z <= zn, yn, y)
        return y

    def material(self, x, z, y):
        """0 gravel, 1 grass (set by subclasses / params)."""
        return np.zeros(np.shape(y), int)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- raycaster -------
def raycast(cam, terrain, z_min=2.0, z_max=400.0, nz=900):
    """per pixel: kind (0 sky, 1 ground, 2 water), z, x, y, facing (slope of the hit surface toward
    the camera, dy/dz), depth (water), and for water: refl (0 sky, 1 ground), refl_row, refl_z,
    refl_y, graze (rad)."""
    W, H = cam.W, cam.H
    zs = np.geomspace(z_min, z_max, nz)
    rows = np.arange(H) + 0.5
    s = (rows - cam.hz) / cam.f                                  # ray drop per metre (slope)
    G = {k: np.zeros((H, W)) for k in ('z', 'x', 'y', 'facing', 'depth', 'refl_row', 'refl_z', 'refl_y', 'graze')}
    G['kind'] = np.zeros((H, W), int)
    G['refl'] = np.zeros((H, W), int)
    for c in range(W):
        xz = cam.x_of(c + 0.5, zs)
        yt = terrain.height(xz, zs)                              # ground height along this column
        ys = np.maximum(yt, 0.0)                                 # the surface seen from above
        ray = cam.h - s[:, None] * zs[None, :]                   # H x nz ray heights
        below = ray <= ys[None, :]
        hitany = below.any(1) & (s > 0)
        k = np.argmax(below, 1)
        # refine: linear interpolation between k-1 and k
        k0 = np.maximum(k - 1, 0)
        d0 = ray[np.arange(H), k0] - ys[k0]; d1 = ray[np.arange(H), k] - ys[k]
        t = np.clip(d0 / np.maximum(d0 - d1, 1e-9), 0, 1)
        zh = zs[k0] + t * (zs[k] - zs[k0])
        yh = np.interp(zh, zs, ys)
        wet = np.interp(zh, zs, yt) < 0
        fac = np.gradient(ys, zs)[k]
        G['z'][:, c] = np.where(hitany, zh, np.inf)
        G['x'][:, c] = cam.x_of(c + 0.5, G['z'][:, c])
        G['y'][:, c] = yh
        G['facing'][:, c] = fac
        G['kind'][:, c] = np.where(hitany, np.where(wet, 2, 1), 0)
        G['depth'][:, c] = np.where(wet, -np.interp(zh, zs, yt), 0)
        # reflections for water pixels
        wrows = np.nonzero(hitany & wet)[0]
        for r in wrows:
            z0 = zh[r]
            g = s[r]                                             # tan of the grazing angle
            G['graze'][r, c] = np.arctan(g)
            mask = zs > z0
            rr = (zs[mask] - z0) * g
            hit = rr <= ys[mask]
            hit &= ys[mask] > 0.0
            if hit.any():
                j = np.argmax(hit)
                zr = zs[mask][j]; yr = ys[mask][j]
                G['refl'][r, c] = 1
                G['refl_z'][r, c] = zr; G['refl_y'][r, c] = yr
                G['refl_row'][r, c] = cam.row_of(zr, yr)
            else:
                G['refl'][r, c] = 0
    return G
Write: elements/channel/src/painter.py
"""painter.py -- Ferrari's rules applied to the G-buffer from world.raycast.

Order matters: sky, ground (gravel/grass) are painted first; the water then COPIES the painted
ground for its reflections (tone law), mirrors the sky on the sky ramp as a checker material,
and adds its own marks (sheen, shelves, stones, wind lines, lap line).
"""
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
import channel as ch


def grey_palette():
    return ch.Pal([
        ('sky', ch.grey(240, 230, 220, 210, 200, 190, 180, 170)),
        ('grav', ch.grey(40, 56, 72, 88, 104, 120, 136, 152, 170, 190)),
        ('wat', ch.grey(84, 102, 120, 140, 160)),
        ('grass', ch.grey(28, 44, 62, 82, 104, 128, 152)),
        ('earth', ch.grey(26, 38, 52, 68)),
        ('bould', ch.grey(46, 66, 90, 116, 144, 174)),
        ('wil', ch.grey(34, 50, 68, 88, 110)),
    ])


def vals(P, name):
    return [c[0] for c in P.arr[P.r(name)]]


def sky_value(elev_deg, col, W, clouds=()):
    e = np.maximum(elev_deg, 0)
    v = 234 - 44 * np.clip(e / 14.0, 0, 1) ** 0.8
    for k, (c0, hw, eb, et) in enumerate(clouds):
        x = (col - c0 * W) / (hw * W)
        bulge = np.clip(1 - x ** 2, 0, 1) ** 0.5 * (0.8 + 0.2 * np.cos(x * 7 + k))
        top = eb + (et - eb) * bulge
        a = np.clip((top - e) / 0.8, 0, 1) * np.clip((e - eb) / 0.3 + 1, 0, 1) * np.clip((1 - np.abs(x)) / 0.15, 0, 1)
        t = np.clip((e - eb) / np.maximum(top - eb, 1e-3), 0, 1)
        v = v * (1 - a) + (204 + 34 * t ** 0.6) * a
    return v


DEFAULT = dict(
    clouds=((0.2, 0.18, 7.6, 12.5), (0.66, 0.14, 9.0, 13.5), (0.93, 0.12, 8.2, 11.0)),
    ground_top=100.0, ground_far=128.0, haze_z=70.0,
    wet_h=0.07, wet_drop=48.0, face_dark=0.35,
    stones=(0.03, 0.16),
    body=104.0, body_deep=64.0, depth_scale=0.45,
    shelf=(0.035, 0.09), sheen=26.0, nearness=14.0, streaks=16.0, wind=0.3,
    lap=0.45, grass=None,
)


def paint(G, cam, P, rng, prm=None):
    prm = dict(DEFAULT, **(prm or {}))
    H, W = G['kind'].shape
    cv = np.zeros((H, W), int)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    par = (xx + yy) % 2 == 0
    sky_i, sky_v = P.r('sky'), vals(P, 'sky')
    grav_i, grav_v = P.r('grav'), vals(P, 'grav')
    wat_i, wat_v = P.r('wat'), vals(P, 'wat')
    kind = G['kind']
    elev = np.degrees(np.arctan((cam.hz - (yy + 0.5)) / cam.f))
    # ---- sky
    m = kind == 0
    sv = sky_value(elev, xx + 0.5, W, prm['clouds'])
    cv[m] = ch.to_ramp(sv, sky_v, sky_i, 0.5)[m]
    # ---- ground: value from distance haze, facing (faces toward us get less sky), wetness by height
    gm = kind == 1
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(G['z']), G['z'], 1e3)
    base = prm['ground_top'] + (prm['ground_far'] - prm['ground_top']) * np.clip((z - 6) / prm['haze_z'], 0, 1)
    fac = np.clip(G['facing'], 0, 4)
    base = base * (1 - prm['face_dark'] * np.clip(fac / 1.5, 0, 1))
    wetness = np.clip(1 - G['y'] / prm['wet_h'], 0, 1) ** 1.5
    base = base - prm['wet_drop'] * wetness * gm
    base = base + (pk.value_noise(H, W, 6, rng, 2, aniso=(0.4, 2.0)) - 0.5) * 18
    zrow = np.array([np.median(z[r][gm[r]]) if gm[r].any() else np.nan for r in range(H)])
    g = ch.gravel(gm, base, zrow, cam.f, rng, grav_i, grav_v, prm['stones'], 1.0)
    cv[gm] = g[gm]
    grass_layer = None
    if prm['grass'] is not None:
        grass_layer = paint_grass(cv, G, cam, P, rng, prm['grass'])
    # ---- water
    wm = kind == 2
    gr = G['graze']
    F = ch.fresnel_art(gr)
    skyrefl = sky_value(np.degrees(gr), xx + 0.5, W, prm['clouds'])
    d = G['depth']
    body = prm['body'] - (prm['body'] - prm['body_deep']) * np.clip(d / prm['depth_scale'], 0, 1)
    Vs = F * skyrefl + (1 - F) * body
    # sheen: directly under a ground reflection the water mirrors the lowest sky
    under = np.zeros((H, W))
    for c in range(W):
        last = -99
        for r in range(H):
            if wm[r, c] and G['refl'][r, c] == 1:
                last = r
            elif wm[r, c]:
                under[r, c] = r - last if last >= 0 else 99
    Vs = Vs + np.where((under >= 1) & (under <= 3), prm['sheen'] - 6 * under, 0)
    Vs = Vs + (pk.value_noise(H, W, 1, rng, 1, aniso=(1.0, 22.0)) - 0.5) * prm['streaks']
    # the index climbs with nearness
    wr = np.nonzero(wm.any(1))[0]
    if len(wr):
        t = np.clip((yy - wr.min()) / max(wr.max() - wr.min(), 1), 0, 1)
        Vs = Vs - t * prm['nearness']
    ws_v = sky_v + wat_v; ws_i = np.r_[sky_i, wat_i]
    Vs = np.minimum(Vs, sky_v[1] - 3)
    msky = wm & (G['refl'] == 0)
    cv[msky] = ch.to_ramp_checker(Vs, ws_v, ws_i, 0.3)[msky]
    # reflected ground: copy the painted pixel at the hit's direct row, tone law on its ramp
    mg = wm & (G['refl'] == 1)
    rr, cc = np.nonzero(mg)
    src = np.clip(np.floor(G['refl_row'][rr, cc]).astype(int), 0, H - 1)
    vals_src = cv[src, cc]
    out = vals_src.copy()
    for rname in ('grav', 'grass', 'earth'):
        out = ch.tone_down(out, P.r(rname))
    # smear sideways a little: reflections streak horizontally
    cv[rr, cc] = out
    for r in sorted(set(rr)):
        cs = cc[rr == r]
        x0 = 0
        rowv = cv[r].copy()
        while x0 < len(cs):
            L = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
            rowv[cs[x0:x0 + L]] = rowv[cs[x0]]
            x0 += L
        cv[r, cs] = rowv[cs]
    if grass_layer is not None:
        mirror_grass(cv, grass_layer, G, cam, P, wm)
    # the reflected band's lower edge steps: where the sky shows through, in runs
    # (already stepped by the terrain's breathing; nothing to add)
    # shelves: see-in where it's shallow -- flat tints from depth bands, a light lip on the outer shelf
    sh1, sh2 = prm['shelf']
    msh = wm & (G['refl'] == 0)
    inner = msh & (d < sh1)
    outer = msh & (d >= sh1) & (d < sh2)
    near_side = G['z'] < np.nanmedian(np.where(wm, G['z'], np.nan))     # the see-in side (toward us)
    cv[inner & near_side] = np.where(d[inner & near_side] < sh1 * 0.45, wat_i[0], wat_i[1])
    cv[outer & near_side] = wat_i[2]
    lip = msh & near_side & (d >= sh2) & np.roll(outer & near_side, -1, 0) & (rng.random((H, W)) < 0.55)
    cv[lip] = wat_i[3]
    # stones in the shelf: flat lozenges, sky-lit top; the ones at the edge sit on the lap line
    cand = np.argwhere((inner | outer) & near_side)
    for i in range(int(len(cand) * 0.05)):
        r, c0 = cand[int(rng.integers(0, len(cand)))]
        L = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
        cs = np.arange(c0, min(W, c0 + L))
        if not (wm[r, cs].all() and r > 0):
            continue
        cv[r, cs] = wat_i[0]
        if L >= 3:
            cv[r - 1, cs[1:-1]] = wat_i[3] if d[r, c0] > sh1 * 0.5 else wat_i[4]
    # wind lines on the open mirror
    ws_sorted = list(np.array(ws_i)[np.argsort(ws_v)])
    for r in wr:
        if rng.random() > prm['wind']:
            continue
        x0 = int(rng.integers(0, W - 4)); L = int(rng.integers(3, 12))
        seg = [x for x in range(x0, min(W, x0 + L)) if msky[r, x] and cv[r, x] in ws_sorted and not (inner | outer)[r, x]]
        if len(seg) < 3:
            continue
        j = max(ws_sorted.index(cv[r, x]) for x in seg)
        for x in seg:
            cv[r, x] = ws_sorted[min(j + 1, len(ws_sorted) - 2)]
    # the near waterline: a broken light lap line on the last water row above the near bank
    last_water = wm & np.roll(gm, -1, 0) & near_side
    runs = np.zeros(W, bool); x = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
    while x < W:
        L = int(rng.integers(2, 7)); runs[x:x + L] = rng.random() < prm['lap']; x += L + int(rng.integers(2, 8))
    sel = last_water & runs[None, :]
    cv[sel] = wat_i[4]
    return cv


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- grass ----------
def paint_grass(cv, G, cam, P, rng, gp):
    """grass on ground above gp['min_y'] (bank tops): blades rising from ground points, length =
    blade height projected; paired [dark gap][highlight][stem]; tips break the silhouette; at a bank
    lip the tufts droop over the face. Returns a layer (-1 = none) of indices for mirroring, with
    each blade pixel's foot row at water level."""
    H, W = cv.shape
    gi = P.r('grass')
    layer = np.full((H, W), -1)
    foot = np.full((H, W), -1.0)
    kind = G['kind']
    on = (kind == 1) & (G['y'] > gp.get('min_y', 0.1))
    # base texture of the grass mass: dark with the mid tones as strands
    for c in range(W):
        rows = np.nonzero(on[:, c])[0]
        for r in rows:
            layer[r, c] = gi[1] if rng.random() < 0.6 else gi[2]
            foot[r, c] = cam.row_of(G['z'][r, c], 0.0)
    # blades: from each grass pixel, sometimes, a stroke upward of projected length
    pts = np.argwhere(on)
    n = int(len(pts) * gp.get('density', 0.35))
    order = pts[rng.integers(0, len(pts), n)] if len(pts) else []
    order = sorted(order, key=lambda t: t[0])          # far (higher rows) first
    for r, c in order:
        z = G['z'][r, c]
        L = max(1, int(round(gp.get('blade_m', 0.35) * cam.f / z * rng.uniform(0.5, 1.1))))
        lean = rng.normal(gp.get('lean', 0.25), 0.25)
        xf = c + 0.5
        lit = rng.random() < gp.get('lit', 0.35)
        for s in range(L):
            y = r - s
            x = int(xf)
            if y < 0 or not (0 <= x < W):
                break
            tone = gi[3] if (lit and s > L * 0.3) else gi[2]
            if s == L - 1 and lit:
                tone = gi[4]
            layer[y, x] = tone
            foot[y, x] = cam.row_of(z, 0.0)
            if lit and x + 1 < W:
                layer[y, x + 1] = gi[1]                     # the stem/dark partner beside the highlight
                foot[y, x + 1] = cam.row_of(z, 0.0)
            xf += lean * (s / max(L, 1)) ** 1.5
    sel = layer >= 0
    cv[sel] = layer[sel]
    return dict(layer=layer, foot=foot)


def mirror_grass(cv, gl, G, cam, P, wm):
    """billboard mirror of the grass marks about each mark's own foot row at water level; hidden
    behind nearer ground reflections is automatic since we only write onto water pixels whose
    reflection is sky or ground further than the grass."""
    H, W = cv.shape
    layer, foot = gl['layer'], gl['foot']
    rs, cs = np.nonzero(layer >= 0)
    rm = np.floor(2 * foot[rs, cs] - rs - 0.5).astype(int)
    ok = (rm >= 0) & (rm < H)
    rs, cs, rm = rs[ok], cs[ok], rm[ok]
    ok = wm[rm, cs]
    rs, cs, rm = rs[ok], cs[ok], rm[ok]
    vals = ch.tone_down(layer[rs, cs], P.r('grass'))
    cv[rm, cs] = vals

"""Stage 2: the edge. Cells: far/near edges against gravel (sloping and cut banks) and grass."""
import sys, time
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
import world, painter

P = painter.grey_palette()
W, H = 128, 80


def cell(name, pitch, terrain, prm=None, seed=1, f=420):
    cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=f, h=1.6, pitch_deg=pitch)
    t0 = time.time()
    G = world.raycast(cam, terrain)
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, P, np.random.default_rng(seed), prm)
    return P.rgb(cv), G, cam


if __name__ == '__main__':
    which = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'a'
    if which == 'a':
        t = world.StraightChannel(5.0, 7.0, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), seed=3)
        rgb, G, cam = cell('far_slope', 12.0, t)
        pk.save(rgb, '../study/s2_a.png', 6)

p='channel.py'; s=open(p).read()
a=s.index("def gravel("); b=s.index("\n\n\ndef ", a+10) if "\n\n\ndef " in s[a+10:] else len(s)
b = s.find("\n\n\ndef ", a+10)
new='''def gravel(mask, base_v, zrow, f, rng, ramp_i, ramp_v, stone_m=(0.03, 0.14), density=1.0, matrix=None):
    """gravel at a low angle, as stones on an ordered base.
    base: the value field through a 4x4 ordered dither (the smooth mass).
    stones: each its own albedo (rivers mix rock types); a flat-bottomed lozenge whose rows follow an
    ellipse (short lit top row, full body, shaded underside) with a shorter contact row beneath;
    width = stone size projected at that row; far stones shrink to 1-px flecks; random y; nearer
    stones overlap farther ones."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    matrix = pk.BAYER4 if matrix is None else matrix
    vals = np.asarray(ramp_v, float); n = len(vals)
    step = np.interp(base_v, vals, np.arange(n))
    out_step = pk.ordered(step, matrix).astype(float)
    marks = []
    for y in range(h):
        z = zrow[y]
        if not np.isfinite(z) or z <= 0:
            continue
        ppm = f / z
        k = int(w * density * (0.10 + 0.30 * np.clip(ppm / 60.0, 0, 1)))
        for _ in range(k):
            s = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(stone_m[0]), np.log(stone_m[1])))
            marks.append((y + rng.random(), rng.uniform(-3, w), s * ppm, rng.normal(0, 0.8)))
    marks.sort(key=lambda t: t[0])
    for (yf, x0, wpx, alb) in marks:
        y = int(yf)
        if wpx < 0.8:
            if rng.random() < 0.5:
                continue
            xi = int(x0)
            if 0 <= xi < w and mask[y, xi]:
                out_step[y, xi] = step[y, xi] + (1.2 if alb >= 0 else -1.2)
            continue
        L = max(1, int(round(wpx)))
        hgt = max(2, int(round(wpx * 0.42)))            # rows: a stone seen at a low angle
        cx = x0 + L / 2
        for j in range(hgt + 1):
            yy = y + j
            if yy >= h:
                break
            if j < hgt:
                # ellipse profile with a flat bottom: rows widen quickly from the top
                t = (j + 0.5) / hgt
                half = L / 2 * np.sqrt(max(0.0, 1 - (1 - min(1.0, t * 1.6)) ** 2))
                if j == 0:
                    off = 1.5 + 0.3 * alb                   # lit top
                elif j == hgt - 1:
                    off = -0.9 + 0.3 * alb                  # shaded underside
                else:
                    off = 0.5 + 0.6 * alb                   # body in its own albedo
            else:
                half = L / 2 * 0.7                          # contact shadow, shorter
                off = -1.9
            xs = np.arange(int(np.floor(cx - half + 0.5)), int(np.floor(cx + half + 0.5)))
            xs = xs[(xs >= 0) & (xs < w)]
            if len(xs) == 0:
                continue
            sel = mask[yy, xs]
            out_step[yy, xs[sel]] = step[yy, xs[sel]] + off
    q = np.clip(np.round(out_step), 0, n - 1).astype(int)
    return np.asarray(ramp_i)[q]'''
s=s[:a]+new+s[b:]
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, channel as ch, painter

p='painter.py'; s=open(p).read()
a=s.index("def paint_grass("); b=s.index("def mirror_grass(")
s=s[:a]+'''def paint_grass(cv, G, cam, P, rng, gp):
    """grass on ground above gp['min_y'] (the bank top): masses first, detail at the edges.
    body: vertical strands (runs of adjacent tones), lighter near the crest, darker deeper, with
      paired [dark gap][highlight][stem] marks;
    crest: clumps every 3-8 px, each fanning 3-6 single 1-px blades, dark against what is behind,
      a rim-lit tip now and then (the fringe, never a cut edge);
    lip: where the mass ends over a cut face, a few blades droop down over the dark face, lit.
    The cut face under the grass is earth (dark), not gravel."""
    H, W = cv.shape
    gi = P.r('grass'); ei = P.r('earth')
    layer = np.full((H, W), -1)
    foot = np.full((H, W), -1.0)
    kind = G['kind']
    on = (kind == 1) & (G['y'] > gp.get('min_y', 0.1)) & (G['facing'] < 1.5)
    face = (kind == 1) & (G['facing'] >= 1.5) & (G['y'] > 0.04)
    # earth face: dark, with a lighter checker near its top (the root mat catches light)
    for c in range(W):
        rows = np.nonzero(face[:, c])[0]
        for k, r in enumerate(rows):
            layer[r, c] = ei[1] if rng.random() < 0.7 else ei[0]
            if k < 2 and (r + c) % 2 == 0:
                layer[r, c] = ei[2]
            foot[r, c] = cam.row_of(G['z'][r, c], 0.0)
    zmed = np.nanmedian(np.where(on, G['z'], np.nan)) if on.any() else 6.0
    bl = max(3, gp.get('blade_m', 0.35) * cam.f / zmed)          # blade length in px
    for c in range(W):
        rows = np.nonzero(on[:, c])[0]
        if len(rows) == 0:
            continue
        top = rows.min()
        r = top
        tone = 3
        while r <= rows.max():
            L = int(max(2, rng.integers(int(bl * 0.25) + 1, int(bl * 0.6) + 2)))
            dt = (r - top) / max(bl * 1.5, 1)
            target = 3.4 - 2.2 * min(dt, 1.0) + rng.normal(0, 0.5)
            tone = int(np.clip(round(tone + np.sign(target - tone)), 1, 4))   # adjacent walk
            for k in range(L):
                rr = r + k
                if rr > rows.max() or not on[rr, c]:
                    break
                layer[rr, c] = gi[tone]
                foot[rr, c] = cam.row_of(G['z'][rr, c], 0.0)
            if tone >= 3 and c + 1 < W and rng.random() < 0.6:        # the stem partner
                for k in range(L):
                    if r + k < H and on[r + k, c + 1]:
                        layer[r + k, c + 1] = gi[tone - 1]
            if tone >= 3 and c - 1 >= 0 and rng.random() < 0.35:      # the dark gap
                for k in range(L):
                    if r + k < H and on[r + k, c - 1]:
                        layer[r + k, c - 1] = gi[0]
            r += L
    # crest fringe: clumps fanning single blades above the mass top
    tops = [(c, np.nonzero(on[:, c])[0].min()) for c in range(W) if on[:, c].any()]
    c = int(rng.integers(0, 4))
    while c < W:
        tt = [t for t in tops if t[0] == c]
        if tt:
            r0 = tt[0][1]
            nb = int(rng.integers(3, 7))
            for i in range(nb):
                spread = (i - (nb - 1) / 2) / max(nb, 1)
                lean = spread * 1.2 + gp.get('lean', 0.0) + rng.normal(0, 0.1)
                L = int(bl * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.9))
                xf = c + 0.5 + rng.normal(0, 0.8)
                lit = rng.random() < gp.get('lit', 0.35)
                for sidx in range(L):
                    y = r0 - sidx
                    x = int(xf)
                    if y < 0 or not (0 <= x < W):
                        break
                    tone = gi[1] if not (lit and sidx >= L - 2) else gi[4]
                    if layer[y, x] < 0 or sidx > 0:
                        layer[y, x] = tone
                        foot[y, x] = cam.row_of(G['z'][r0, min(c, W - 1)], 0.0)
                    xf += lean * (sidx / max(L, 1)) ** 1.3
        c += int(rng.integers(3, 9))
    # lip: blades drooping over the cut face
    for c in range(W):
        rows = np.nonzero(face[:, c])[0]
        if len(rows) == 0 or rng.random() > gp.get('droop', 0.45):
            continue
        r0 = rows.min()
        L = int(rng.integers(2, max(3, int(bl * 0.35))))
        x = c
        for k in range(L):
            if r0 + k < H and face[r0 + k, x]:
                layer[r0 + k, x] = gi[3] if k < L - 1 else gi[2]
            if rng.random() < 0.3:
                x = min(W - 1, max(0, x + rng.choice([-1, 1])))
    sel = layer >= 0
    cv[sel] = layer[sel]
    return dict(layer=layer, foot=foot)


'''+s[b:]
open(p,'w').write(s)

Bash: Crest tufts; grass on far cut bank
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='painter.py'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""    # crest fringe: clumps fanning single blades above the mass top
    tops = [(c, np.nonzero(on[:, c])[0].min()) for c in range(W) if on[:, c].any()]""","""    # crest tufts: the mass top is never a ruled line -- domes of 4-10 px rising 2-6 px above it,
    # lit on their crests, dark holes between them
    c = 0
    while c < W:
        tw = int(rng.integers(4, 11)); th = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, int(bl * 0.18)) + 1))
        for x in range(c, min(W, c + tw)):
            if topr[x] >= H:
                continue
            u = (x - c + 0.5) / tw
            hh = int(round(th * np.sqrt(max(0, 1 - (2 * u - 1) ** 2))))
            for k in range(1, hh + 1):
                y = topr[x] - k
                if y >= 0:
                    layer[y, x] = gi[4] if k == hh else (gi[3] if k >= hh - 1 else gi[2])
                    foot[y, x] = foot[topr[x], x]
            if hh == 0 and topr[x] < H:
                layer[topr[x], x] = gi[0]                      # a hole between tufts
        c += tw
    newtop = np.full(W, H)
    for x in range(W):
        rr_ = np.nonzero(layer[:, x] >= 0)[0]
        rr_ = rr_[rr_ <= topr[x]] if topr[x] < H else []
        if len(rr_):
            newtop[x] = rr_.min()
    # crest fringe: clumps fanning single blades above the tufts
    tops = [(c, int(newtop[c])) for c in range(W) if newtop[c] < H]""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
p='s2_edges.py'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("dict(grass=dict(side='far', min_y=0.2,","dict(grass=dict(side='far', min_y=0.08,")
open(p,'w').write(s)

    # one-row checker, which reads as a dotted line)
    ext = np.zeros(W, int); x = 0
    while x < W:

import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, s2_edges as S
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

          'c_near_gravel': 'near edge, gravel', 'd_far_grass': 'far edge, tussock over a cut face',
          'e_near_grass': 'near edge, tussock fringe on the water'}

    items.append((rgb, lab))

      (pk.photo_crop('../photos/commons_Wilkin_River_close_to_its_confluence_with_Makaro_3b477161.jpg', (560, 300, 340, 212), (128, 80)), 'photo: Wilkin tussock bank'),
      (pk.photo_crop(R + 'braided_lowangle_healy.jpg', (380, 500, 640, 400), (128, 80)), 'photo: Healy bar edges')]

d2 = pk.lw_load('V16PM'); fr2 = pk.rgb(d2['idx'], d2['pal'])

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; cat >> LESSONS.md <<'EOF'

---

## Stage 2: the edge

`src/world.py` (the terrain and a raycaster that produce a per-pixel G-buffer), `src/painter.py` (Ferrari's rules applied to the G-buffer), `src/s2_edges.py` (cells), `src/s2_sheet.py`.

![edges](out/s2_edges.png)
8× cells: `out/s2_*_8x.png`.

**A change of machinery, and why.** Stage 1 painted a strip whose geometry was hand-coded row by row. Edges need true geometry, and so will winding channels (stage 5). So the world is now a ground **heightfield**; the water surface is y = 0 wherever the ground is below it. Each screen column is a vertical plane through the eye, so it is raycast as a 1-D profile. A **reflected ray stays in its column**, so a water pixel's reflection is found by marching the same column upward from the water point. The painter gets, per pixel: kind, distance, height, facing, water depth, and for water, what the reflected ray hits (sky elevation, or a ground point and the screen row where that point is painted). The reflection then *copies the painted pixel* at that row and applies the tone law. Anything the mirror sees on a heightfield is also visible directly, so the copy always exists. (One bug here was instructive: reflected hits snapped to the march grid made a cut bank's reflection a set of vertical stripes. Hits are now interpolated like direct hits.)

**What the edges are made of, from the geometry and the photos:**

- **The far edge is dark, and the dark is two things.** The bank's wet foot seen directly (wetness from height above the water: darker below about 7 cm, textured by the gravel painter, not a flat band) plus that foot's reflection. A *sloping* bank reflects 1–3 rows; a *cut* bank reflects as tall as its face. Under grass the face is earth (dark, lighter at the root mat) with lit blades drooping over the lip. This is V02's "far bank dark", and the Denali and Healy photos show it as one thin dark line.
- **A reflection is still water.** Streaked sideways in runs of 2–5 px and crossed by dotted light wind lines. Its lower edge *steps* 0–2 rows in runs of 3–10 px. (I first dissolved it with a checker row and got the dotted-line tell yet again.)
- **The near edge is light.** A broken lap line on the last water row (runs of 2–6 px), the shelf where the bed shows (two flat tints with stepped edges), stones as flat lozenges, then the wet margin of the near bank.
- **Glacial water is milky.** I first computed the body as the bed seen through clear water. Close to the edge, at steeper angles, that made the water as dark as the gravel. Braided rivers carry rock flour and are nearly opaque, so the body is the bed fading into the water's own pale "milk" with depth (visibility of a few cm). This single parameter (`visibility`) is what turns a braid's channels into bright ribbons. It will be a sheet axis: clear spring-fed channels and milky glacial ones differ exactly here.
- **The bed is flat near the margins** (depth ∝ (4s(1−s))^1.6). Otherwise the shelf where you see the bottom is a single row.
- **Edges breathe:** both edge lines and the bank height wander in piecewise-constant runs of 0.3–1.2 m along the channel. No smoothing, since smoothed steps made "hills".
- **Gravel close up** (a small swatch study, `study/gravel_swatch.png`): stones must be **light blobs on dark interstices**, rounder than I thought (height ≈ 0.75 × width at these angles, because you see the top *and* the front face), each with its own albedo, at modest density. Flatter, denser stones read as brick courses every time.
- **Tussock against water** (after the grass study's POST and V16): masses first. A body of short leaning strokes with paired partners. A crest of dense 1-px ticks of random height, never a ruled top. Then sparse single dark blades fanning in V and Y shapes out over the water, counterchanged against its light.

**Honest comparison.** Every edge now has its parts in the right order and value: dark far foot and reflection, sheen, shelf, lap line, wet margin, fringe. **Not Ferrari:**

1. My grass is a speckled weave, where his is dense olive strands drawn as curved blades in clumps. My fringe blades are too even in spacing and too straight low down.
2. The breathing far edge is a staircase of clean steps in places, but where two runs meet at different depths it makes little "teeth".
3. My gravel reads as gravel only at a squint. Up close it is busy, and a cut face looks like the bar top rather than an exposed section (his rocks are planes; mine are speckle).
4. The cut-bank cell is mostly bank and reflection, with little water. It's true to a 0.45 m bank from 1.6 m, but it's a weak composition.
EOF

def strands(h, w, rng, classes_fn, u_fn, runmean_fn, mask=None, spread=0.55, rho_fn=None,
            tuft=None, pair_p=0.6, pair_side=+1, gap_p=0.0, gap_class=None, jog_p=0.3,
            adjacent=True, jump_p=0.2, target_fn=None, lean_dir=0):
    """Ferrari's lawn micro-structure (measured on Mirror Pond, see LESSONS.md):
      * STACKED: down a column, runs step through ADJACENT values (dark -> mid -> lit -> mid);
        a highlight run is capped above and below by the mid class ~75% of the time.
      * PAIRED: a highlight run has a partner run one value darker in the neighbouring
        column on a consistent side, over the same rows (peak at dy=0) -- stem + highlight,
        a blade two pixels wide.  In shadow grass the pair also has a black gap on the
        other side ([black][hi][partner]).
    classes_fn(y, x) -> ordered class ids dark->light;  u_fn(y, x) -> target position (float);
    runmean_fn(y, x) -> mean vertical run length.  Returns index canvas (-1 outside mask)."""
    from scipy.special import ndtr
    cv = np.full((h, w), -1, int)
    runs = []
    for x in range(w):
        y = 0
        prev = None
        while y < h:
            if mask is not None and not mask[y, x]:
                y += 1; prev = None
                continue
            m = runmean_fn(y, x)
            L = 1 if m <= 1.1 else int(rng.geometric(1.0 / m))
            yc = min(h - 1, y + L // 2)
            cls = classes_fn(yc, x)
            n = len(cls)
            rh = rho_fn(yc, x) if rho_fn is not None else 0.0
            z = rh * (tuft[yc, x] if tuft is not None else 0) + np.sqrt(1 - rh ** 2) * rng.normal()
            if target_fn is not None:
                target = int(target_fn(yc, x, z))
            else:
                target = int(np.clip(np.round(u_fn(yc, x) + spread * z), 0, n - 1))
            if prev is None or not adjacent or rng.random() < jump_p:
                k = target
            else:
                pk = min(prev, n - 1)
                if target > pk:
                    k = pk + 1
                elif target < pk:
                    k = pk - 1
                else:
                    k = pk + (rng.choice([-1, 1]) if rng.random() < 0.6 else 0)
                k = int(np.clip(k, 0, n - 1))
            c = cls[k]
            xx = x
            y1 = min(h, y + L)
            # leaning blade: a clean 2:1 or 3:1 staircase (short straight runs, regular steps)
            lean = 0; step = 99
            if L >= 3 and rng.random() < jog_p:
                lean = int(rng.choice([-1, 1])) if lean_dir == 0 else lean_dir
                step = int(rng.choice([2, 3, 3, 4]))
            for yy in range(y, y1):
                if mask is not None and not mask[yy, x]:
                    y1 = yy
                    break
                if lean and yy > y and (y1 - 1 - yy) % step == 0:
                    xx = int(np.clip(xx + lean, 0, w - 1))
                cv[yy, xx] = c
            if y1 > y:
                runs.append((x, y, y1,
