# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- objects --------
def paint_objects(cv, G, cam, P, rng, objs):
    """billboard objects standing on the ground: dict(kind='boulder'|'willow', x, z, w, h) in metres.
    Depth-tested against the ground; returns a layer (index, foot row at water level, kind)."""
    if not objs:
        return None
    H, W = cv.shape
    layer = np.full((H, W), -1); foot = np.full((H, W), -1.0); kinds = np.full((H, W), '', 'U8')
    for o in sorted(objs, key=lambda o: -o['z']):
        c0 = cam.col_of(o['x'], o['z'])
        # the ground height under the object: sample the G-buffer's heights near its foot row
        y0 = o.get('y0', 0.0)
        base = cam.row_of(o['z'], y0)
        wpx = o['w'] * cam.f / o['z']; hpx = o['h'] * cam.f / o['z']
        sub = np.random.default_rng(o.get('seed', 7))
        if o['kind'] == 'boulder':
            m, lab = ch.boulder_mask(H, W, c0, base, wpx, hpx, sub)
        else:
            yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
            u = (xx + 0.5 - c0) / (wpx / 2)
            top = base - hpx * np.clip(1 - u ** 2, 0, 1) ** 0.6 * (0.85 + 0.15 * np.cos(u * 9 + o.get('seed', 0)))
            m = (np.abs(u) < 1) & (yy + 0.5 >= top) & (yy + 0.5 < base)
        # depth test: hidden where the ground in front is nearer
        m &= ~((G['kind'] != 0) & (G['z'] < o['z'] - 0.3))
        tmp = cv.copy()
        if o['kind'] == 'boulder':
            ch.paint_faceted(tmp, m, lab, P.r('bould'), sub, top=(3.1, 3.9), sun=(1.9, 2.9), shade=(0.5, 1.2))
        else:
            ch.paint_shrub(tmp, m, P.r('wil'), sub)
        layer[m] = tmp[m]; kinds[m] = o['kind']
        foot[m] = cam.row_of(o['z'], 0.0)
    cv[layer >= 0] = layer[layer >= 0]
    return dict(layer=layer, foot=foot, kind=kinds)


def mirror_objects(cv, ol, G, cam, P, wm, rng):
    """a pixel at row r reflects to 2*R0 - r (R0: the object's foot at water level); only onto water
    whose own reflection is sky or ground FARTHER than the object (nearer bank reflections occlude)."""
    H, W = cv.shape
    rs, cs = np.nonzero(ol['layer'] >= 0)
    rm = np.floor(2 * ol['foot'][rs, cs] - rs - 0.5).astype(int)
    ok = (rm >= 0) & (rm < H)
    rs, cs, rm = rs[ok], cs[ok], rm[ok]
    ok = wm[rm, cs] & ~((G['refl'][rm, cs] == 1) & (G['refl_z'][rm, cs] < cam.f * cam.h / np.maximum(ol['foot'][rs, cs] - cam.hz, 1e-3) - 0.2))
    rs, cs, rm = rs[ok], cs[ok], rm[ok]
    vals = ol['layer'][rs, cs]
    kd = ol['kind'][rs, cs]
    out = vals.copy()
    b = kd == 'boulder'
    out[b] = ch.tone_down(ch.tone_down(vals[b], P.r('bould')), P.r('bould'), dark_up=False)
    out[~b] = ch.tone_down(vals[~b], P.r('wil'))
    cv[rm, cs] = out
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

"""Stage 1, rebuilt on the stage-2 machinery (world.raycast + painter): the still strip with a
boulder and a willow on the far bank. Same scene as s1_still.py, one technique."""
import sys
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
import world, painter

P = painter.grey_palette()
W, H = 192, 108


def render(seed=1, prm=None):
    cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=300, h=1.6, pitch_deg=7.0)
    t = world.StraightChannel(7.0, 11.0, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), seed=seed + 4)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t)
    objs = [dict(kind='boulder', x=-2.3, z=11.9, w=1.5, h=0.62, y0=0.2, seed=11),
            dict(kind='willow', x=2.8, z=12.6, w=2.6, h=1.9, y0=0.2, seed=5)]
    q = dict(objects=objs, stones=(0.03, 0.14))
    q.update(prm or {})
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, P, np.random.default_rng(seed), q)
    return P.rgb(cv), G, cam


if __name__ == '__main__':
    rgb, G, cam = render()
    pk.save(rgb, '../out/s1_still.png', 4)
    pk.save(rgb[44:96, 0:96], '../out/s1_still_8x_left.png', 8)
    pk.save(rgb[0:96, 96:192], '../out/s1_still_8x_right.png', 8)
    grey = lambda a: np.stack([(a @ np.array([0.3, 0.59, 0.11])).astype(np.uint8)] * 3, -1)
    ph = grey(pk.photo_crop('~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_bars_denali.jpg', (60, 400, 450, 230), (90, 46)).astype(float))
    d = pk.lw_load('V16PM'); fr = grey(pk.rgb(d['idx'], d['pal']).astype(float))[392:438, 250:340]
    pk.triptych(ph, rgb[50:96, 0:90], fr, '../out/s1_triptych.png', s=6,

The images above now come from the same raycaster and painter as every later stage, so there is one technique rather than two. The boulder and willow are billboards, depth-tested against the ground, and they reflect by the billboard mirror (hidden behind nearer bank reflections). This fixed round 4's points:

- The boulder reflection is now a squat inverted dome cut off by the bank's reflection, with no point.
- The willow's reflection carries its clump texture.
- The water has one shelf tint plus stones instead of stacked bands.
- The near gravel is clearly coarser than the far bar (stones fill a row by *area*, so big stones are few), and far flecks fade out with distance.
The old hand-coded version is kept as `src/s1_still.py` for the record.

**Honest comparison (the triptych).** The strip reads as water at a glance, with the right notan.""")

Bash: Add riffles and bed stones to terrain
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='world.py'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""        self.bar_rise = bar_rise
""","""        self.bar_rise = bar_rise
        self.riffles = []
        self.stones = []

    def add_riffle(self, xc, half, strength=1.0, n_stones=40, seed=0):
        \"\"\"a gravel sill crossing the channel at x=xc: the bed rises (shallow), the surface breaks.\"\"\"
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.riffles.append((xc, half, strength))
        for i in range(n_stones):
            x = xc + rng.normal(0, half * 0.6)
            z = rng.uniform(self.z_near + 0.1, self.z_far - 0.1)
            r = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(0.05), np.log(0.22)))
            self.stones.append((x, z, r))

    def roughness(self, x, z):
        R = np.zeros(np.broadcast(x, z).shape)
        for (xc, half, st) in self.riffles:
            R = np.maximum(R, st * np.exp(-((x - xc) / half) ** 2))
        return R
""")
# riffle raises the bed: depth scaled down in riffles
s=s.replace("""        bed = -self.depth * np.clip(4 * s * (1 - s), 0, 1) ** 1.6 - 0.01""","""        sill = 1.0
        for (xc, half, st) in getattr(self, 'riffles', []):
            sill = sill * (1 - 0.7 * st * np.exp(-((x - xc) / half) ** 2))
        bed = -self.depth * sill * np.clip(4 * s * (1 - s), 0, 1) ** 1.6 - 0.01""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

Bash: Flow marks painter; stage 3 first render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='painter.py'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""    if grass_layer is not None:                 # grass stands in front of the water: redraw it""","""    if prm.get('terrain') is not None and getattr(prm['terrain'], 'riffles', None):
        paint_flow(cv, G, cam, P, rng, prm['terrain'], prm)
    if grass_layer is not None:                 # grass stands in front of the water: redraw it""")
s += '''

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- moving water ---
def paint_flow(cv, G, cam, P, rng, T, prm):
    """a riffle, said with as few deliberate marks as possible (flow along +x):
    1 the mirror breaks: inside the rough zone, reflected banks are chopped into dashes with glare
      between (a broken reflection is still recognisably the bank);
    2 wave marks: short horizontal light-over-dark PAIRS (the back of a wave mirrors low bright sky,
      its face toward us mirrors high sky / shows the bed), laid along crest lines that cross the
      channel, bowed downstream in the middle, broken where the zone thins;
    3 foam: white 1-3 px flecks on the crests at the sill's lip only;
    4 stones: emergent ones are dark wet lozenges with a white pillow upstream and a dashed V wake
      downstream; submerged ones show as dark pockets with a light hump dash just downstream;
    5 streaks: below the riffle, long faint light lines along the flow."""
    H, W = cv.shape
    sky_i = P.r('sky'); wat_i = P.r('wat')
    wm = G['kind'] == 2
    R = np.where(wm, T.roughness(G['x'], G['z']), 0)
    glare, foam, darkw = sky_i[2], sky_i[0], wat_i[1]
    # 1 broken reflections
    refl = wm & (G['refl'] == 1) & (R > 0.2)
    for r in range(H):
        cs = np.nonzero(refl[r])[0]
        x = 0
        while x < len(cs):
            L = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
            if rng.random() < 0.55 * R[r, cs[x]]:
                cv[r, cs[x:x + L]] = glare if rng.random() < 0.6 else sky_i[3]
            x += L + int(rng.integers(1, 4))

    def put(r, c, v):
        if 0 <= r < H and 0 <= c < W and wm[r, c]:
            cv[r, c] = v

    def proj(x, z):
        return int(np.floor(cam.row_of(z, 0.0))), int(np.floor(cam.col_of(x, z)))
    # 2 + 3 crest lines
    for (xc, half, st) in T.riffles:
        lam = prm.get('wave_m', 0.45)
        xs = np.arange(xc - 2.2 * half, xc + 2.2 * half, lam)
        for x0 in xs:
            a = st * np.exp(-((x0 - xc) / half) ** 2)
            if a < 0.12:
                continue
            zs = np.arange(T.z_near + 0.05, T.z_far - 0.05, 0.06)
            bow = 0.25 * lam * np.sin(np.pi * (zs - T.z_near) / (T.z_far - T.z_near))
            z = zs[0]; i = 0
            while i < len(zs):
                seg = int(rng.integers(3, 10))
                on = rng.random() < 0.35 + 0.6 * a
                for k in range(i, min(len(zs), i + seg)):
                    if not on:
                        continue
                    xx = x0 + bow[k] + rng.normal(0, 0.03)
                    r, c = proj(xx, zs[k])
                    ppm = cam.f / zs[k]
                    L = max(1, int(round(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.22) * ppm * (0.6 + 0.6 * a))))
                    for j in range(L):
                        put(r, c + j, foam if (a > 0.75 and rng.random() < 0.45) else glare)
                        if rng.random() < 0.8:
                            put(r + 1, c + j, darkw)
                i += seg + int(rng.integers(1, 5))
    # 4 stones
    for (x, z, rad) in T.stones:
        dep = -T.height(np.array([x]), np.array([z]))[0]
        if dep <= 0:
            continue
        r0, c0 = proj(x, z)
        ppm = cam.f / z
        wpx = max(1, int(round(2 * rad * ppm)))
        if rad * 0.9 > dep:                                   # emergent
            hpx = max(1, int(round(wpx * 0.35)))
            for j in range(hpx):
                for i in range(wpx):
                    put(r0 - j, c0 + i - wpx // 2, darkw if j else wat_i[0])
            for i in range(max(1, wpx - 2)):
                put(r0 - hpx, c0 + i - wpx // 2 + 1, wat_i[3])          # sky-lit wet top
            for j in range(hpx + 1):
                put(r0 - j, c0 - wpx // 2 - 1, foam)                    # pillow upstream
            # V wake downstream: two dashed lines diverging at +-25 deg in the ground plane
            for sgn in (-1, 1):
                for t in np.arange(rad, 5 * rad, 0.04):
                    if int(t / 0.04) % 3 == 2:
                        continue
                    rr, cc = proj(x + rad + t * np.cos(0.44), z + sgn * t * np.sin(0.44))
                    put(rr, cc, glare)
        elif dep < 0.14:                                      # submerged: pocket + hump dash
            for i in range(wpx):
                put(r0, c0 + i - wpx // 2, darkw)
            for i in range(max(1, wpx)):
                put(r0 - 1, c0 + i - wpx // 2 + max(1, wpx // 2), glare)
    # 5 streaks below the riffle
    for (xc, half, st) in T.riffles:
        for i in range(int(12 * st)):
            x = xc + half * rng.uniform(1.0, 3.5)
            z = rng.uniform(T.z_near + 0.2, T.z_far - 0.2)
            r, c = proj(x, z)
            L = int(rng.integers(6, 24))
            for j in range(L):
                if rng.random() < 0.85:
                    put(r, c + j, sky_i[3])
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
cat > s3_flow.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 3: moving water. A channel crossing the view (flow left to right) with a riffle over a sill."""
import sys
import numpy as np
import pixkit as pk
import world, painter

P = painter.grey_palette()


def render(strength=1.0, pitch=9.0, zn=6.0, zf=9.5, f=380, W=160, H=90, seed=1, extra=None):
    cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=f, h=1.6, pitch_deg=pitch)
    t = world.StraightChannel(zn, zf, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), seed=seed + 4)
    if strength > 0:
        t.add_riffle(0.0, 1.2, strength, n_stones=int(30 * strength) + 4, seed=seed)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t)
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.0 + 1.2 * np.sin(u * 9) + 0.5 * np.sin(u * 23), scrub=(120.0, 3.0))
    q = dict(terrain=t, backdrop=bd, stones=(0.03, 0.14))
    q.update(extra or {})
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, P, np.random.default_rng(seed), q)
    return P.rgb(cv)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    rgbs = [(render(s), 'flow %.1f' % s) for s in (0.0, 0.5, 1.0)]
    pk.stack([pk.panel(rgbs, 4)]).save('../study/s3_v1.png')
EOF

    # us mirror higher sky and let the bed show -- laid by DENSITY: solid core, checker margin,
    # sparse spray, cut into streaks along the crests (across the flow)

    # the zone is densest

                r, c = proj(x0 + bow[k], zs[k])

                white = a > 0.8 and rng.random() < 0.3
                for j in range(L):
                    put(r, c + j, foam if white else glare)
'''+s[b:]

open(p,'w').write(s)

                r += seg + int(rng.integers(1, 4))
'''+s[b:]

            hpx = max(1, int(round(wpx * 0.4)))

            for i in range(max(1, wpx - 2)):
                put(r0 - hpx, c0 + i - wpx // 2 + 1, wat_i[3])          # sky-lit wet top
            put(r0, c0 - wpx // 2 - 1, foam)                             # pillow upstream
            if hpx > 1:
                put(r0 - 1, c0 - wpx // 2 - 1, glare)
            # wake downstream: a dashed light run leaving the stone's downstream side, thinning
            Lw = int(rng.integers(3, 7) + wpx)
            for j in range(Lw):
                if j % 3 != 2 and rng.random() < 1 - j / (Lw + 1):
                    put(r0, c0 + wpx // 2 + 1 + j, glare)
                    if j > 2 and rng.random() < 0.4:
                        put(r0 - 1, c0 + wpx // 2 + 1 + j, glare)
'''+s[b:]
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

"""stage 3 sheet: flow strength x distance, plus a willow whose reflection breaks in the riffle."""
import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, s3_flow as S
rows = []
for dist, (zn, zf, f, pitch) in (('close (6-9.5 m)', (6.0, 9.5, 380, 9.0)), ('mid (14-22 m)', (14.0, 22.0, 380, 5.0))):
    items = [(S.render(s, pitch=pitch, zn=zn, zf=zf, f=f), '%s  flow %.1f' % (dist, s)) for s in (0.0, 0.5, 1.0)]
    rows.append(pk.panel(items, 3))
wil = dict(objects=[dict(kind='willow', x=1.2, z=10.4, w=2.6, h=1.8, y0=0.2, seed=3)])
rows.append(pk.panel([(S.render(1.0, extra=wil), 'a willow: its reflection breaks in the riffle'),
                      (S.render(0.0, extra=wil), 'the same willow over still water')], 3))

pk.stack(rows).save('../out/s3_flow.png')

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; cat >> LESSONS.md <<'EOF'

---

## Stage 3: moving water

`painter.paint_flow`, riffles in `world.StraightChannel.add_riffle`, `src/s3_flow.py`, `src/s3_sheet.py`.

![flow](out/s3_flow.png)
8×: `out/s3_riffle_8x.png`.

**The model.** A riffle is a gravel **sill** crossing the channel. Its **lip** is a line in plan (skewed across the channel, as sills usually are). Upstream of the lip the water is a smooth glassy tongue; the bed rises to the lip and falls away behind it. Downstream the surface breaks, hardest just below the lip, and dies away over a set distance (a standing-wave train). Roughness R(x, z) comes from that, and every mark is placed by it. Nothing is scattered at random over the water.

**What took several wrong tries:**

1. **Marks follow the streamlines.** I first drew wave crests (lines across the channel). At a low angle they foreshortened into vertical chains that read as fence posts. Then I noticed that V11AM's "jets" run up the screen because *its* river flows toward the viewer: Ferrari's marks follow the flow. Here the flow crosses the view, so the marks are **horizontal**: dashed runs of glare, each with a shorter dark run just under it (the back of a wave mirrors low bright sky; its face mirrors high sky or shows the bed). They are dense below the lip and spaced wider downstream. This matches the golden-hour photo's streaked sheets exactly.
2. **The broken zone is a tone, laid by density (V11's grammar).** A solid darker core, a checker margin and sparse spray, cut into streaks along the flow by anisotropic noise. The mirror gives way to darker tones, because facets tilted toward you mirror higher sky and let the bed show.
3. **The lip is one deliberate mark.** A broken bright line of foam and glare, stepped (never ruled), where the tongue breaks.
4. **Where the mirror breaks** (the willow pair in the sheet): over still water the willow's reflection is a solid dark mass. In the riffle the same reflection is chopped into dashes with glare between, but still recognisably the willow's.
5. **Stones:** emergent ones are dark wet lozenges with a sky-lit top, one white pixel of pillow on the upstream side, and a dashed light wake leaving the downstream side and thinning. Submerged ones in shallow water are a dark pocket with a light hump dash just downstream. My first stones were half a metre across and dominated; river cobbles in a riffle are 5–25 cm.
6. **Level of detail:** at 14–22 m the same riffle is a thin slanted band of glare streaks with the lip. The flow reads by tone and direction, not by stones.

**Colour cycling (not yet done):** every mark here has a natural phase along the flow (distance from the lip). The V11 finding is that dashes carry *consecutive* cycle indices so a light runs along them. The marks can take cycle slots in stage 6's palette; I haven't built that yet.

**Honest comparison.** Beside the golden-hour photo, the riffle reads: a slanted lip, streaked broken water, and a willow's reflection breaking. **Not Ferrari:**

1. His V11 riffle is a few decisive jets with plain mirror between. Mine is still a *field* of streaks. More marks than needed, more even than his.
2. My glare runs are close to the same length; his dashes vary more, and their tones step along the dash.
3. The dark sill shallows on the near side make a heavy wedge at the lower left.
4. At mid distance the riffle is almost invisible. He would exaggerate it (a clear slanted band of light dashes), and so should I: "the medium stylizes beyond nature".
EOF

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 6 — 2026-09-25 22:24 — stage 2 `out/s2_edges.png` and `out/s2_*_8x.png`
**REVISE.** Your "honest comparison" list is accurate, and I agree with all four points. The heightfield and column-raycast reflection is a genuinely good move: the reflections now come from the geometry and copy the painted pixels, which is exactly Ferrari's mirror. (My read; your call.)

Beyond your list:

1. **The fringe blades read as a row of forks** ("near edge, tussock fringe on the water"): dark Y and V shapes at nearly even spacing along one line, all about the same height, like dead saplings or tuning forks planted in a row. Compare "Ferrari V16: grass fringe on water": a few long curved blades grouped in one clump, very different lengths, most leaning one way, a couple crossing. Group them, vary the heights 3:1, curve them, and leave long gaps.
2. **The tussock body is a weave** (far and near tussock cells): short vertical ticks at even density, which reads as fabric. Wilkin photo: tussock is clumps with a lit crown and dark skirts, and the light falls in drifts across the clumps, not evenly.
3. **The gravel is camouflage.** In every gravel cell the stone blobs are the same mid-light tone on a slightly darker ground, at uniform density, so at 1× it reads as camo print. Your swatch finding (light blobs on dark interstices) is right, but the interstices aren't dark enough yet. In the Healy and Denali photos the bar reads as a smooth pale surface at distance, with texture only close up. Try letting the far gravel go nearly flat.
4. **The heart reflection in stage 1** is still in `s1_still_8x_left.png`. If the stage-1 strip is now rendered by the new machinery, re-render it.

"""Hour-of-day light: sun tint, ambient (sky) tint, sky ramp.  Every material
ramp in a scene is derived from an albedo under one of these, so a whole
scene re-lights by changing one word."""
import numpy as np
from .core import hexrgb, lit_ramp, mix, haze

HOURS = {
    'dawn':  dict(sun=(255, 196, 150), amb=(120, 130, 190), sky=['#5a6aa8', '#8a8cc0', '#c7a8c4', '#f0c0a8', '#ffdcb0'], glow=0.6),
    'noon':  dict(sun=(255, 250, 235), amb=(150, 180, 220), sky=['#3f7fd0', '#5a98dc', '#7fb2e6', '#a6cbee', '#cfe3f4'], glow=0.0),
    'after': dict(sun=(255, 236, 200), amb=(140, 165, 210), sky=['#4a7cc4', '#6a96d0', '#8fb0da', '#b8cde2', '#dde2e2'], glow=0.2),
    'dusk':  dict(sun=(255, 168, 110), amb=(150, 128, 196), sky=['#8d7fbf', '#a88dc4', '#c69cc6', '#dfa6b4', '#eeb4a4', '#f6c8a8'], glow=0.8),
    'night': dict(sun=(120, 140, 200), amb=(40, 50, 95), sky=['#0c1030', '#141c48', '#1e2a5c', '#2c3a6e', '#3e4c7c'], glow=0.0),
    'overcast': dict(sun=(210, 214, 220), amb=(160, 168, 180), sky=['#8c96a4', '#9ea8b4', '#b0b8c2', '#c2c8d0', '#d2d6dc'], glow=0.0),
}


def sky_cols(hour):
    return [hexrgb(c) for c in HOURS[hour]['sky']]


def material(hour, albedo, n, lo=0.2, hi=1.0, dist_haze=0.0):
    h = HOURS[hour]
    cols = lit_ramp(albedo, np.array(h['sun'], float), np.array(h['amb'], float), n, lo, hi)
    if dist_haze > 0:
        cols = haze(cols, sky_cols(hour)[-1], dist_haze)
    return cols


def water_body(hour, tint=(40, 80, 90), n=4, murk=0.0, sand=(170, 150, 110)):
    """Deep -> shallow body colours.  Deep is the water's own tint in ambient
    light; shallow is lit bottom seen through a film of sky, so that the
    Fresnel dither between mirror and body is always between close values."""
    h = HOURS[hour]
    amb = np.array(h['amb'], float) / 255
    sun = np.array(h['sun'], float) / 255
    sk = sky_cols(hour)
    deep = mix(np.array(tint, float) * (0.45 + 0.5 * amb), sk[0] * 0.5, 0.25)
    shallow = mix(np.array(sand, float) * (0.35 + 0.5 * sun) * (0.5 + 0.5 * amb), sk[1], 0.45)
    if murk:
        shallow = mix(shallow, np.array([120, 110, 70.0]) * (0.4 + 0.6 * amb), murk)
        deep = mix(deep, np.array([60, 60, 35.0]) * (0.4 + 0.6 * amb), murk)
    cols = [np.clip(mix(deep, shallow, (i / (n - 1)) ** 0.9), 0, 255) for i in range(n)]
    return cols


def spec_cols(hour):
    s = sky_cols(hour)
    sun = np.array(HOURS[hour]['sun'], float)
    return [mix(s[-1], sun, 0.3), np.clip(mix(s[-1], (255, 255, 255), 0.55), 0, 255)]
Write: elements/channel/src/colour.py
"""colour.py -- the palette is the light (Ferrari). The index canvas painted in grey is kept as is;
an hour swaps every ramp's colours. Each colour keeps the luminance its index proved in grey
(scaled by the hour's key), and takes its hue from a walk between the ambient light (shadows) and
the sun (lights), times the material's albedo. The sky is its own hand-set ramp per hour, and the
water reuses it (plus a few water-only tints)."""
import numpy as np
import channel as ch

HOURS = {
    'midday': dict(sun=(255, 248, 232), amb=(150, 178, 222), key=1.0, lift=0.0,
                   sky=['#e6eef2', '#d6e6f0', '#c4dcee', '#b2d0ea', '#a0c4e6', '#8eb8e0', '#7eacda', '#709fd2'],
                   milk=(150, 176, 178), bed=(96, 88, 76)),
    'overcast': dict(sun=(214, 216, 222), amb=(170, 176, 188), key=0.92, lift=0.06,
                     sky=['#dcdee2', '#d4d6dc', '#cdd0d6', '#c6c9d0', '#bfc2ca', '#b8bbc4', '#b1b4be', '#aaaeb8'],
                     milk=(158, 170, 172), bed=(92, 90, 86)),
    'dusk': dict(sun=(255, 160, 104), amb=(120, 100, 170), key=0.78, lift=-0.04,
                 sky=['#f6c09a', '#f0ae94', '#e8a09a', '#dc96a2', '#cc90aa', '#bc8cb0', '#aa88b4', '#9a84b6'],
                 milk=(170, 140, 150), bed=(80, 62, 70)),
}

ALBEDO = {
    'grav': (148, 140, 128), 'grass': (176, 150, 92), 'earth': (110, 88, 70), 'bould': (140, 136, 132),
    'wil': (110, 128, 96), 'ridge': (120, 124, 140), 'scrub': (80, 96, 76),
}


def _lin(c):
    c = np.asarray(c, float) / 255
    return np.where(c > 0.04045, ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4, c / 12.92)


def _srgb(l):
    l = np.clip(l, 0, 1)
    return np.clip(255 * np.where(l > 0.0031308, 1.055 * l ** (1 / 2.4) - 0.055, 12.92 * l), 0, 255)


def _Y(lin):
    return lin @ np.array([0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722])


def fit_lum(rgb_lin, target_Y):
    y = max(_Y(rgb_lin), 1e-6)
    out = rgb_lin * (target_Y / y)
    if out.max() > 1:                                  # desaturate toward white rather than clip hue
        m = out.max()
        out = out / m
        yy = _Y(out)
        t = (target_Y - yy) / max(1 - yy, 1e-6)
        out = out + (1 - out) * np.clip(t, 0, 1)
    return out


def hexrgb(h):
    h = h.lstrip('#'); return np.array([int(h[i:i + 2], 16) for i in (0, 2, 4)], float)


def palette(Pgrey, hour):
    """a colour Pal with exactly the grey Pal's layout."""
    L = HOURS[hour]
    sun, amb = _lin(L['sun']), _lin(L['amb'])
    cols = np.zeros_like(Pgrey.arr, dtype=float)
    for name, idx in Pgrey.ramps.items():
        greys = Pgrey.arr[idx][:, 0].astype(float)
        n = len(idx)
        if name == 'sky':
            sk = [hexrgb(c) for c in L['sky']]
            for k, i in enumerate(idx):
                cols[i] = sk[min(k, len(sk) - 1)]
            continue
        if name == 'wat':
            milk, bed = _lin(L['milk']), _lin(L['bed'])
            for k, i in enumerate(idx):
                t = k / max(n - 1, 1)
                c = bed * (1 - t) + milk * t
                Yt = _Y(_lin((greys[k],) * 3)) * L['key']
                cols[i] = _srgb(fit_lum(c, Yt))
            continue
        alb = _lin(ALBEDO.get(name, (128, 128, 128)))
        g0, g1 = greys.min(), greys.max()
        for k, i in enumerate(idx):
            t = (greys[k] - g0) / max(g1 - g0, 1)
            light = amb * (1 - t ** 1.3) + sun * t ** 1.3          # shadows to ambient, lights to sun
            c = alb * light
            Yt = _Y(_lin((greys[k],) * 3)) * L['key'] * (1 + L['lift'] * (1 - t))
            cols[i] = _srgb(fit_lum(c, Yt))
    P = ch.Pal([])
    P.ramps = Pgrey.ramps
    P.arr = np.clip(cols, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    P.cols = [tuple(c) for c in P.arr]
    return P

"""Stage 4: colour. The same index canvases re-lit by palette: midday, overcast, dusk."""
import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, world, painter, colour

Pg = painter.grey_palette()


def still(seed=1):
    cam = world.Cam(192, 108, f=300, h=1.6, pitch_deg=7.0)
    t = world.StraightChannel(7.0, 11.0, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), seed=seed + 4)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t)
    objs = [dict(kind='boulder', x=-2.3, z=11.9, w=1.5, h=0.62, y0=0.2, seed=11),
            dict(kind='willow', x=2.8, z=12.6, w=2.6, h=1.9, y0=0.2, seed=5)]
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.2 + 1.3 * np.sin(u * 192 / 23.0) + 0.6 * np.sin(u * 192 / 7.3), scrub=(140.0, 3.0))
    return painter.paint(G, cam, Pg, np.random.default_rng(seed), dict(objects=objs, stones=(0.03, 0.14), backdrop=bd))


def riffle(seed=1):
    cam = world.Cam(160, 90, f=380, h=1.6, pitch_deg=9.0)
    t = world.StraightChannel(6.0, 9.5, far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), seed=seed + 4)
    t.add_riffle(0.0, 1.2, 1.0, n_stones=34, seed=seed)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t)
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.0 + 1.2 * np.sin(u * 9) + 0.5 * np.sin(u * 23), scrub=(120.0, 3.0))
    wil = [dict(kind='willow', x=1.2, z=10.4, w=2.6, h=1.8, y0=0.2, seed=3)]
    return painter.paint(G, cam, Pg, np.random.default_rng(seed), dict(terrain=t, backdrop=bd, stones=(0.03, 0.14), objects=wil))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    a, b = still(), riffle()
    rows = []
    for hour in ('midday', 'overcast', 'dusk'):
        P = colour.palette(Pg, hour)
        rows.append(pk.panel([(P.rgb(a), 'still strip, ' + hour), (P.rgb(b), 'riffle, ' + hour)], 3))
        pk.save(P.rgb(a)[40:100, 0:120], '../out/s4_still_%s_8x.png' % hour, 8)
    pk.stack(rows).save('../out/s4_colour.png')
    # palette cards
    cards = []
    for hour in ('midday', 'overcast', 'dusk'):
        P = colour.palette(Pg, hour)
        n = len(P.arr); card = np.zeros((12, n * 6, 3), np.uint8)
        for i in range(n):
            card[:, i * 6:(i + 1) * 6] = P.arr[i]
        cards.append((card, hour + ': ' + ' | '.join(P.ramps)))
    pk.stack([pk.panel([c], 4) for c in cards]).save('../out/s4_palettes.png')

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel; cat >> LESSONS.md <<'EOF'

---

## Stage 4: colour

`src/colour.py`, `src/s4_colour.py`.

![colour](out/s4_colour.png)
![photo | mine | Ferrari](out/s4_triptych.png)
Palette cards: `out/s4_palettes.png`. 8× crops: `out/s4_still_*_8x.png`.

**The palette is the light.** Every image in stages 1–3 was painted as an index canvas in grey. Stage 4 does not repaint anything. The *same* canvas is re-lit by swapping every ramp's colours, which is what Ferrari's Mirror Pond and Mountain Stream files show (identical index maps for morning and afternoon). Each colour keeps the luminance its index proved in grey (scaled by the hour's key) and takes its hue from a walk from the ambient light (shadows) to the sun (lights), times the material's albedo. Distance ramps (ridge, scrub) are pulled toward the horizon sky (aerial perspective).

**The water reuses the sky ramp, plus water-only tints:**

- **The sky ramp is hand-set per hour.** At dusk it is Ferrari's near-isovalue hue walk (peach at the horizon to lilac higher up), so the far water, which mirrors low sky, turns peach and the near water lilac: V02's ribbons. At midday it's pale to blue, with value falling upward. Overcast is a near-flat grey-lavender.
- **The milky mirror (`wmir`)** was the one addition colour forced. At midday the reused sky ramp made the channels saturated swimming-pool blue, where every glacial photo shows a muted grey-turquoise. Rock flour clouds the mirror. So the water's mirror is a second ramp with the *same greys* (the index plan is untouched) whose colours are the sky's steps mixed toward the water's own milk. Clear, spring-fed water would switch back to the sky ramp itself. `milky` is a parameter.
- **Five water-only tints** (the bed seen through thin water, grading to milk) carry the shelves, stones and wet shallows. The dusk riffle's glare streaks come out pink from the mirror ramp without anything added.

**Honest comparison.**

- *Dusk* is the most Ferrari-like: warm lights on the stones, a lilac and peach mirror, a violet ridge.
- *Overcast* matches the NZ photo's silver ribbons on dark ground in value, but it is duller than his V14 storm, where the dark is richer and bluer.
- *Midday* is right in water colour now, but the gravel is a neutral grey-brown with little light in it. His midday (V11AM) makes every shadow violet. My ambient hue is too weak in the ground ramps.
- The foreground cobbles are too large and too contrasty for all three hours, and they compete with the water.

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; sed -i '' "s/'midday': dict(sun=(255, 248, 232), amb=(150, 178, 222)/'midday': dict(sun=(255, 246, 226), amb=(126, 146, 232)/" colour.py; cat >> world.py <<'EOF'


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- the braid ------
class BraidPlain:
    """a braided plain: threads x_k(z) that wander, split and rejoin (their union is the braid),
    each with a width w_k(z) and depth; bars between them undulate a little above the water.
    Threads run roughly along z (toward the viewer) by default; `angle` rotates the whole plain.
    height(x, z) is vectorised; distances are computed segment by segment in plan."""

    def __init__(self, seed=0, n_threads=4, z0=4.0, z1=600.0, width=(2.0, 7.0), depth=0.45,
                 spread=18.0, angle=0.0, bar_h=0.25, x_center=0.0, lam=(25.0, 90.0), turb=0.6):
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.depth, self.bar_h, self.angle = depth, bar_h, np.radians(angle)
        zs = np.geomspace(z0, z1, 700)
        self.threads = []
        for k in range(n_threads):
            x0 = x_center + rng.normal(0, spread * 0.5)
            lam1 = rng.uniform(*lam); lam2 = rng.uniform(lam[0] * 0.4, lam[0])
            a1 = rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0) * spread * 0.5; a2 = a1 * 0.3
            ph1, ph2 = rng.uniform(0, 6.3, 2)
            # amplitude grows with distance a little (the plain widens toward the horizon)
            x = x0 + (a1 * np.sin(zs / lam1 + ph1) + a2 * np.sin(zs / lam2 + ph2)) * (1 + zs / 300.0)
            w = rng.uniform(*width) * (0.6 + 0.4 * np.sin(zs / rng.uniform(15, 40) + rng.uniform(0, 6))) + 0.4
            # some threads dry up for a stretch (they split off and rejoin)
            gate = np.ones_like(zs)
            if rng.random() < 0.6:
                zc = np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(z0 * 3), np.log(z1 * 0.5)))
                gate = np.clip(np.abs(np.log(zs / zc)) / 0.25, 0, 1) if rng.random() < 0.5 else gate
            self.threads.append((zs, x, w * gate))
        self.bar_rng = rng.integers(0, 10 ** 6)

    def _rot(self, x, z):
        if self.angle == 0:
            return x, z
        c, s = np.cos(self.angle), np.sin(self.angle)
        zm = 40.0
        return c * x - s * (z - zm), s * x + c * (z - zm) + zm

    def channel_depth(self, x, z):
        x, z = self._rot(x, z)
        d = np.zeros(np.broadcast(x, z).shape)
        for (zs, xs, ws) in self.threads:
            xc = np.interp(z, zs, xs); wc = np.interp(z, zs, ws)
            # horizontal distance to the thread's centre, corrected for its slope in plan
            dxdz = np.gradient(xs, zs)
            sl = np.interp(z, zs, dxdz)
            dist = np.abs(x - xc) / np.sqrt(1 + sl ** 2)
            u = np.clip(1 - (dist / np.maximum(wc / 2, 1e-3)) ** 2, 0, 1)
            d = np.maximum(d, self.depth * u ** 1.6 * np.clip(wc / 3.0, 0.3, 1.0))
        return d

    def height(self, x, z):
        d = self.channel_depth(x, z)
        # bars: gentle undulation above the water line; channels cut below it
        bar = self.bar_h * (0.55 + 0.45 * np.sin(0.21 * x + 0.05 * z) * np.cos(0.13 * z - 0.3 * x))
        y = np.where(d > 0.02, -d, bar * np.clip(1 - 0 * d, 0, 1))
        # a narrow sloping margin: bars fall to the water over ~0.6 m (the wet foot lives here)
        return y
EOF

"""Stage 5: the channel as one form -- a braid of ribbons that bend, narrow, split and rejoin,
seen at a low angle and at 30 degrees oblique."""
import sys, time
import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, world, painter, colour

Pg = painter.grey_palette()


def render(view='low', seed=3, hour='overcast', W=256, H=144, **kw):
    if view == 'low':
        cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=320, h=1.7, pitch_deg=4.0)
    else:
        cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=260, h=30.0, pitch_deg=30.0)
    t = world.BraidPlain(seed=seed, **kw)
    t0 = time.time()
    G = world.raycast(cam, t, z_min=2.0, z_max=900.0)
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.4 + 1.4 * np.sin(u * 7 + seed) + 0.6 * np.sin(u * 19), scrub=(700.0, 6.0))
    q = dict(backdrop=bd, stones=(0.03, 0.14))
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, Pg, np.random.default_rng(seed), q)
    P = colour.palette(Pg, hour)
    return P.rgb(cv), cv, time.time() - t0


if __name__ == '__main__':
    rgb, cv, dt = render(sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'low')
    print('%.1fs' % dt)
    pk.save(rgb, '../study/s5_%s.png' % (sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'low'), 4)
EOF

Bash: Designed single channel with split; render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='world.py'; s=open(p).read()
s+='''

def designed_channel(z0=8.0, length=60.0, x0=0.0, width=5.0, depth=0.45, bar_h=0.25, split=True, lateral=6.0):
    """one channel as a single form: an S-bend that narrows downstream (toward the viewer), splits
    around a lozenge bar and rejoins. Flows from far (z0+length) to near (z0)."""
    B = BraidPlain.__new__(BraidPlain)
    B.depth, B.bar_h, B.angle = depth, bar_h, 0.0
    zs = np.linspace(z0 - 5, z0 + length + 5, 600)
    t = (zs - z0) / length                                   # 0 near .. 1 far
    xc = x0 + lateral * np.sin(2.4 * np.pi * t + 0.6) * (0.6 + 0.8 * t)
    w = width * (0.55 + 0.6 * t) * (1 + 0.25 * np.sin(9 * t))   # narrows toward the viewer, pinches
    threads = []
    if split:
        # between t=0.35 and 0.6 the flow divides around a bar: two arms bowing apart, then rejoining
        env = np.clip(np.sin(np.pi * np.clip((t - 0.33) / 0.3, 0, 1)), 0, 1)
        sep = 0.95 * w * env
        wa = np.where(env > 0.05, w * (0.5 + 0.12 * (1 - env)), w)
        threads.append((zs, xc - sep, wa, 0.0, 0.0))
        threads.append((zs, xc + sep * 1.15, wa * np.where(env > 0.05, 0.8, 1.0), 0.0, 0.0))
    else:
        threads.append((zs, xc, w, 0.0, 0.0))
    B.threads = threads
    return B
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
cat >> s5_braid.py <<'EOF'


def render_designed(view='low', hour='dusk', W=256, H=144, z0=8.0, length=60.0, seed=2):
    if view == 'low':
        cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=320, h=1.7, pitch_deg=4.0)
    else:
        cam = world.Cam(W, H, f=260, h=30.0, pitch_deg=30.0)
    t = world.designed_channel(z0=z0, length=length)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t, z_min=2.0, z_max=900.0)
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.4 + 1.4 * np.sin(u * 7 + seed) + 0.6 * np.sin(u * 19), scrub=(700.0, 6.0))
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, Pg, np.random.default_rng(seed), dict(backdrop=bd, stones=(0.03, 0.14)))
    return colour.palette(Pg, hour).rgb(cv), cv

"""stage 5 sheet: the channel as one form (designed: bends, narrows, splits, rejoins) and a braid
plain, at a low angle and at 30 degrees; plus a distance ladder of the same channel."""
import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, s5_braid as S
rows = []
a, _ = S.render_designed('low', 'dusk')
b, _ = S.render_designed('low', 'overcast')
c, _ = S.render_designed('oblique', 'dusk', z0=40, length=60)
rows.append(pk.panel([(a, 'one channel, low angle, dusk'), (b, 'low angle, overcast'), (c, '30 deg oblique, dusk')], 2))

## Round 9 — 2026-09-25 23:00 — stage 5 studies `study/s5_oblique.png`, `study/s5_low.png` (WIP, no LESSONS entry yet)
Notes only, since stage 5 is in progress.

- **`s5_oblique`: the bars are cut paper.** Every bar is a flat grey polygon with straight edges and sharp angular corners (wedges, triangles, a dagger shape at the right). In the aerial refs (`transfer/refs/braided_aerial_krossa.jpg`, `braided_aerial_nz.jpg`) bars are *lobes*: a rounded blunt head upstream, a long tapering tail downstream, and edges that curve continuously. The channels between them vary in width and brightness (deep threads darker, shallow sheets lighter), where yours are one uniform pale tone. It reads as a vector map.
- The channel edges in `s5_oblique` have a nice darker lip on some bars. That's your near-light/far-dark rule, and it's working.
- **`s5_low`: the far treeline is one bump repeated** at a regular interval (a scalloped border), and the V-shaped cloud reflections from stage 3 are back in the near water. The long low bars as horizontal streaks are right for a low angle and read well at 1×.

    """clear sky, lighter to the horizon; cloud BANKS (stratocumulus): soft horizontal bands whose
    top edge undulates gently and whose base is flat -- mirrored, they lie in the water as soft
    horizontal light shapes, never as hanging cones."""
    e = np.maximum(elev_deg, 0)

    for k, (c0, hw, eb, et) in enumerate(clouds):

        along = np.clip(1 - np.abs(x) ** 3, 0, 1)                       # a long flat bank, soft ends

        t = np.clip((e - eb) / np.maximum(top - eb, 1e-3), 0, 1)

    return v

---

## Stage 5: a channel as one form

`world.BraidPlain`, `world.designed_channel`, `src/s5_braid.py`, `src/s5_sheet.py`.

![channel](out/s5_channel.png)
4×: `out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png`, `out/s5_braid_low_midday_4x.png`, `out/s5_braid_oblique_4x.png`. 8×: `out/s5_low_dusk_8x.png`.

Row 1 is one designed channel: an S-bend that narrows toward the viewer, pinches, splits around a lozenge bar and rejoins. It is seen at a low angle (dusk, overcast) and at 30° from 30 m up. Row 2 is a generated braid plain (threads that wander, split and rejoin, with relic dry channels on the bars). Row 3 is the same channel at three distances.

**What the form taught me:**

1. **At a low angle a channel is its cross-view reaches.** My first threads ran along z and projected, far off, as short vertical slivers. Ferrari's far channels, and the photos', are horizontal ribbons, because the reaches that cross the view are the ones that show. Oblique threads (rotated frames, σ ≈ 22°) give the braid its zigzag of horizontal ribbons at a low angle, and its weave from 30°.
2. **Far threads are drawn one row tall, whether or not a ray hits them (V02).** Far off, a screen row spans tens of metres of ground, so a 3 m channel covers a few percent of a pixel and single rays miss it. So a ground pixel becomes water when water covers enough of its footprint, only at the local maximum of that coverage, and only where the marks make a horizontal run of 3 px or more. A thread is a *run*, never a ladder.
3. **Level of detail in plan:** meanders shorter than a row's footprint are averaged into a straight "belt" as wide as they swing. Otherwise far meanders alias into vertical ladders. The window scales with the camera (row footprint ∝ z²/(f·h)), so the 30° view keeps its curves.
4. **Far threads always mirror the sky.** Physically, the reflected ray from a far channel at 1° grazing hits the next bank a few metres on, so it should be dark. Ferrari paints them as the sky ramp, and the photos (taken from terraces) agree. I force it, and the 160 m thread in row 3 is a bright one-row run.
5. **Low-angle distance needs a little height.** From 1.7 m, bars 0.25 m high hide every channel beyond about 100 m (true, and dull). Row 3 uses a 4 m eye, a terrace, which is where the photos were taken from and roughly Ferrari's vantage in V02.
6. **Bars are calm fields** (the critic's rounds 6–8, measured against my own triptychs). Stones only appear close up, where a row covers less than about 4 cm, and interstices only closer still. The rest is an ordered base of a slow field. With the texture off, the water became the subject of every picture.
7. **Colour holds up:** the dusk land key is lower than the sky's (0.55 against 0.78), so the ribbons glow against the plain, V02's notan.

**Honest comparison.**

- *Low angle* is the strongest: the dusk channel and the midday braid read as braided rivers, and the far threads are V02's one-row ribbons.
- *30° oblique* reads as a braid from the air, but the bars are cut paper (the critic's round 9). Mine are polygons between smooth threads. The aerial photos' bars are *lobes*, with a blunt rounded head upstream, a long tapering tail and curving edges. The channels are also one uniform tone, where the photos' deep threads are darker than the shallow sheets. **Not solved.** The fix is to generate bars as lobes and derive channels from them, rather than the reverse, and to let the body tone follow depth from above.
- My dusk ribbons are pale lilac-peach; his burn orange. His water at dusk mirrors a much hotter horizon and adds hot glint clusters, which I don't place yet.
EOF
echo ok

ok

        self.z_near, self.z_far, self.depth = z_near, z_far, depth

cat > technique.py <<'EOF'
"""technique.py -- the braided-channel painter as one function with parameters.

    render(dict(width=3.5, depth=0.4, flow=0.0, hour='overcast', bank='gravel', camera='low',
                distance=7.0, seed=1, milky=True, bend=0.0, W=160, H=90)) -> (rgb, index canvas)

width    channel width across (m)            depth   max depth (m)
flow     0 still .. 1 riffle (sill strength)  hour    midday | overcast | dusk
bank     gravel | cut | grass | tussock_near  camera  low (1.6 m, ~7 deg down) | oblique (30 deg, 12 m up)
distance near edge distance (m)               seed    layout only
milky    glacial water (True) or clear (False)  bend  lateral wander of the strip (m)
"""
import numpy as np
import world, painter, colour

Pg = painter.grey_palette()
BANKS = {
    'gravel': dict(far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), grass=None),
    'cut': dict(far=('cut', 0.35), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0), grass=None),
    'grass': dict(far=('cut', 0.22), near=('slope', 0.12, 1.0),
                  grass=dict(side='far', min_y=0.08, blade_m=0.35, density=0.4, lean=0.3, lit=0.4)),
    'tussock_near': dict(far=('slope', 0.2, 0.6), near=('cut', 0.3),
                         grass=dict(side='near', min_y=0.15, blade_m=0.4, density=0.4, lean=-0.3, lit=0.4)),
}


def camera_for(p):
    W, H = p.get('W', 160), p.get('H', 90)
    zmid = p['distance'] + p['width'] / 2
    if p['camera'] == 'low':
        h = 1.6
        # frame the strip: put its centre about 60% down the frame
        ang = np.degrees(np.arctan(h / zmid))
        f = 300 * (zmid / 9.0) ** 0.5
        pitch = ang - np.degrees(np.arctan((0.1 * H) / f))
        return world.Cam(W, H, f=f, h=h, pitch_deg=pitch)
    h = 12.0
    f = 260 * (zmid / 9.0) ** 0.5
    pitch = np.degrees(np.arctan(h / zmid))
    return world.Cam(W, H, f=f, h=h, pitch_deg=pitch)


def render(p):
    p = dict(dict(width=3.5, depth=0.4, flow=0.0, hour='overcast', bank='gravel', camera='low', distance=7.0,
                  seed=1, milky=True, bend=0.0), **p)
    b = BANKS[p['bank']]
    zn = p['distance']; zf = zn + p['width']
    t = world.StraightChannel(zn, zf, depth=p['depth'], far=b['far'], near=b['near'], seed=p['seed'] + 4,
                              bend=p['bend'], bend_len=6.0 + zn * 0.3)
    if p['flow'] > 0:
        t.add_riffle(0.0, 0.8 + 0.1 * zn, p['flow'], n_stones=int(10 + 30 * p['flow']), seed=p['seed'])
    cam = camera_for(p)
    G = world.raycast(cam, t, z_min=1.0, z_max=900.0)
    bd = dict(ridge=lambda u: 2.2 + 1.3 * np.sin(u * 8 + p['seed']) + 0.6 * np.sin(u * 21 + 2 * p['seed']),
              scrub=(max(80.0, zf * 8), 3.0))
    q = dict(terrain=t, backdrop=bd, stones=(0.03, 0.14), grass=b['grass'], milky=p['milky'])
    if not p['milky']:
        q.update(visibility=0.6)
    cv = painter.paint(G, cam, Pg, np.random.default_rng(p['seed']), q)
    P = colour.palette(Pg, p['hour'])
    return P.rgb(cv), cv
EOF
cat > sheets.py <<'EOF'
"""stage 6: parameter sheets. python3 sheets.py [all|1|2|3|4|5]"""
import sys
import numpy as np, pixkit as pk, technique as T


def sheet(name, rows, cols, fn, title_fn, s=2):
    out = []
    for r in rows:
        items = []
        for c in cols:
            rgb, _ = T.render(fn(r, c))
            items.append((rgb, title_fn(r, c)))
        out.append(pk.panel(items, s))
    pk.stack(out).save('../out/sheet_%s.png' % name)


def main(which):
    if which in ('all', '1'):
        sheet('1_width_x_depth', [0.08, 0.25, 0.6], [1.5, 3.5, 7.0],
              lambda d, w: dict(width=w, depth=d, hour='overcast'),
              lambda d, w: 'width %.1f m, depth %.2f m' % (w, d))
    if which in ('all', '2'):
        sheet('2_flow_x_hour', ['midday', 'overcast', 'dusk'], [0.0, 0.5, 1.0],
              lambda h, f: dict(flow=f, hour=h, width=3.5),
              lambda h, f: '%s, flow %.1f' % (h, f))
    if which in ('all', '3'):
        sheet('3_bank_x_camera', ['low', 'oblique'], ['gravel', 'cut', 'grass', 'tussock_near'],
              lambda cam, b: dict(bank=b, camera=cam, hour='midday'),
              lambda cam, b: '%s bank, %s camera' % (b, cam))
    if which in ('all', '4'):
        sheet('4_distance_x_hour', ['midday', 'dusk'], [4.0, 9.0, 25.0, 70.0],
              lambda h, d: dict(distance=d, hour=h, width=3.5, bend=0.4),
              lambda h, d: '%s, near edge at %d m' % (h, d))
    if which in ('all', '5'):
        sheet('5_seeds_milky', [True, False], [1, 2, 3],
              lambda m, sd: dict(seed=sd, milky=m, hour='midday', flow=0.4 * (sd % 2), bend=0.5),
              lambda m, sd: 'seed %d, %s' % (sd, 'glacial milk' if m else 'clear water'))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main(sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'all')

Bash: Curved dense lip, short streaks, crest arcs
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/channel/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
p='world.py'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""    def lip_u(self, x, z, xc):
        zmid = 0.5 * (self.z_near + self.z_far)
        return x - (xc + getattr(self, 'skew', 0.0) * (z - zmid))""","""    def lip_x(self, z, xc):
        \"\"\"the lip follows the bed: skewed across the channel and bowed downstream mid-channel,
        where the thalweg runs fastest.\"\"\"
        zmid = 0.5 * (self.z_near + self.z_far)
        sf = np.clip((z - self.z_near) / (self.z_far - self.z_near), 0, 1)
        return xc + getattr(self, 'skew', 0.0) * (z - zmid) + 0.35 * (self.z_far - self.z_near) * 4 * sf * (1 - sf)

    def lip_u(self, x, z, xc):
        return x - self.lip_x(z, xc)""")

p='painter.py'; s=open(p).read()
a=s.index("        # the lip: a stepped (never ruled) bright broken line"); b=s.index("        # streaks along the flow, starting at the lip")
s=s[:a]+'''        # the lip: where the marks are DENSEST (not a line of dots) -- a 2-4 px band of broken foam
        # and glare following the curved lip, jittered row by row
        for rr in range(r_far + 1, r_near):
            z = cam.z_of_row(rr + 0.5)
            ppm = cam.f / z
            c0 = int(cam.col_of(T.lip_x(z, xc), z))
            wband = max(2, int(0.12 * ppm))
            for j in range(wband):
                if rng.random() < 0.7 - 0.4 * j / wband:
                    put(rr, c0 + j + int(rng.integers(-1, 2)), foam if rng.random() < 0.5 * st else glare)
'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace("            xl = xc + getattr(T, 'skew', 0.0) * (z - zmid)\n            u = rng.exponential(half * 0.5)","            xl = T.lip_x(z, xc)\n            u = rng.exponential(half * 0.5)")
s=s.replace("                    L = max(2, int(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.4) * ppm * (0.5 + a)))","                    L = int(np.clip(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.2) * ppm * (0.5 + a), 1, 5))")

- The lip now **follows the bed**: skewed and bowed downstream mid-channel. It is drawn as the *densest* band of broken foam and glare (2–4 px, jittered row by row), never as a line of dots.
- Streak dashes are short (1–5 px). Some turn into short **crest arcs** across the flow (stepping a row every 2 px), the Iori photo's broken curving foam lines.

**Colour cycling (not yet done):**""")
s += '''

---

## Stage 6: the technique as code, swept on sheets

`src/technique.py`: one function.



rgb, idx = technique.render(dict(width=3.5, depth=0.4, flow=0.0, hour=&#x27;overcast&#x27;, bank=&#x27;gravel&#x27;,
                                 camera=&#x27;low&#x27;, distance=7.0, seed=1, milky=True, bend=0.0))



`src/sheets.py all` writes the sheets (about a minute on one core). Underneath: `world.py` (terrain, braid plain, raycaster with sub-pixel threads), `painter.py` (Ferrari's rules on the G-buffer), `channel.py` (quantizers, gravel, shallows, boulder, lawn strands ported from the master-copy student), `colour.py` (the palette as the light).

![width x depth](out/sheet_1_width_x_depth.png)
![flow x hour](out/sheet_2_flow_x_hour.png)
![bank x camera](out/sheet_3_bank_x_camera.png)
![distance x hour](out/sheet_4_distance_x_hour.png)
![seeds, milky vs clear](out/sheet_5_seeds_milky.png)

**What the axes do:**

- **width × depth**: shallow channels are mostly shelf (the bed shows, darker, with stones). Deeper ones are open milky mirror with a narrow shelf at the near edge.
- **flow**: 0 is a mirror. At 0.5, a curved lip with a light streak zone. At 1.0, the lip foams and the reflections of the far bank break.
- **hour**: the same index canvas under three palettes.
- **bank**: sloping gravel (thin dark far foot), cut gravel (a tall dark face and its reflection), tussock on the far bank (strands, a crest fringe, a dark reflected band), tussock on the near bank (a fringe over the water).
- **camera**: low (1.6 m, the strip a band of sky-mirror) or 30° from a bluff (the strip seen *into*: milk, shelves on both margins).
- **distance**: 4 m (full detail) → 9 m → 25 m (a few rows, one bright row) → 70 m (a one-row thread).
- **seed/milky**: layout only; clear water shows the bed and turns bluer.

**Honest assessment of the sheets:**

- Every cell reads as a channel in a braided plain, in the right value order. Nothing is noise.
- **But they are one composition repeated**: a strip across the frame, bar above, cobbles below. The *technique* sweeps well; the *pictures* are plain. Stage 5's braid plains are the pictures.
- Weakest cells: the cut-bank cells (bank and reflection are a heavy dark band) and the 25 m cells (the channel almost vanishes behind a sliver of bar, which is physically right from 1.6 m but dull). At 70 m the one-row thread is right.
- The foreground cobbles still claim the lower third of every low-camera cell.

---

## Where it stands against the master (plainly)

- **Copy A (V02 ribbons): matched** by the critic's judgement (round 3 PASS). The grammar learned there (water is the sky ramp indexed by nearness; low-angle shape is horizontal runs; near edge light, far edge dark; stepped bites) runs through every later stage.
- **Water: my strongest part.** The still mirror (checker material, sheen, soft cloud banks, textured reflections, shelves) and its colour; the critic called the midday and dusk mirrors the best in the studio. At 8× the open water would pass beside his; the edges mostly would.
- **Not at his level yet:**
  1. The **riffle** is a field of marks, where his is a few decisive jets with plain mirror between.
  2. **Bars from above** are cut paper; they should be lobes.
  3. **Grass** is his strands, via the master-copy port, but my tussock clumps and fringes are still more even than his.
  4. The **boulder and willow** props are far below his rocks and trees (other students own those).
  5. **Hot glints at dusk** and **colour cycling** of the marks are not built.
- **Next, in order:** lobed bars (generate bars, derive channels); riffle jets (fewer, longer marks along streamlines with plain mirror between; copy B again with his shapes traced, as copy A was); cycle slots on marks (phase = distance from the lip); a composition layer for the sheets, so each cell is a picture and not a strip.

**Resources that would help:** transcripts or captures of Ferrari's GDC 2016 talk (nobody has it); higher-resolution aerial and low-oblique photos of braided bars in low sun (the lobed-bar problem needs them); and the other *Living Worlds* scenes with rivers, if more exist in the Canvas Cycle set than the twelve already fetched.
'''
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 11 — 2026-09-25 23:26 — sheets `out/sheet_1…5`; LESSONS stage 5
**Sheets: REVISE.** Your stage-5 notes are excellent, and point 6 ("bars are calm fields… with the texture off, the water became the subject") is the lesson. (My read; your call.)

1. **The near gravel hasn't calmed yet on the sheets.** In sheet 2 (flow × hour) and sheet 4 (distance × hour) the near bank is rounded light blobs on a dark ground, now cloud- or popcorn-shaped, at high contrast. It's still the loudest thing in every cell. Your stage-5 rule says stones only close up; these cells *are* close up, so the stones are allowed. They need Ferrari's value discipline: the stone tops one or two steps above the ground, not four.
2. **Far threads as Morse** (sheet 4, "near edge at 25 m" and "70 m"). The far water line is a row of short white dashes with even gaps. Your rule 2 (a thread is a run of at least 3 px, never a ladder) is right, but the gaps between runs are regular, so it reads as a dotted line. Vary the run lengths and gaps (a long run, a short one, a long gap).
3. **The riffle front** (sheet 2, flow 0.5 and 1.0) is still a ruled diagonal of light dots in every hour.
4. From above, the bars are still cut paper and the channels still cross in X's (your own "not solved").

Ready in my view, and strong: **the water as the sky ramp indexed by reflected elevation**, the **near-light/far-dark edge grammar**, the **reflection as copied painted pixels with a tone law**, and **far threads as one-row runs** with LOD by row footprint. These are Ferrari-grade and generalise.
Bash: Value-disciplined stones, varied threads; rebuild all

s=s.replace("""        drop = T & ~keep""","""        # vary the run pattern: bridge some short gaps so a thread never reads as evenly dotted
        rng_t = np.random.default_rng(7)
        for r in range(H):
            xs_ = np.nonzero(keep[r])[0]
            for a_, b_ in zip(xs_[:-1], xs_[1:]):
                if 1 < b_ - a_ <= 4 and rng_t.random() < 0.6:
                    keep[r, a_:b_] = True
                    G['kind'][r, a_:b_] = 2; G['depth'][r, a_:b_] = 0.2; G['refl'][r, a_:b_] = 0
                    G['graze'][r, a_:b_] = np.arctan((r + 0.5 - cam.hz) / cam.f)
        drop = T & ~keep""")
